- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry that reads a network at every step of a fresh training run rather than at a checkpoint, and its headline is the sharpest warning the folder has produced about what a per-unit reading even is: at step 0, before a single weight update, attribution patching already recovers the task partition at about 3.6x the paper's own measured substrate, while the sibling pairs sit *below* it - so the structure being read on an untrained net is the interaction of task structure, random features and the output head, and the paper says so in as many words ("at step 0 there are no circuits to describe").** Read against run 768 (influential data moved the arrival rate of a circuit, not whether it arrives) and run 765 (existence, availability and use are three separate facts), the recurrence is that **every structural reading in this folder is implicit about when it was taken, and taking it earlier is the cheapest control available.** Mechanical rule for us: **every per-unit attribution our white-box model publishes ships a step-0 or random-initialisation arm computed on the identical task and prompt set, and any structure that survives at initialisation is filed as substrate rather than as learning - because a readout that is 3.6x its own floor on random weights is a statement about the task battery and the architecture, and the only thing that distinguishes it from a finding is the arm we did not run.**
- **Pattern:** **The measurement discipline here is the folder's most transferable anti-inflation package, and each piece answers a failure this folder has already committed: overlap is scored as a deviation from an empirically measured substrate (0.0422, the mean overlap of a non-dominant sibling pair) rather than from chance, with the paper noting that 15.26x chance equals 3.6x substrate; every event is published under two calibrations at once because the same second jump is 20.4 sigma quiet-window and 6.2 sigma global; and two defensible ranking calibers whose rosters overlap at Jaccard 0.28 are frozen, labelled and never mixed.** Read against run 764 (matched control at 90.5% reported next to a treatment at 97.1%) and run 766 (a composite score published without its two factors), the recurrence is that **an effect size in this field is always a ratio to a chosen zero point, and the zero point is a free parameter that is almost never defended.** Mechanical rule for us: **every overlap, concentration or selectivity number our model emits names its zero point in the record, that zero point is measured from the model rather than assumed (a structurally similar but non-dominant pair, a shuffled-label arm, an init arm), and a raw multiple-of-chance figure is published only alongside its substrate-relative twin - because "15x random" and "3.6x floor" are the same measurement and one of them is honest about what the floor is.**
- **Pattern:** **The stability result is the folder's cleanest demonstration that an aggregate curve can be true and uninformative at once: the locked partition shows zero turnover and zero migration on 0/117 adjudicated cases, yet inside the lock window old-member retention is only 60% (run2) / 44% (run1), and at neuron resolution the shared set is a 165-neuron skeleton carrying >= 90% of the set from step 24 onward surrounded by 1,124 neurons that enter and leave at least three times, 240 transient, 353 late, and 421 unclassified out of 2,303 that ever entered.** Read against run 763 (a circuit description no more similar to itself than to its neighbours, exposed by a data split) and run 759 (a dormancy spectrum hidden by an aggregate), the recurrence is that **"this object persisted" is a claim about a core, and every persistence claim in this folder so far has been made on the whole set.** Mechanical rule for us: **any persistence or stability claim our model makes about a unit set is reported with a fate-class breakdown over time (always-present core, transient, late, alternating) and the core's share, not a single overlap number - because a 0/117 turnover record and a 165-of-2,303 core are the same run, and only the second number tells you what a checkpoint-to-checkpoint carry actually carries.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry that computes a whole-model connectivity object from the weights with an exact, non-sampled chance level, and the exactness is what makes the census readable: because the Haar-rotation null gives 1/d in the mean and a closed-form Beta law for rank-one pairs, every one of 6.3 x 10^8 neuron pairs can be judged against a distribution rather than a permutation, and the resulting fact - 140,927 pairs beyond |cos| = 0.2 where chance predicts about 14 - is a statement about the substrate rather than about a sampling budget.** That is the shape run 764's transcoder pipeline could not reach (its 0.97-to-0.82 preservation fall was a statement about features it could not see) and the opposite of run 768's influence scores, which needed 384 A100-hours to earn a ranking. Mechanical rule for us: **any score our white-box model emits over more than a handful of candidate units carries a closed-form or exactly-fit null per stratum, stated with its two moments, and we report the number of candidates and the null's expected count at the same threshold - because "this pair is coupled" with no chance level is unfalsifiable, and with an exact one it is arithmetic.**
- **Pattern:** **The census's most transferable finding is that the trained network contains a large actively-avoidant population alongside the coupled one, and the two are learned at different times: at initialisation 2-3% of pairs clear |z| = 2, super-coupling is already 61-84% of K/Q pairs within the first thousand Pythia steps with none below chance, and avoidance appears only between steps 1,000 and 4,000 and keeps growing to the end of training (V-composition avoidant share 0% -> 63%).** Read against run 768 (influential data changes the arrival rate of a circuit, not whether it arrives) and run 765 (a component's existence, availability and use are three separate facts), the recurrence is that **the absence of a connection is an acquired property, so any white-box model that treats "no edge" as a default is missing a learned fact.** Mechanical rule for us: **our model records negative edges (deliberate non-coupling) with their own score, and wherever we have a training trajectory we report the coupling and avoidance shares at least three times along it - because in this folder every "the circuit appeared at step N" claim has so far been made without asking what appeared alongside it.**
- **Pattern:** **The community ablation is the folder's most honest instance of a matched control refusing to certify its own treatment: the GPT-2 numbers are excellent (94.7% of induction gain destroyed against a 1.9% matched-control median, and a nested decomposition that is strongly non-additive - 60.8% + 1.9% + 13.5% against 94.7%), but at Pythia-2.8B the layer-matched control itself reaches 90.5% and at GPT-2-large 20.3%, and at GPT-Neo the control reaches 97.1%, so the paper files GPT-Neo as a caveat rather than as evidence.** Read against run 764 (matched on size AND layer, where the floor held) and run 751 (a specificity ratio whose matched-random control was the same order as the effect), the recurrence is that **match quality is a property of the treatment set's shape: when a selected set occupies more than half of a layer's heads, no layer-matched control can be disjoint from it, and the number that should be reported is the control's own effect, not the treatment's.** Mechanical rule for us: **every selection-based causal claim we publish reports the matched control's number in the same cell as the treatment's, plus the overlap the matching forced - because a control at 90.5% and a treatment at 97.1% is a null result reported as a win, and only the overlap column tells you which of the two you have.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry that separates possessing a mechanism from using it, and the separation is a measurement rather than a caveat: five of seven training routes in Table IV end up with the induction stages physically present and score at most 0.508 on unseen two-hop pairs against a 0.500 chance floor, including two stages trained only on single-hop tasks and a model trained from scratch on endpoints, while the intermediate-aligned route reaches 0.980 and a swap of the first-hop intermediate moves the endpoint margin by 19.15.** The parallel result on state tracking is the same fact in another currency - endpoint-only stacked, looped and latent-token arms stall at 0.25-0.51 on the group-accumulator and reference tiers, and the aligned versions of all three routes reach 0.98-1.00, with one-token CoT at 1.000 throughout. Read against run 768 (a head forming even when 95 percent of its window is gradient-masked - rate versus trigger) and run 765 (four independent rankings failing together on the same unit class), the recurrence is that **a circuit's existence, its availability and its use are three separate facts, and this folder has now measured all three coming apart in different papers.** Mechanical rule for us: **every composed mechanism our white-box model records ships a bridge test - patch the intermediate representation and check whether the endpoint prediction moves - because a 0.508 at chance with the units in place is the signature of an inventory rather than a mechanism, and no amount of attribution on the units will distinguish the two.**
- **Pattern:** **The specificity ladder here is the best-instrumented instance in the folder for a functional-interface claim, because the four controls are not random sets but the *other three things one could have patched at the same interface*: restoring the source key recovers 83-100 percent of the disrupted margin in six settings, while restoring the source value moves it by at most +0.17, restoring an off-site key makes it worse, and patching the query moves it by exactly 0.00 - and the same ladder reappears downstream in the enrichment experiment, where replacing distractor keys removes or introduces the competition failure and replacing values does neither.** Read against run 764 (a random-head floor matched on size and location) and run 751 (a specificity ratio whose matched-random control was the same order as the effect), the recurrence is that **the strength of a causal claim is set by how precisely the control is matched, and the tightest possible match is a different functional slot of the same interface at the same position.** Mechanical rule for us: **whenever we claim a component carries a specific function, the control arm is the same intervention at the neighbouring functional slots (value instead of key, query instead of source, off-site instead of on-site) and not a random set - because "this key matters and that key does not" is a claim about a computation, and "this key matters and random units do not" is a claim about sparsity.**
- **Pattern:** **The horizon result is the one place in this folder where a white-box model derives, from wiring alone, which context a component cannot see, and then measures its own boundary: R_local <= L(W-1) predicts that three sliding-window layers of width 2 cannot carry lag-4 information into the pre-receiver block, and at W=2 in-horizon lag-3 carrying is positive while out-of-horizon lag-4 carrying in that block is identically zero, with wider windows (W in {4,8,16}, R_local >= 9) retaining positive lag-4 effects.** The companion visibility theorem is the same idea in the disambiguation setting - a paired-prompt bound pins accuracy at 1/2 once the identity cue falls outside the writer's radius - and the paper then shows the multi-layer alternative, where adjacent relays compose as S∘S = S^2 so that a richer address is built out of short shifts rather than a wider kernel. Read against run 763 (structural predicates transferring where components do not) and run 764 (a unit menu that silently excludes a component type), the recurrence is that **every reading in this folder is implicitly a claim about reach, and reach is usually left unstated.** Mechanical rule for us: **every sub-part of our white-box model carries a computed context-reach bound with its derivation, and a measured effect beyond that bound is reported as a violation of the derivation's assumptions (global mixing, recurrent state, a relay we did not model) rather than as a discovery - because the bound is the cheapest falsifiable prediction the architecture gives us for free.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry where an attribution target is a behavioural probe on the *parameters* rather than on the activations, and it is the cleanest instance yet of the substitution rule paying off: replace the validation loss with the unit's own score and shrink the curvature to that unit's weight subspace, and the ranking stops describing "globally hard samples" and starts describing "samples that shaped this head" - with the dissociation visible as an order-of-magnitude gap between the projection scores of induction and non-induction heads on the same selected set.** Read against run 767 (the same swap made at the attribution-graph level, where rooting the graph at a probe direction instead of a logit flipped 0% of tokens while the logit-rooted graph flipped 100%) and run 766 (a signed ranking credited only because it predicted the intervention it fed), the recurrence is now two independent pipelines: **the target of a reading is a free experimental variable, and changing it is cheaper and more informative than improving the tracer.** Mechanical rule for us: **every influence-style or gradient-style score our white-box model emits names its target function and its parameter subspace in the record, and ships a second arm scored against the unit's rival target (global loss vs unit probe) - because a ranking that moves with the target is measuring the target, and one that does not is measuring the model.**
- **Pattern:** **The mechanism finding is a rate-versus-trigger distinction that the paper states in a single paragraph and that would otherwise be invisible in the headline: deletion suppresses and augmentation accelerates, yet both arms converge to comparable saturation scores, samples from early/mid/late windows are equally effective, and the head still forms when 95% of the window's samples are gradient-masked - so the influential data changes how fast the circuit arrives, not whether it arrives.** Read against run 764 (a base-vs-instruct result where behaviour moved by half a scale while unit identity barely moved - a gain change on a fixed set) and run 762 (post-training that moved a circuit, where the cost was that the circuit described a different network), the recurrence is that **the most common causal effect this folder measures is a change in a rate constant, and an intervention study that reports only the treated arm's endpoint will report it as a change in the endpoint.** Mechanical rule for us: **every intervention we report carries the post-intervention endpoint of both arms as well as their trajectories, and if the arms re-converge we say the effect was on the rate and not on the mechanism - because "deleting these samples delays the head" and "deleting these samples prevents the head" are the same figure read at two x-values.**
- **Pattern:** **The control set here is the folder's best example of matching a data-side null on content rather than on count: the augmentation baseline inserts the *same bigrams and trigrams* found in the MDA-selected samples without the influence ranking, and the MDA arm still wins - which is the only way to separate "this specific set" from "any set with this surface statistic".** Paired with the QK-only probe variant (worse than the full-head probe, so the V/O pathway is doing real work in the ranking) and the cross-head projection check (the selected samples score an order of magnitude higher on induction heads), the entry shows what content-matched means on data rather than on units. Read against run 763 (random-head floor matched on size *and* layer) and run 765 (a comparative win scored against a baseline run at one eighth of the budget), the recurrence is that **a specificity claim is only as strong as the dimension it was matched on, and for data-side interventions the dimension is the surface statistic, not the count.** Mechanical rule for us: **any "this data mattered" claim we make ships a content-matched insertion arm built from the same n-gram or template statistics without the ranking, at the same count - because a count-matched random arm answers "is it data at all?" and the content-matched arm answers "is it this data?", and the second question is the one a data-curation tool has to answer.**

Figures read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**.
- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry where the one thing changed is the scalar at the root of the graph, and it is the cleanest demonstration yet that an attribution pipeline is a function of its target rather than of its tracer: the same Cross-Layer Transcoders, the same Jacobian, the same 80/95 pruning, rooted at a probe direction instead of a logit, yields a graph whose ablation arm flips the token 0% of the time on every concept in both models while the logit-rooted graph on identical prompts flips it 100% (92% for reasoning).** That is a double dissociation in the strict sense, run in both directions with the shared subgraph as the third arm, and the paper does not have to argue that the two graphs are different because it ablates each one against the other's outcome. Read against run 766 (where a ranking was only credited once it predicted the intervention it fed) and run 760 (where the record with the most fields scored second-worst), the recurrence is that **in this folder the target of a reading is an experimental variable that is almost never varied, and varying it is cheaper than any method improvement on top of a fixed target.** Mechanical rule for us: **every attribution or circuit our white-box model emits names its root scalar explicitly, and we run the alternative root (the probe score, the next token, the layer's own output) as a second arm on the same prompts - because a graph that explains the logit and a graph that explains the representation are both true and neither is the model, and only the double ablation tells us which one we published.**
- **Pattern:** **The graph-structure predictor is the folder's first probe-free diagnostic that is scored as a regression across layers with the leakage the target would otherwise permit closed off - whole (concept, layer) groups held out, a de-meaned target, a layer-mean baseline that goes negative on the raw target, and a permutation null at −0.005 ± 0.128 - and the honesty is in the three numbers that fall apart as you move off the headline: ρ 0.905 raw and 0.925 de-meaned on Gemma becomes 0.808 raw but 0.34 de-meaned on Llama with an interval straddling zero, and held-out-concept transfer runs −0.74 to +0.48.** The authors attribute the collapse to 16 layers compressing the structural variation and to near-flat accuracy trajectories leaving nothing to fit, which is a claim about the substrate stated as a limitation rather than buried. Read against run 766 (where the ranking was credited only through its predicted effect size) and run 761 (where a method's gain lived entirely in the tasks it was designed around), the recurrence is that **a predictor's headline is the version of it with the most variance left in the target, and the de-meaned, cross-concept and cross-model versions are where a diagnostic earns the right to be used on a new checkpoint.** Mechanical rule for us: **every predictive statistic our model reports is published twice, once on the raw target and once on the target with the group mean removed, with the number of held-out groups stated - because "structure predicts probe quality" at 0.91 raw and 0.34 de-meaned is one sentence and two different tools, and the second one is the one that would survive a new model.**
- **Pattern:** **The measurement that makes this entry a safety rather than a demonstration is the exclusive-versus-shared split, and its most informative cell is the one where the dissociation is weakest: truthfulness on Gemma loses 93% of its probe score and flips 67% of tokens when the SHARED subgraph is ablated, while the probe-exclusive arm only reaches a 25% flip rate - so the concept whose representation is most entangled with generation is the one where "the model knows" and "the model says" stop being separable, and the leakage is localised to the intersection rather than to either affinity.** The three concepts that dissociate cleanly have logit-exclusive subgraphs 1.77-1.94x the size of the probe-exclusive ones, reasoning inflates to 4.15x at an anti-aligned probe position (baseline score −21.44, all ΔS signs inverted and the dissociation still qualitatively intact), and Llama truthfulness at layer 1 has about 11 exclusive nodes and 55.2% accuracy, so its clean 0% flip is a statement about a circuit too weak to move anything. Read against run 759 (a per-input dormancy spectrum that an aggregate hid) and run 755 (a headline dissolved by a per-unit denominator), the recurrence is that **an ablation arm's null result has two readings - the pathway is separate, or the circuit is too small to see - and the size column beside the effect column is the only thing that distinguishes them.** Mechanical rule for us: **every dissociation we report carries the node count of each ablated subspace next to its effect, and a concept that only dissociates through its shared arm is reported as entangled rather than as separable - because a 0% flip from eleven nodes and a 0% flip from two hundred are not the same evidence, and safety claims built on probes are exactly where that difference decides whether the monitor is reading the model's belief or its phrasing.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first screen whose ranking is scored as a predictor of the intervention it feeds, and the score is what makes the whole entry usable: the signed importance predicts the linear functional at Pearson 0.97 with 3.7% relative error, predicts the gap drop, and predicts the greedy-decoding flip count (12/14 at K = 50, 14/14 at K = 1000) - three levels of the same quantity, all reported.** That is the shape our white-box model has to answer to. Read against run 765 (per-unit readings published as group objects with a certificate vector, where the honest ranking was barely better than a shuffled null) and run 760 (six fields of a record, the richest of which read second-worst), the recurrence is that **a ranking's value is decided by the intervention column beside it, and the only ranking worth publishing in this folder is one whose predicted effect sizes are printed next to the measured ones.** Mechanical rule for us: **every per-unit score we emit ships with its predicted intervention effect as a number, and the comparison runs at three levels - the linear functional, the behavioural proxy, and the end-to-end output - because a 0.97 at the proxy level and 12/14 at the output level are the same result told with different honesty.**
- **Pattern:** **The null here is the folder's cleanest case of a method that returns something and means nothing, and the paper's diagnostic names the reason before the ablation is run: six of eight behaviours produce top-50 neuron sets with high signed importance whose ablation moves the behaviour by 0%, while the FFN/Skip ratio separates the two populations at R² = 0.81 across 13 models.** The mechanism is stated as a sign structure rather than a strength one - in routing circuits the identified neurons are *responders* with high `|Δa_n|` and negligible `|c_n|`, so they react to the perturbation and do not project onto the decision. Read against run 765 (activation-only scores beaten by a shuffled null) and run 754/755's per-unit collapses, the recurrence is that **an attribution score is a product of two factors that can each be high for the wrong reason, and the diagnostic that matters is which factor is doing the work.** Mechanical rule for us: **we never report a composite score without its two factors separately, and when a factor is near zero on a behaviour we report that behaviour as out of scope for the method rather than reporting a weak score - because a 0% ablation next to a top-50 list is a statement about the unit type, and the entry that says so is worth more than the one that tunes it.**
- **Pattern:** **The behavioural classifier is what turns this entry from an alarm into an audit: ablating 50 neurons changes 80% of 520 AdvBench response templates, but the direction of that change is a swap from a formulaic refusal to a pre-training harm warning (296/520), with 18% hedged and 3 actionable - so the fragile layer is the *phrasing* of refusal and the robust layer underneath it survives 500-neuron ablation.** The three-regime finding (template 0-50, warning 50-200, factual argument 200+) is measured by progressive ablation on the same prompts, which is the cheap version of an ablation ladder nobody else in this folder runs to that depth. Read against run 759 (a second replacement value weakening a "removable" claim) and run 757 (a set whose removal improved held-out accuracy), the recurrence is that **an ablation's headline number and its behavioural meaning are separated by a classifier, and the classifier is where the entry earns or loses its safety claim.** Mechanical rule for us: **every ablation we publish in a behavioural setting is scored by outcome category, not by trigger rate, and the categories are named in the entry - because "80% of responses changed" and "0.6% of responses became harmful" are compatible with each other and only the second one is a safety result.**

- **Pattern:** **This entry is the folder's first where the winning configuration is measured outside the losing method's feasible range, and the paper says so in a figure caption rather than in the result: LRP's best compression row needs 8,192 reference samples, and Wanda's official implementation "cannot" exceed 1,024 for GPU memory, so the 11.35-perplexity win is scored against a baseline run at one eighth of the budget.** Read against run 758 (a predictive statistic reported with its saturation point, so the reader could see where the fit stopped buying) and run 761 (a method whose gain concentrated entirely in the tasks it was designed around), the recurrence is that **in this folder a comparative headline is usually a comparison of two different experimental budgets, and the budget gap is disclosed somewhere in the appendix.** Mechanical rule for us: **every arm in one of our comparisons is run at the sample budget of the cheapest arm, and any result reported above that budget carries the budget it used in the same cell - because "ours is better when it gets 8x the data" is a sentence about data, and a reader who cannot see the 8x will file it as a sentence about method.**
- **Pattern:** **The most useful negative result here is a null that localises the unit rather than the method: structured pruning of attention heads for IOI fails for LRP, Wanda, gradient and activation alike, while the same four methods succeed on MLP neurons - and the authors read it as OPT-125M's heads cooperating rather than any small subset being dispensable.** That is the folder's clean answer to "what does a method that returns nothing mean": not weak attribution, wrong unit. Read against run 764 (component identity survived fine-tuning while edge wiring subtyped the verdict) and run 760 (all six fields at Bonferroni p = 1.0 on GPT-2 while the CNN arm passed both directions), the recurrence is that **a causal unit menu is an empirical finding, and the test of the menu is whether every method in a panel fails on it together.** Mechanical rule for us: **when our white-box model returns a mirage on one unit type, we do not tune the attribution - we re-run the panel at the coarser unit and report both, because a null shared by four independent rankings is evidence about the substrate, and the entry that reports the null is more predictive than the entry that reports the win.**
- **Pattern:** **Differential attribution is the cheapest editing primitive in the folder and it is a pure double-difference: score every parameter twice, once on references that elicit the behaviour and once on a general corpus, subtract, prune the most negative - with no patching, no fine-tuning, and no forward pass over the target inputs at all.** The cost statement in App. G is what makes it a real option: one forward-backward per reference sample at 5 seconds, and the toxicity arm concentrates on 100 fc1 neurons (0.3%) while repetition needs about 7,000 weight elements (0.03%), so the size of the edit is itself a readout of how concentrated the behaviour is. Read against run 762 (a trainable intervention certified against the untouched model) and run 760 (a vocabulary pivot that moved recovery from 3/16 to 13/16), the distinction is that the trained objects require a behavioural proof to be trusted while a subtraction requires only the reference sets to be right - which is precisely the limitation the authors name and do not solve. Mechanical rule for us: **any contrastive score we emit carries the two reference distributions that produced it, in the record, not in the appendix - because a differential is an assertion about what distinguishes two corpora, and if the benign corpus overlaps the behaviour corpus the score measures the overlap, not the behaviour.**

Read abstract, §I Introduction with all four contributions, §II A-E in full (the superposition framing, the TopK-SAE objective Eq. 1, ACDC/attribution-patching §II-C, the edge-pruning optimisation Eq. 2, and the validation-circularity admission in §II-E), §III A-F in full (Eqs. 3-10: the cross-layer prediction head, the three-term composite loss, Algorithm 1, the gradient-activation attribution, Definition 1 and the normalized Laplacian Eq. 6, **Theorem 1 and its proof read line by line**, the graph-attention messages Eqs. 7-8, and the necessity/sufficiency protocol Eqs. 9-10), §IV A-D with **Tables I-VI read cell by cell** (the nine-model spec grid; the modular-arithmetic discovery table including ACDC's OOM row; the natural-language table; the transfer-coefficient matrix; the four-configuration ablation with relative times; the four-scale memory/time/throughput table), §V A-E with every preservation, node, edge and runtime number, §VI A-D including **all five named limitations in full**, and §VII Conclusion. Figures 1-4 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; references read for works cited in §I-§II and to attribute the 15-20% residual claim, which the paper states as a critique of SAE methods **without a citation or a measurement of its own** - so it is not a number from this paper's experiments and the entry says so.
- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first circuit-discovery pipeline that states its search cost as a theorem, and the theorem is the part that transfers while the object it searches for is the part that does not: O(n^2 log n) against O(2^n) is a real reduction in the search space, but the search runs over transcoder features with attention excluded entirely, so the polynomial win is a win on a candidate graph that cannot contain half of what a transformer computes.** The authors state the consequence themselves - for coreference, where token-to-token routing is the computation, the extracted circuit is a fragment - and then still report a 0.86 preservation for WinoGrande at 312 nodes. Read against run 764 (base-vs-instruct, where component identity survived fine-tuning while edge wiring sub-typed the verdict) and run 762 (circuit condensation, where the object was built rather than found and had to answer for the untouched model), the recurrence is that **every circuit in this folder is defined relative to an earlier choice of what counts as a node, and the choice is the assumption that does the work.** Mechanical rule for us: **our white-box model's unit menu is declared before any measurement, and every claim that comes out of it is labelled with what the menu cannot express - because a pipeline that omits a component type is not a partial pipeline, it is a pipeline for a different model, and "preserves 86% of behaviour" on the excluded half is a number about the residue.**
- **Pattern:** **The reduction in this paper is obtained by coarsening the graph before searching it, which is the cleanest statement in this folder of a technique we already half-use: cluster the vertices spectrally at each resolution, replace each cluster with a supernode, and let a learned policy decide which supernodes are worth descending into - so the refinement depth becomes a parameter instead of an implicit property of the method.** The price is stated honestly in Table V and is small: removing the hierarchy takes modular-arithmetic preservation from 0.91 to 0.89 while cutting runtime to 0.3x, meaning most of the coarse structure was recoverable without the ladder, whereas the cross-layer prediction head is what costs quality when removed (0.91 -> 0.84 modular arithmetic, 0.85 -> 0.76 QA). Read against run 763 (circuit records compared by theta-subsumption, where the comparison relation carried the value) and run 762 (Rank/Prune/Heal, where the search was replaced by a controller), the recurrence is that **in this folder the productive move is never a better search over the full object - it is a change of resolution, and the change is worth measuring against the cheap version of itself.** Mechanical rule for us: **every screening method we publish ships with its own coarse-only arm and its finer arm on the same benchmark, so the reader sees what the extra resolution bought in exchange for the extra factor - as here, where 3.3x the runtime buys 0.02 of preservation.**
- **Pattern:** **The transfer matrix is the entry's most reusable artefact and it says the thing the folder has now measured three ways: 0.82 for Llama-7B to Llama-70B, 0.71-0.73 for Pythia to Llama and GPT-2 to Pythia on modular arithmetic, 0.38 for GPT-2 to Llama on WinoGrande - similarity falls with family distance and with linguistic rather than algorithmic task type, and both effects are visible in one table rather than argued.** The authors read the within-family edge as tokenizer and training-recipe influence on topology, which is exactly the reading run 764's base-vs-instruct result supports from a different direction (fine-tuning a shared checkpoint preserves components; changing the whole training regime does not). Read against run 763 (structural predicates transfer where components do not, with node Jaccard 0.09-0.24) and run 762 (the condensed circuit describes the adapted network), the recurrence is that **transfer is not a property of a circuit but of a distance between two networks along a training axis, and the axis with the smallest distance is same-architecture same-recipe at different scale.** Mechanical rule for us: **any cross-model claim we make states the axis (scale, fine-tuning step, recipe, architecture) and the measured distance along it - because "circuits transfer" at 0.82 and at 0.38 are the same sentence with opposite advice for a deployed tool.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry that answers a base-versus-instruct question at the level of the circuit, and the answer splits cleanly along the axes: behaviour moves by half the CRR scale while head identity barely moves at all.** The same late-layer heads carry the decision in both variants (J@10 0.67-0.82, |score| Spearman 0.82-0.91, random-set null cleared at z = 24-40), only 2-3 heads per family change memory/context role, and median depth shifts at most two layers, so what tuning buys is a **gain change on a fixed set**, not a new mechanism. Read against run 763 (theta-subsumption, where "these two circuits are the same kind" became four assessable answers) and run 762 (circuit condensation, where post-training moved the circuit and the cost was that the circuit now described a different network), the recurrence is that **networks reached from one another by training keep their components far more often than the field's framing of "fine-tuning changes circuits" implies, and the observable that moves is amplitude.** Mechanical rule for us: **when our white-box model is fitted on one checkpoint and evaluated on another, we decompose the prediction error into a structural term (unit identity and ranking) and a gain term (per-unit weights) and report them separately - because "the circuit is preserved" and "the behaviour changed" are both true here and any single number would have to trade one against the other.**
- **Pattern:** **The framing experiment is the cheapest result in the entry and it invalidates the headline the entry would otherwise have led with: the same false fact as a fluent evidential passage sends both base and instruct to 0.88-1.00 and collapses the base/instruct gap from -0.412/-0.425/-0.501 to -0.029/-0.122/-0.005.** The terse-vs-coherent contrast is one prompt rewrite, and it converts "instruction tuning makes models robust to injection" into "instruction tuning makes models reject unsupported one-liners specifically" - with abstention rising alongside (Gemma 0 to 48 "neither" prompts), which is why the "neither" bucket had to be measured rather than scored as memory-following. Read against run 761 (a method's gain concentrated entirely in the multi-step-composition tasks) and run 755 (an aggregate differential that dissolved under a per-unit denominator), the recurrence is that **a behavioural effect size is a property of the stimulus set at least as much as of the model, and the cheapest variance-reduction technique is to vary the phrasing while holding the fact fixed.** Mechanical rule for us: **every behavioural claim we make about a mechanism is paired with a surface-form paraphrase condition, and if the effect does not survive the paraphrase we report the effect as a property of the phrasing rather than of the mechanism - one templated rewrite is the cheapest control in this whole folder.**
- **Pattern:** **The random-head floor here is the best-instrumented instance of the folder's standing "matched control" rule, because it matches on the two things that would otherwise explain the result: size (5 heads) and location (same upper-half layers), with the selected heads explicitly excluded.** Ten random sets per direction land at -0.03 to +0.02 mean Delta CRR against -0.10 to -0.83 for the selected context heads, so the selected effect beats the most extreme of ten random sets in all six variants for context suppression and four of six for memory suppression - and the two exceptions are named rather than buried (Llama, where the memory effect is small in absolute terms). Paired with the pre-registered sign test (36/36, binomial p about 2^-36) and the locally-insignificant path-patching leg (per-cell p 0.10-0.42, confidence from six-cell consistency), the entry shows the ladder: a null, a sign test, a floor, and an honest statement of which rung is load-bearing. Read against run 759 (a zero-ablation result materially weakened by a distribution-matched replacement) and run 751 (a specificity ratio whose matched-random control was the same order as the treated effect), the recurrence is that **"specificity" is only ever as strong as the control it was matched on, and location and size are the two dimensions that most often go unmatched.** Mechanical rule for us: **every selection-based causal claim ships a floor computed from random sets matched on count AND on the structural covariate (layer, depth, neighbourhood) that the selection rule used, and the number of random draws is stated - because "beats random" against an unmatched random set is not a specificity claim.**

Authors' affiliations are **not on the arXiv record** (no institution field), so the entry names the author list without an organisation and says so.
- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry where the artefact is the RECORD rather than the finding, and it supplies the one thing every previous entry was missing: an explicit relation between two mechanisms' descriptions. Theta-subsumption turns "these two circuits are similar" into four assessable answers - generalises, specialises, equivalent, incomparable - and the paper demonstrates the payoff on a five-task pool where the single-predicate IOI clause subsumes everything, Time subsumes Path, and Location is incomparable to both because it commits to different motifs.** Read against run 762 (where the circuit was an objective and the proof obligation was behavioural) and run 760 (where a six-field record lost to a two-field one), the recurrence is that **the interpretability field's bottleneck this folder keeps bumping into is representational rather than causal: we can measure an intervention precisely and still be unable to say whether two measurements are about the same thing.** Mechanical rule for us: **our white-box model's unit of storage is a record with a comparison operation, not a table of per-unit scores - and the operation is a partial order, so the model is allowed to answer "incomparable" rather than forcing every pair of mechanisms into a distance.**
- **Pattern:** **The transfer result separates retrieval from identity in a way worth copying wholesale, and the separation is visible in one column of Table 14: node overlap across models is essentially nil (Jaccard 0.09-0.24 within Pythia, lower across families), so structural transfer by component identity is impossible, yet scale-invariant predicates retrieve acceptable candidates for all five tasks in both directions - and the accept/reject pattern is identical from a source whose IOI circuit has Delta_T = -0.91 and one whose IOI circuit has Delta_T = 5.24, which proves the signature, not the source's behaviour, is driving retrieval.** The authors are careful to call this a proposal-and-validation procedure rather than proof of mechanism identity, and the retrieval is genuinely loose at the binding end: every semantic role selects **Goal**, a task absent from the source pool, on motif-vocabulary grounds alone, with accept rates from 3/32 to 25/41. Read against run 762 (a condensed circuit that must answer for the original model) and run 754 (cross-granularity comparison where the baseline's grammar, not its optimiser, explained part of the gap), the recurrence is that **a structural description travels where the components do not, but what travels is a hypothesis about kind, not a claim about identity.** Mechanical rule for us: **when we carry a reading from one checkpoint to another we report retrieval rate and validation rate as two numbers, never one accuracy - because a 22/29 acceptance with the wrong task name and a 3/32 acceptance with the right one are both failures of a single collapsed metric, and the signature's job is only the first number.**
- **Pattern:** **The failure mode here is the folder's most familiar one arriving at the level of the explanatory object itself: the record that scores 0.93 on IOI scores 0.31 on Location and 0.31 on Beneficiary, and the diagnostic that explains it is a ratio of within-task to between-task structural distance near 1.0 - three prompt splits of the same role circuit are as structurally different from each other as they are from other tasks' circuits, while LLaMA IOI sits at 2.3x.** Note the split is *not* by architecture but by task kind: the attention-copying mechanism is prompt-invariant and the MLP-binding one is not, which also predicts the transfer asymmetry (IOI and GT find exact task matches at 8/41 and 2/37-41; binding tasks accept 3 to 25 candidates and land on the wrong role). Read against run 760 (a per-field ablation showing the richest record is second-worst) and run 755 (an aggregate differential dissolved by a per-unit denominator), the recurrence is that **in this folder every summary statistic is really a statement about variance across the samples that produced it, and the cheapest exposure is always a split.** Mechanical rule for us: **every mechanism record we emit carries a stability column computed across discovery splits (at least three disjoint prompt or data subsets) before it is admitted to the registry - a mechanism whose description is not more similar to itself than to its neighbours is a single-sample description, whatever its confidence on that sample.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry where the circuit is an OBJECTIVE rather than a finding, and the argument structure it introduces is worth more than the result: if the object you want to read cannot be extracted from the frozen model, you may be allowed to build one - provided you then prove the built object still answers for the untouched model, with a behavioural test and not a distributional one.** The proof here is two-layered and the second layer is the interesting one: KL to the original (median 0.215; IOI anchor 0.056 against the matched frozen circuit's 0.50) is the distributional half, and the competence readout - ranking the ORIGINAL model's own errors by the margin read through the condensed circuit, AUROC 0.894 against 0.645 at matched edge budget, ahead in 10 of 11 evaluable cells - is the behavioural half that a re-expressed activation could not pass by accident. Read against run 760 (where the richest record was the second-worst of six and the fix was leave-one-out within the full record) and run 754 (where the method's own justification for corrupting rather than zeroing was that zeroing lets the target learn a new solution), the recurrence is that **any intervention strong enough to make a model legible is strong enough to make it a different model, and the only tests that separate explanation from re-description are the ones scored against the pre-intervention behaviour.** Mechanical rule for us: **whenever we make anything trainable in order to make it readable - an adapter, a transcoder, an interpretable bottleneck, a probe fitted on a re-expressed activation - the claim ships with a matched test against the untouched weights, and that test is the model's own error predictions, not KL alone; a KL of 0.2 nats can coexist with a circuit that has quietly become the explanation of a different network.**
- **Pattern:** **The verification ladder in this entry is the most directly reusable artefact for our own evaluation design, because it says exactly where each question becomes answerable and why the thresholds are what they are: subset enumeration over all 2^k circuits is practical to k <= 13 (5,952 masked circuits), unusable by k = 17, and every matched frozen circuit sits above the limit (smallest 68 edges, median 416); per-edge necessity needs k <= 8 to be total (100% of edges individually necessary at <= 8 edges against a 2.5-15% median across tasks and 0.0% for matched frozen circuits); and the pair test scales as k(k-1)/2, which is why the 169,476 ablations only became affordable after condensation.** The pair test is also what produced the entry's strongest negative result: independence of edge effects failed in every circuit tested, with a median 7.4 interacting partners per edge and 87% of the strongest interactions sub-additive, so a circuit can be mapped completely and still not be readable one edge at a time. Read against run 754 (per-layer topology as the reusable summary, and the warning that a component story built on one checkpoint cannot separate mechanism from layer-index accident) and run 755 (a per-unit denominator dissolving a headline), the recurrence is that **the granularity at which a claim is checkable is a property of set size, not of the method, and a claim that was only sampled must be labelled as a sample.** Mechanical rule for us: **every interpretability claim we emit states its exhaustive limit - the k at which we enumerated every subset, the k at which necessity is total, the number of pairs actually run against the number implied - because "no smaller circuit suffices" and "no smaller circuit we sampled sufficed" are different sentences and only one of them is worth a theorem.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry that separates the score from the selection rule and shows the improvement lives entirely in one half of the benchmark, and it is the cleanest warning yet against reading an aggregate win as a general one: on the two local-association tasks in the suite (gender bias, capital-to-country) all three estimators land within about two points of each other and the new method sometimes loses, while on IOI, SVA and hypernymy - the tasks that need multi-step composition - the gap opens to 17.7 points; the paper's own explanation is that local tasks are direct feature mappings rather than reasoning.** Read against run 755 (a single recovered set repairing three measurement failures at once, where the repair was readable in one table) and run 757 (a beneficial-ablation result where one screen produced opposite-signed sets), the recurrence is that this folder keeps discovering that a method's reported gain is concentrated in a subset of its own evaluation, and that the subset is usually the one the method was designed around. Mechanical rule for us: **every attribution or discovery score we compare against a baseline gets a per-task spread table and an explicit floor check - name the tasks on which the baseline already saturates and show we do not lose there - because a mean over a suite containing saturated tasks is a number about the suite's composition, not about the method.**
- **Pattern:** **The estimator critique here is cheap and transferable, and it is about *where you evaluate the gradient* rather than about what you measure: integrating along a model-independent straight line from the clean to the corrupted activation spends most samples where the loss is flat, and the fix is to bend the path with one gradient step per sample toward the corrupted prediction.** The paper states the consequence for us directly - the number of steps is its only hyperparameter, `T = 1` collapses to plain EAP and `T = 2` to averaging the clean and corrupted gradients, so the sweep is a continuum from the old method to the new one rather than a replacement, and more steps can *degrade* the result once the normalised step size grows in a region where directions are unstable. Read against run 758 (a residual read at the wrong measurement point correlating at −0.05 while the MLP end of the same block correlated at −0.21) and run 759 (a dormancy rule that failed on one model because a structural assumption was smuggled into a cheap statistic), the recurrence is that **where in the computation and where along the interpolation you read the gradient decides the sign and size of the answer more than the reading method does**. Mechanical rule for us: **any attribution we publish states its interpolation path, its sample count per path, and whether a one-step and a two-step version of the same score were run as controls - because otherwise a null result is indistinguishable from a path that never left the flat region.**

Figures 1-23 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**.
- **Pattern:** **The field-ablation result here is the folder's best evidence that adding information to a record can degrade it, and the interference is not a bug but is priced and published: on the GPT-2 schema, adding E on top of G+S+R drops recall below the G+S+R level at p < 1e-8, adding D at p = .028, and the full six-field schema reaches 0.196 against 0.411 for the two-field core, so the record that contains the most information is the second worst of the six subsets tested.** Read against run 759 (a per-input dormancy spectrum showing an aggregate hides two populations) and run 755 (a headline dissolved by a per-unit denominator), the recurrence is that **in this folder the richer representation is repeatedly the one that reads worse, and the mechanism is always that the extra field changes the retrieval/scoring surface rather than merely adding to it.** Mechanical rule for us: **every field we add to the emitted record is admitted by a leave-one-out test inside the context of the other fields, not by a standalone usefulness judge - because a field that is individually informative can still win the retrieval budget away from a stronger field, and the only measurement that sees this is the ablation of the field from the full record.**
- **Pattern:** **Sufficiency and necessity come out of this paper with radically different confidence, and the difference is a property of the substrate rather than of the method: on the CNN the same retrieval rule gives +69.6 points of sufficiency and +34.7 points of necessity over matched random, while on GPT-2 all six fields return Bonferroni p = 1.0 and the framework loses to the oracle at oracle-set-matched k = 7 (0.037 restored against 0.117) with the apparent win at k = 30 coming from a 4.3x larger head set.** The authors say this in the appendix in as many words. Read against run 757 (a signed ablation set where removal helps) and run 756 (a recovery set whose knockout is the only test that separates it from "more of the same"), the distinction is that those entries show an ablation number can be the wrong sign or the wrong control, while this one shows the **same method can pass both directions on one architecture and neither on another**, so a causal claim carried across architectures is a fresh claim. Mechanical rule for us: **we state per architecture whether a unit set passed sufficiency, necessity, both or neither, and we never transfer a causal verdict between substrates - because a dispersed circuit (the 7 oracle IOI heads themselves restore only 0.117) has no small sufficient set, and an upper bound on restoration is a property of the circuit's fan-out, not of our reading.**
- **Pattern:** **The vocabulary pivot is the most operationally useful artefact in the entry: the same schema with S populated by 48 surface linguistic concepts recovers 3 of 16 literature-documented heads, and with four MI-derived positional primitives (induction target, IO-position, previous token, self) it recovers 13 of 16, with the three misses named as one backup name-mover and two S-inhibition heads.** The paper states the general rule that follows - a component's S vocabulary should be drawn from the space of outputs that component type produces, not from surface features of its inputs - and pairs it with a cheap falsification step: a pilot recovery test against a small reference set before committing to full extraction. Read against run 758 (a predictive statistic fitted on units rather than samples, with its saturation point stated) and run 759 (a structural statistic that encodes an unstated location assumption), the recurrence is that **a feature vocabulary is an assumption about where a component's meaning lives, and the assumption is testable for the price of a pilot.** Mechanical rule for us: **before we index any unit by a vocabulary we run a reference-set recovery pilot against heads our own literature already labels and report recall@threshold, because a 3/16 is not a weak result to be improved later - it is evidence the vocabulary is measuring the wrong space.**
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** Priority block empty, so the carried **sufficiency** thread (open since run 758) was searched: `abs:"circuit" AND abs:"sufficient" AND abs:"mechanistic" AND cat:cs.LG` (12 hits) surfaced this paper at rank 7 and `2609.35686` (Rethinking Circuit Evaluation: Do Circuits Explain Model Errors?) which gate-tested **already read** (`aether 758 True`); `2603.30002` is also already read. `2607.00089` gate-tested FREE before the fetch (`aether 758 False core 193 False`) and again after the write (`aether 759 True core 193 False`); title-substring count on the INDEX body was 0 before the write. **The abstract-only claim was checked against the body and one number was corrected during writing:** the `/abs/` page says CNN filters found by the schema "satisfy causal sufficiency and necessity", which is true only on the CNN - the GPT-2 arm is negative on both directions (App. G). **A discovery-query note:** the arXiv API was not rate-limited this session and `/tmp/lit/qq.py` (a 3-attempt retry wrapper printing `id | date | title`) worked first try; the first query tried, `abs:"sufficiency" AND abs:"circuit" AND abs:"reconstruct"`, returned ten physics and quantum-coding papers, i.e. the phrase "circuit" pulls hard out of cs.LG unless `cat:cs.LG` is pinned. **Pin the category on every query from now on.**

Figures 1-14 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**.
- **Pattern:** **The losing definition here fails for a locatable reason and not for noise, and the reason is a structural assumption smuggled into a cheap statistic: "dormant" defined as high average attention to position zero presumes the sink sits at position zero, so on OLMo-2-1124-7B - which splits attention between two sinks, the second at an intermediate position - it identifies 0.0% of heads at every budget on every task, including GSM8K, while the output-norm rule reaches 1.8/14.5/16.0%.** Read against run 758 (an ablation read at two measurement points inside one block correlating at −0.05 and −0.21, so the residual was the wrong place to read the write) and run 755 (a dual head set whose 4.8-6.4x aggregate differential vanished per unit), the recurrence is that **every cheap statistic in this folder encodes an unstated assumption about where the interesting quantity lives, and the assumption is invisible until a model violates it.** Mechanical rule for us: **for every structural statistic we use to select units we state the invariant it presumes (fixed sink position, monotone depth, layer-uniform scale, single-corpus stationarity) and we test it on the one model in our panel most likely to violate it before it is allowed to select anything - because a selection rule that returns an empty set on one model is not a weak signal, it is a false premise, and the diagnostic is a single per-model null-return check that costs one forward pass.** The layer-relative normalisation is the transferable fix: dividing a unit's statistic by the mean over its own layer is what let one threshold array work across 28-48 layer networks.
- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's sharpest evidence that "unimportant" is a per-input selection and not a property of a unit - the same head is dormant on one question and active on the next, the set is re-chosen every forward pass, and the aggregate accuracy table hides a per-head dormancy spectrum that is bimodal: 64-82% of heads are dormant on at most 3% of MMLU questions, 46 of Llama-3.1-8B's 1,024 heads on at least 95% of them, and almost none (12 in Qwen2.5-14B, 0-1 in the other five) on all 14,042.** The paper is honest about the consequence it cannot buy: the rule cannot be turned into a static pruned model, and it says so before an efficiency reader can file it as a pruning paper. Read against run 757 (a beneficial-ablation set frozen on a disjoint discovery split, which is the same selection made *once*, offline, and averaged) and run 758 (importance estimated by repeated random joint ablation, 3,000 trials, one ranking per task), the distinction is that those entries estimate a corpus-level average and treat it as the unit's importance, while this one measures how much that average is a compromise - a head dormant on 95% of inputs contributes ~5% of a signal to the aggregate and is effectively invisible. Mechanical rule for us: **every unit-importance number we publish is accompanied by its input-conditional distribution - the fraction of inputs on which the unit is inert, the fraction on which it is load-bearing, and whether that distribution is bimodal - because a mean ablation delta over a corpus is a frequency-weighted blend of two populations, and the population a white-box model must predict on the next input is not the blend.** This is also why the random control here has to be size-matched *per input*: an across-corpus control zeroes a fixed random set and is not a fair null for a per-input rule.
- **Pattern:** **Two cheap checks in the appendix are worth stealing outright because each turns a plausible worry into a measured non-issue in one forward pass: dormancy rates are re-measured inside the modified model and are unchanged by the intervention (so the fixpoint problem - zeroing heads in layer 6 changes what downstream layers look dormant - does not arise), and the ablation is re-run with Gaussian noise matched to each head's own mean and standard deviation instead of zero, which preserves accuracy for a smaller fraction of heads (over 11% of Llama-3.2-3B's heads at the 0.5% budget).** The second result bounds the first from below: zeroing is not merely "a small perturbation", it is the *right-shaped* one, because head outputs are approximately zero-mean - so "these heads can be deleted for free" is true under a specific replacement value and materially weaker under a distribution-preserving one. Read against run 754 (the paper's own justification for refusing zero and mean ablation because they shift the input distribution) and run 752 (an intervention parameterised as a signed dose in the pair's own units, so the ablation value is on a scale the data chose), the recurrence is that **the ablation value is part of the estimand and the cheapest robustness test is a second replacement value on the same set.** Mechanical rule for us: **every "removable" set we report carries a second-arm replacement (matched-noise, or mean over a held-out corpus) and the fraction removable is reported for both - because a cell that survives zeroing and not a matched-noise swap is measuring the zero, and a cell that survives both is measuring the head.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry where the same intervention is read at two measurement points inside one block, and the two readings disagree by a factor of four - the ablation-induced representational change correlates with head-importance rank at -0.05 after the attention residual and -0.21 after the MLP - which converts a null into a localisation: the residual made the attention write look flat, and the MLP is where the write is actually consumed.** Read against run 750 (an aggregate loss delta at z +1.95 next to a target logit at z -52.5, diagnosed as downstream redundancy) and run 755 (a dual head set whose 4.8-6.4x aggregate differential vanishes per unit), the recurrence is that **where you read an intervention decides what it appears to do, and the residual stream is the worst place to read it.** Mechanical rule for us: **every intervention we score carries its measurement point named explicitly, and for any target quantity read off the residual stream we also read the same quantity after the next sublayer in the same block before concluding the effect is weak - because a residual add is a running sum, an effect that is flat there may merely have been added to something larger, and the before/after pair inside one block is the cheapest localisation available (no extra forward passes, one hook in, one hook out).**
- **Pattern:** **The sign-flipped ranking here is produced by a *different property*, not by a joint ablation, which is the cleanest contrast to run 757's beneficial-ablation result: cumulatively ablating the most spatially local 25% of heads first costs 43.6 points on Action against 34.3 for the top 25% by importance, quartile cross-tabs show the two orderings pick largely different heads, and the mechanism is attributed rather than assumed - fixed spatiotemporal kernels restore 56% of the local-set loss versus 27% of the importance-set loss, so much of what local heads do is a static stencil, not input-dependent routing.** Read against run 757 (a signed beneficial-ablation result whose sign the theory derives from conflicting objectives) and run 756 (a zero-before-output-projection ablation that finds a set whose removal *improves* held-out accuracy), the distinction is that those entries get negative and positive deltas for the *same* ranking under different seeds, while this one gets a larger positive-importance delta for a set chosen by an orthogonal statistic - so importance is not merely sign-ambiguous, it is not sufficient. Mechanical rule for us: **we keep at least one control ordering chosen by a property that is not the one we rank by (locality, distance, frequency, depth), and when that control out-damages our ranking we report the overlap between the two sets and a routing-only replacement test - because an importance score is a summary of one intervention family, and a set that loses more under cumulative ablation may be doing a simpler and more substitutable job than the mechanism we are looking for.**
- **Pattern:** **The predictive reading of the checkpoint is reported with its ceiling in the same table and the ceiling is the transferable part: ten cheap attention statistics, mRMR-selected and fitted with ExtraTrees inside repeated six-fold cross-validation, reach a held-out Spearman of 0.282 on V-JEPA (0.356 / 0.355 / 0.418 on the other three), saturating at seven properties, with mean spatial attention distance the dominant cue in all four models and the post-MLP representational response second in three.** The authors say plainly that the correlations are modest and that individual head properties explain only part of the variation, and then let the residue *set the agenda* for the rest of the paper (one section for each of the two surviving cues) - which is the honest use of a weak predictor rather than a dismissal of it. Read against run 752 (a weights-only wiring statistic at rho 0.55-0.82 published with what it cannot see - sign, magnitude, indirect path) and run 750 (two cheap proxies each failing in both directions across training), the recurrence is that **a structural predictor good enough to rank is not good enough to reason with, and the gap between the two is where the next experiment should go.** Mechanical rule for us: **every predictive statistic we publish for our white-box model is a held-out rank correlation (not a training-set fit), on a cross-validation scheme over the *units* rather than the samples, with its saturation point in property count stated - because a statistic fitted and evaluated on the same heads measures memorisation of 384 labels, and "we can rank heads at rho 0.3 from attention weights" is a real result only when the heads being ranked were never in the fit.**

- **Done:** arxiv-2610.21294 - Can Capability Enhancement be Localized? (read full v1 HTML: 114,377 chars plain-stripped / 1,547 lines, then `/tmp/lit/math21294.py` substituted the 807 `alttext=` annotations back in from the 332-`<math>` build into 90,818 chars / 1,555 lines with 155 rebuilt `<tr>` rows - needed because the plain strip's abstract was cut off mid-sentence and table cells were empty; read abstract, §1 Introduction with all three contributions, §2 Related Work, §3.1 (notation, the zero-before-output-projection ablation, the signed gain Δ, beneficial definition, the disjoint-discovery objective), §3.2 (three 3B models, four benchmarks, **Algorithm 1 in full**), §3.3 with **Table 1 read cell by cell** (all twelve rows incl. relative error reduction), §4.1 (the two conflicting Rademacher tasks, fixed coordinate encoding, paired memory bank, the closed-form softmax weight and head output, diagonal parameterisation, population-risk objective, small-Gaussian init and full-batch GD), §4.2 with **Proposition 4.1 in full** (the c_hj = o·v·tanh(qk) coefficient, w = sum_h c_hj, the exact risks R_A, R_B, and the unique aggregate optimum (a/2, b, c)), §4.3 with **Theorem 4.2** (convergence, per-feature localisation to one head with other coefficients O(α^4), the finite checkpoint N_α), §4.4 with **Theorem 4.3 and the event E = {h_0 ∉ {h_1,h_2\}}, Pr = 4/9**, the G_A = t^2 and D_B = 3t^2 statements and the liminf form, §5 Discussion and Limitations in full, §6 Conclusion, the AI-use statement; **Appendix A with Tables 2 and 3 read cell by cell**, B.0.1-B.2 as headings plus their statement lines. Proofs read as statements and proof sketches, **not** line by line. Figures: the paper has none in the body prose beyond tables; not inspected as images.
- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first signed beneficial-ablation result and it makes the point the whole ranking literature assumes away: the same screen produces a "task-critical" set when you look for damage and a "beneficial" set when you look for improvement, and neither is identified by effect size.** Every one of the twelve model-task pairs gains on held-out data once the subset is frozen on a disjoint discovery split (+13.10 points on SmolLM3 arithmetic, +6.30 on LLaMA arithmetic, ten pairs between +1.29 and +5.20), and the theory says why the sign can be negative at all: the shared coordinate's coefficient is pinned by the conflict, the optimum constrains only the *sum* w_j over heads, and GD's chosen decomposition puts that conflicted coordinate in one head - so deleting it is a positive move on the task that wanted it suppressed. Read against run 751 (a pattern with the opposite sign of causal effect, diagnosed as an interferer) and run 756 (a score family that must tie a dormant backup to an inert unit), the distinction is that run 751 documented a sign failure empirically and run 756 proved a ranking family blind, while this one gives a *generative account* of negative signs: they arise where two objectives disagree about one shared coordinate, not from noise. Mechanical rule for us: **every unit we call part of a mechanism carries a signed ablation delta per task, and a negative delta is reported as evidence that the objective conflicted rather than as an anomaly or a bad run - because the set that helps when removed and the set that hurts when removed are the same measurement with opposite signs, and a white-box model that predicts magnitude without sign cannot tell them apart.**
- **Pattern:** **The theoretical instrument is the deployment of a decomposition-of-the-optimum argument as a *null*, and it is cheap to reuse: the aggregate predictor is unique, the decomposition across heads is free, and the only thing that decides which decomposition training picks is the trajectory.** Proposition 4.1 does the first half in four lines of exact algebra (the model reduces to a linear predictor w^T x with w = sum over heads of o·v·tanh(qk), so the risks are quadratics in w and the joint optimum is (a/2, b, c)); Theorem 4.2 does the second half by showing small-initialisation GD makes one head per coordinate win with the losers at order α^4; Theorem 4.3 shows the consequence only fires on an event of probability 4/9. Read against run 750 (a proposal stage whose null passed while the verdict inverted) and run 753 (any fixed function of clean per-unit statistics ties a dormant unit to an inert one), the distinction is that those entries locate the blind spot in a *statistic*, while this one locates it in the gap between the optimum and the allocation - the loss landscape says nothing about which head holds what. Mechanical rule for us: **when we claim a weight-space statistic reveals a mechanism, we state the identification null explicitly - what the optimum pins (sums, aggregates) versus what it leaves free (allocation, decomposition) - and we treat the free part as the part that must be read from training dynamics or measured by intervention rather than inferred from the objective, because an optimum is a constraint on sums and a mechanism is a statement about terms.**

- **Pattern:** **The aggregate-versus-per-unit split run 755 named at the statistical level turns up here as a seed-dependence, and it is the entry's most transferable warning: the same score reports 0.91 AUC when seeded with the eight hand-labelled primaries, 0.80 with one primary, 0.60 with an attribution-derived seed, and 0.30 with an undirected energy-top seed - below the first-order floor of 0.33 it is supposed to beat.** The paper prints all four (Table 24) and draws the honest conclusion itself, that CoAx "completes a first-order-identified primary circuit by returning the backups that circuit hides" rather than discovering anything from scratch, and that as a standalone finder it peaks at 0.60 against a seed-free baseline's 0.82. Read against run 755 (a headline dissolved by a per-unit denominator) and run 751 (a specificity ratio whose matched-random control was of the same order as the treated effect), the recurrence is that **a benchmark number is a joint statement about a method and its input, and the input is usually the part a reader assumes away.** Mechanical rule for us: **every score we publish is reported across a ladder of input qualities (oracle seed, method-derived seed, structure-free seed) with the ladder's worst rung stated in the abstract's terms, not buried - because the top rung measures the score and the bottom rung measures the pipeline anyone else would actually run, and where the bottom rung sits below the baseline we are claiming to beat, the contribution is the completion step and must be described as one.**
- **Pattern:** **The formal statement here is the second impossibility result in the folder and it is the sharpest kind - about a family of scores rather than a method, and it comes with the exact pair of objects that makes the claim testable: any score that is a fixed function of a unit's clean-state per-unit statistic must assign a dormant backup and an inert unit the same value, since both statistics vanish; the escaping construction is the growth in effect after the seed is already removed, which for a dormant unit reduces to the energy of its second-order coupling to the seed.** The paper then prices the ingredients and the price is lopsided - conditioning alone takes 0.33 to 0.80, centring against the output distribution takes it to 0.91, Fisher weighting adds 0.01 - and demonstrates the failure empirically with a synthetic benchmark where first-order energy *anti*-ranks 100 planted backups at 0.42. Read against run 753 (the same shape: a proof that a whole family of clean-statistic scores cannot separate dormant from absent) and run 750 (a proposal stage that clears its null while the verdict inverts), the distinction is that run 753's theorem was the paper's headline support and this one's is load-bearing scaffolding for a downstream instrument - the same theorem used to justify a tool rather than a claim. Mechanical rule for us: **we keep one statement per entry of the form "no score of shape X can separate A from B", and we pair it with the measured number that shows X is what people actually use - because an impossibility result with no baseline ladder under it is rhetoric, and an impossibility result with the ladder's 0.33-under-a-0.91-headline is an engineering spec.**
- **Pattern:** **The downstream trio is the folder's cleanest demonstration that one recovered set can repair three different measurement failures at once, and the repair is readable in a single number that the paper puts in the same table as the failure: ablation of the "circuit" leaves IOI accuracy at 0.97 of 1.00 (behaviour surviving in full), knockout with the recovered backups added reaches 0.70 against a documented oracle of 0.72, the same number of the model's own next-ranked heads overshoots to 0.24, and the attribution of the same heads rises from a 0.22 logit-difference drop to 1.76.** The pruning arm isolates what conditioning is worth by holding the signal fixed: the static conditioned order and the sequential one share an identical score and differ only in re-measurement after each removal, and the gap between them (112.6 versus 80.6 perplexity at 50% sparsity, dense 47.9) is the value of conditioning alone. Read against run 752 (a dose axis showing a single deletion is one unmarked point on a curve) and run 748 (a capability measured under an intervention that the model had already compensated), the recurrence is that **an ablation result is an upper bound on a unit's importance whenever the model contains a substitute, and the bound is looser the more robust the circuit.** Mechanical rule for us: **before we report any knock-out number we report the knock-out of the primary alone, then the primary-plus-recovered-substitutes, then a size-matched set drawn from our own ranking - because the first number tells us the model repaired itself, the third tells us whether more removal is the whole story, and only the middle one is the circuit.**
