The author prints both and demotes the headline to "set-size signature", keeping only the class-conditional table as load-bearing. Read against run 748 (a suppression table whose null rows were printed, and whose two zero rows were the real scope of the claim) and run 744 (a rank cap that bought tractability at an unprinted accuracy cost), the recurrence is that **the number a reader remembers is usually the one without a denominator.** Mechanical rule for us: **every "group X carries most of the effect" claim we publish gets a per-unit column and a threshold/definition-sensitivity row in the same table, and if the sensitivity range spans the effect size we drop the ratio and keep only the qualitative ordering - because an aggregate differential is a product of per-unit effect and set size, and only one of those two is a fact about the mechanism.**
- **Pattern:** **The functional test is a change of *target*, not a change of *method*, and it is the cheapest decomposition in the folder: one ablation grid scored against two target classes splits a single "sink" into an execution layer that damages both and a detection layer that looks dominant and is null or sign-negative on one.** BOS-specialists are positive on BOS-context and on newline targets in all relevant cells (+3.11 / +0.75 / +7.42 / +1.57), while dual heads go from -0.40 / -0.14 (BOS-context, 1.3B) to -5.79 at 2.7B on newline. Read against run 754 (three tasks yielding three topologies — MLP-dominant, localised, skip-like — from the same method) and run 752 (a dose axis showing a direction's contribution is affine in the upstream dose), the recurrence is that **which sub-behaviour a unit serves is recoverable from the target axis of the intervention, and is usually more informative than the magnitude on the target the method was designed for.** Mechanical rule for us: **we score every intervention against at least two distinct target quantities drawn from the mechanism's own claims, and we report the sign per target before the size — because a set that hurts one target and helps another is not "less important", it is a different role, and averaging over targets is what hides it (the paper's own L0-broadening reading was exactly such a task-averaging artefact).**
- **Pattern:** **The representational evidence in this entry is circular by construction and the author concedes it: dual heads are *defined* as units active on exactly two classes, so a BOS-newline cosine of 0.89 against 0.54 for single-class units is expected from the selection rule rather than evidence about function.** What survives is only that the alignment is not a size artefact (200 size-matched subsamples reproduce 0.900 and 0.888) and that a logistic probe saturates on both head sets (0.996-0.999), i.e. the probe is too easy to discriminate — so neither similarity nor decodability separates the layers, and only ablation does. Read against run 753 (a proof that any fixed-function-of-clean-statistics score must tie a dormant backup to an inert unit) and run 751 (a screen returning the correct-looking pattern with the wrong causal sign), the recurrence is that **a metric computed over a set that was chosen by a monotone function of that metric is a restatement.** Mechanical rule for us: **when a unit set is defined by a criterion, we flag any downstream metric that is a monotone function of the criterion as a restatement and exclude it from the evidence list, keeping at least one metric computed under a different definition of the set - because the whole failure mode is that the definition and the measurement share an axis, and only a change of axis can break it.**

Figures 2-10 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**.
- **Pattern:** **The comparison across unit classes here is honest in the way almost nothing else in this folder is: the method that wins on node sparsity prints its own loss on edge compression in the same appendix, with the number attached (93.73 / 94.69 / 99.21 percent against Edge Pruning's 94.77 / 98.88 / 98.75), and then shows the repair and prices it (about 3.7 and 3.6 further percentage points from post-hoc edge pruning, at +1.34 GB on IOI).** The deeper point is that the win is partly a statement about what the baselines *cannot express*: an edge mask over heads-and-blocks has no vocabulary for "this MLP block is gone", so 50-75% block-level MLP pruning was never on their table, and the paper is careful to report which of its gains are expressiveness and which are optimisation quality. Read against run 753 (a score family provably unable to separate dormant from absent) and run 744 (a rank-capped surrogate whose accuracy cost the headline omitted), the distinction is that those entries characterise a *method's* blind spot theoretically, while this one pays for its own blind spot in currency and repairs it in the appendix. Mechanical rule for us: **every claim that our reading is more compact / smaller / sparser than a baseline states which axes were unavailable to the baseline, and where a repair step exists we run it and print both the gain and its cost in the same table - because "we prune 50% of MLP blocks and they prune none" is partly a grammar difference, and the honest form of the number names the grammar.**
- **Pattern:** **The per-layer summaries are the single most reusable artefact in the entry and they say something the head-level literature cannot: the same three tasks yield three different *topologies*, and the topology is stable across model size — IOI is MLP-dominant with attention pruned in many layers (GPT-2 small keeps MLPs in 8 of 12 layers, Llama-3.1-8B the same shape across 32 layers), GP is localised and discontinuous (GPT-2 small keeps MLP computation in exactly layers 0, 3 and 7; Llama-3.2-1B in the first three layers), and GT is skip-like (a contiguous dead band in layers 2-5 of GPT-2 small, all attention in four layers of GPT-2-XL, each holding all 25 heads).** The GT case is the sharpest: the circuit is not "small" in a diffuse way but concentrated at a handful of depths with a hole in between, which is a prediction about *which* layers a white-box model must read for a numerical-comparison task and which it can skip. Read against run 751 (cross-architecture divergence in composed tasks, dense versus MoE) and run 748 (a MoE whose expert categories simply are not routed), the recurrence is that **where computation lives is a per-task fact first and a per-architecture fact second.** Mechanical rule for us: **for each behaviour we model we publish a per-layer occupancy profile — which depths carry the effect, which depths are dead — before any claim about which components implement it, and we compare the profile across at least two model sizes of the same family - because a component-level story built on one checkpoint cannot distinguish a mechanism from a layer-index accident, and a dead band is as informative as a live block.**
- **Pattern:** **The method's own justification for not ablating to zero is the folder's clearest statement of the compensation objection, and it is worth taking as a standing warning: zero and mean ablation shift the input distribution, so a pruning procedure optimising under them can learn a *new* solution (the authors cite lottery-ticket results) rather than isolate the mechanism, which is why every mask here interpolates toward an actively corrupted stream that flips the answer instead.** That is the right instinct, and it is also precisely the definition that makes the neuron-level claim narrower than it reads: a unit survives if swapping it to its corrupted value breaks the clean-versus-corrupted margin, so "many neurons coarse methods keep are irrelevant" is true *under adversarial interpolation* and says nothing about a neuron that matters under dose (run 752) or that is a dormant backup (run 753). The Llama fidelity row set confirms the tension from the other side: pruning *more* at 8B than at 1B (75% of blocks and 95.7% of heads on GP) at accuracy 0.99 and KL 0.23 is consistent with redundancy growing with scale and equally consistent with the corrupted stream doing more of the work. Mechanical rule for us: **we name the ablation value in every circuit claim and we do not transfer a sufficiency result from one ablation value to another - a unit shown causally necessary under corruption-flip interpolation is re-tested under dose or mean before we call it part of the mechanism, because the intervention defines the estimand and a different intervention asks a different question.**

**Appendix D** and **E.1-E.6** read as prose and headings with Table 26/27/28 values cited in the text, **Appendix F.1 Limitations in full** (every named limitation), F.2-F.4 as headings; figures 1-16 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**.
- **Pattern:** **This is the first entry in the folder with a formal impossibility statement about a *class* of scores rather than about a particular method, and the statement is what makes the benchmark design non-arbitrary: any score that is a fixed function of a unit's clean-state per-unit statistics must assign a dormant backup and an inert unit the same value, because both statistics vanish for a unit that does nothing on the intact model.** The construction that escapes it is minimal - condition on the already-removed set and read the *growth* in effect - and the component ablation prices each ingredient: conditioning alone takes 0.33 to 0.80, centring against the output distribution takes it to 0.91, Fisher weighting adds 0.01. Read against run 751 (a screen that returns the correct-looking pattern with the opposite sign of causal effect) and run 748 (a suppression table whose null read exactly 0.000 in two categories), the distinction is that those entries diagnose *specific* screens and *specific* nulls, while this one proves no screen of a whole family can work and then exhibits the one axis that leaves the family. Mechanical rule for us: **before we publish any unit-importance score, we check whether it is a fixed function of intact-model per-unit statistics - if it is, we state that it cannot separate a component that is dormant from one that is absent, and we report the conditional (post-removal) counterpart alongside it - because "this unit does nothing on the clean model" is evidence for two mutually exclusive stories and no clean-pass number can tell them apart.**
- **Pattern:** **The strongest control in the entry is not the random one, and the difference between them is the whole result: adding the model's own next-ranked components (`+own`) ties CoAx on faithfulness (3.09 vs 3.11 for AtP, 1.75 vs 1.71 for EAP-IG) while recruiting heads at a mean first-order percentile of 0.69 versus 0.97 - and only the knockout test separates them, where `+own` overshoots to 0.24 into the core circuit while CoAx lands at 0.70 against a documented oracle of 0.72.** The paper says this outright rather than hiding the tie, and it applies the same discipline in the other direction on induction, where `+CoAx` and `+own` are genuinely comparable on four of eight models and the authors therefore claim only label-free recovery, not unique identification. Read against run 750 (a proposal stage and a verdict stage with separate nulls) and run 749 (a no-hit audit bounded by its sampling law rather than by the absence), the recurrence is that **a random control is a floor, and a floor is cleared by any structured ranking, including the trivially structured "more of the same".** Mechanical rule for us: **every selection or ranking method we report is benchmarked against a matched-size `+own` control drawn from the same detector's own ranking, not only against random, and where the two tie we say so and name the downstream test that separates them - because "beats random" answers whether the heads are arbitrary and "beats more of the same" answers whether they are the right heads, and only the second is the claim we actually want to make.**
- **Pattern:** **The score that wins here is deliberately the one that does *not* look at the task direction, and the measurements that justify it are the most useful numbers in the entry for us: the eight real IOI backups place a mean energy fraction of 0.002 on the task-answer direction - at or below a random-direction reference of 0.005, and with every head under 0.01 - yet patching their dormant activations removes 55% of the self-repair.** The paper resolves the apparent paradox without hand-waving (the causal weight is real, but it lives in a sliver of the distribution the ablation moves; the primaries themselves place only about 0.002 on that same ray) and then shows the consequence in a sweep: an answer-aligned conditional proxy scores 0.95 where backups write the answer directly and 0.59 where they compensate off-answer, while the full-distribution score holds at 0.90 across the whole sweep. Read against run 745 (a signed net whose zero is set by a gauge choice, and a magnitude summary that survives it) and run 741 (attribution as a pair of estimands), the distinction is that those entries fix the *gauge* of a score, while this one shows the *projection* is the gauge: change the direction you project onto and a component that carries half the repair drops below a random reference. Mechanical rule for us: **when we score a component by its projection onto a direction we chose (an answer direction, a probe direction, a task logit), we report the score's share of the component's total effect-energy on that ray, and if that share is at or below the random-direction reference - 1/|support| - we say the direction is not reading the component rather than treating the small number as evidence of unimportance.**

- **Done:** arxiv-2610.02173 - Every Ablation Is a Dose: Counterweights and the Semblance of Self-Repair (read full v1 HTML - the plain strip was 232,793 chars, then the raw 2.1 MB HTML was re-read and its **2,930** `<math alttext>` annotations substituted back in with `/tmp/lit/math02173.py` into 222,965 chars / 3,877 lines with 4,363 rebuilt table cells, because the strip left every formula and every table cell empty as in runs 744-751; read abstract, the full table of contents, §§1-3 (the dose axis, the nested counterfactual, the estimand), §4 with **Table 1 read cell by cell**, §5 (affine law, holdout, 68/81), §6 (wiring statistic), §7 (deletion on the dose axis), §8 Related Work, §9 Conclusion with the complete Findings, Limitations and Reflection paragraphs, §10 Ethics; Appendix A (claim-to-evidence map, notation), B (weight-space bases, Table 4), C.1-C.3 (template, Known-Facts pairs, **Table 5 sample sizes read cell by cell**), D.2 (**Tables 6-9 read cell by cell** - the four family catalogues), E.1-E.2, F.1 (**Table 10 read cell by cell**), the Table 11-13 captions, **G.1-G.3 in full with Tables 14, 15 and 16 read cell by cell**, H, **I.1-I.7 in full with Tables 17, 18 and 19 read cell by cell** (the renormalisation refutation), **J.1-J.5 with Tables 20-24 read cell by cell**, **K.1-K.5 with Tables 25-27 read cell by cell**, **L.1-L.6 in full with Table 31 read cell by cell** (the GPT-2 IOI arm), **M.1-M.3 with Tables 32-35 read cell by cell** (per-model wiring correlations, placement control), N (derivations) and O/P as headings only; Figures 1-31 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; reference list read only for works cited in §§1-8).
- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry that treats an intervention as an instrument with a calibration curve, and the calibration is the finding: a single deletion is one unmarked point on an axis whose scale the data, not the experimenter, chooses, so "the model repaired itself" and "the downstream unit always opposed the upstream one" are indistinguishable without the axis.** The construction is the one to steal - the arm is a clamp to a signed dose in units of the pair's own class contrast (+1 clean, 0 class-neutral midpoint, -1 full swap), the measurement is a second patch applied *inside* the dosed world so the primary's own shift cancels, and the estimand is the margin erased by the downstream override. On that axis 68 of 81 reachable directions are affine with a stable slope, 52 counterweights to 16 relays, and the crossing dose at which each flips sign is a fitted number rather than a story. Read against run 751 (a screen returning the correct-looking pattern with the *opposite* sign of causal effect) and run 750 (a proposal stage passing its null while the verdict inverted), the distinction is that those entries had a sign but no scale, while this one has a scale and reads the sign off it: run 751's interferers and this paper's counterweights are the same phenomenon seen through different instruments, and only the dose-resolved one says *how much* upstream signal reaches the verdict through that unit. Mechanical rule for us: **every intervention we publish is parameterised by a dose with a named unit (the model's own item contrast, not a global constant), the reported effect is a curve rather than a point, and every ablation-style claim carries the crossing dose at which the unit's sign flips - because a unit that opposes at rest and a unit that switches on under damage produce the identical single-point reading, and the curve is the only thing that separates them.**
- **Pattern:** **The competing explanation here is a pass-through multiplier, and the reason it loses is the dose *profile* rather than the magnitude - which gives us the sharpest null design in the folder so far.** The authors name final-normalisation renormalisation as the rival account, derive what it strictly predicts (a positive scalar cannot change a sign, so a dismissed direction can never become a two-sided contributor; amplification on an opposed direction predicts the *wrong* sign of recruitment; the gain-attributable share is bounded by |1-g|*|E0|), and then measure the gain per family, per class, per dose. The refutation is three-layered and each layer alone would be weak: the gain is dose-flat exactly where it is largest (Gemma's fake side, 1.080 to 1.103 across the sweep, while the unit's effect rises 4.5x), it moves the wrong way on true statements in both joint-core families (down to 0.926 and 0.890), it has the wrong predicted sign in half the audited cells, and where the sign is right it accounts for at most 4.5% of the observed recruitment (0.6% at the on-manifold dose). The paper also states where the null *would* win: deletion-type interventions sit in the shrinking regime where the mediator is real, so the audit explicitly does not transfer to zero/mean/resample ablation and a frozen-denominator control is retained for any such variant. Read against run 748 (a suppression table whose null read exactly 0.000 in two of four categories) and run 738 (a norm gap manufactured by a term in the loss), the recurrence is that **a confound is usually a multiplicative pass-through, and a multiplicative pass-through can counterfeit a small effect but cannot counterfeit a graded one.** Mechanical rule for us: **for every effect we report, we name the multiplicative mediators between the write and the readout (normalisation gain, soft-capping, temperature), derive the predictor's testable *signature* - not just its direction - and test the signature across the sweep; a null that is right in sign but flat where the effect is graded, or bounded at a few percent of it, is excluded and the exclusion is printed - because "the confound is small" is a weaker and harder claim than "the confound's signature is absent".**
- **Pattern:** **The weights-only predictor is reported with its own ceiling stated in the same breath, and the ceiling is the part we need: the wiring alignment ranks coupling magnitude across units (rho 0.55-0.82 per model, Fisher p about 1e-10 over four independently trained networks) and is simultaneously ordinal, unsigned, direct-pathway-only, and entangled with the clean effect.** The entanglement is argued, not glossed: because the clean effect equals intercept plus slope at the on-manifold dose, any statistic tracking one tracks the other, so the authors explicitly decline to claim the weights encode the slope in isolation and claim only that the checkpoint anticipates the *magnitude* of causal coupling. The placement control is what keeps the correlation honest - directions at or above the core's layer cannot read its write, two of them are among the most strongly wired units in the study (z of +14.1 and +10.1) and neither responds at all, and the honest reachable-versus-unreachable separation is eightfold rather than the orders of magnitude the raw medians suggest. Read against run 744 (a CKA proxy validated as a correlate and then used as a ranking, pointing at the wrong block on the strongest model) and run 749 (a coverage number reported without its sampling law), the distinction is that run 744 lets the proxy become a decision rule and this one keeps a measured decision in front of it: the sign, which the statistic cannot see, is always established by intervention. Mechanical rule for us: **a structural predictor we propose for our white-box model is published with (a) a rank correlation per model and combined, (b) an explicit statement of what it cannot see - sign, magnitude, indirect pathways, units outside causal reach - and (c) a placement control drawn from structure we did not choose, scored on its own separation rather than the one the raw medians suggest - because a statistic that predicts magnitude from the checkpoint is a real result and a statistic silently promoted to a sign predictor is a fabricated one, and the two differ only in the list of exclusions printed beside it.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's cleanest demonstration that a component can carry the right internal pattern and still be a correlate, an interferer, or a null - and the three cases are separated only by a signed per-component ablation, never by the pattern itself.** Pythia 1B's name-mover heads show the canonical IOI pattern at high selectivity and *help* the task when ablated (+7pp, and the whole late-layer set +8pp), because by the layers where attention-to-IO is strong the answer is already encoded upstream by prev-token computation; the same model's variable-binding screen returns five heads whose group ablation raises top-1 by **+37.4pp**, four of them first-token attention sinks whose removal deletes a competing signal, and individual ablations make all five interferers uniformly (+13 to +23pp each). Read against run 750 (a proposal stage that cleared its null and a verdict stage that inverted) and run 744 (a proxy validated as a correlate and then used as a decision rule), the distinction is that those entries separate *finding* from *judging* across two instruments, while this one shows the same instrument returning a correct-looking pattern with the opposite sign of effect - and that pattern-matching has no access to the sign. Mechanical rule for us: **no component of our white-box model is reported as load-bearing on the strength of what it looks like; every claimed component carries a signed per-component ablation against a matched-random null, and a positive (helpful-when-removed) result is classified as an interferer and diagnosed rather than dropped - because a screen selects on attention pattern, and attention pattern is exactly the quantity that a downstream readout can exhibit without doing any of the work.**
- **Pattern:** **The method's own null is the variable that moves the result by an order of magnitude, and the paper prints it: the same successor screen is 80.5pp on OLMo 1B against a matched-random of 57.5 +/- 18.1pp in the same layer, so the honest specificity is about 1.4x rather than the 2x the single seed suggested, while the greater-than screens spread across five layers meet a null of 0.0 +/- 0.0pp and are effectively unbounded.** Pythia's successor control ranges over 74 percentage points ([-73, +1]), and the paper's conclusion is not to drop those cells but to prefer the non-L0-concentrated prev-token ablation as the reliable causal claim in them - five random heads in layer zero are destructive regardless of which five. Read against run 748 (a suppression table whose random-null column read exactly 0.000 for two categories) and run 749 (a null whose sampling law, not its absence, set the bound), the recurrence is that **a specificity ratio is a ratio of two measurements and the denominator can be as large as the numerator.** Mechanical rule for us: **every "X beats random" claim we publish carries the control's mean, its SD and its range across seeds, and where the control's own effect is of the same order as the treated effect we say the ratio is uninformative rather than reporting it - because a screen that concentrates its picks in the layer where all input processing happens has not been tested, it has been compared against a control that is doing the same damage.**
- **Pattern:** **Cross-architecture comparison here produces a structural claim the within-model literature cannot: the MoE model routes three of four tasks through a positional substrate that does nothing in either dense model, and the paper prices that in the currency we care about - the substrate ablation costs more than the task-specific screen on greater-than (6pp vs 5pp), successor (57.6pp vs 1.7pp) and variable binding (37.6pp vs 9.2pp), while costing 0pp in Pythia and 0.2pp in OLMo on greater-than.** The design that licenses the comparison is that OLMo and OLMoE share corpus and architecture family and differ only in the expert layer, and the author states the four predictions that would falsify it, including the architecture-versus-data dissociation. Read against run 750 (a closure test that passed on two dense 1B models and failed on OLMoE where the community ablation *improved* loss) and run 748 (a suppression table where two of four categories were null because routing never selected the expert), the recurrence is that **the MoE models in this folder consistently confound a structural reading with a routing-conditional one, and the two dense-versus-MoE pairs land on opposite sides.** Mechanical rule for us: **any claim we make about where computation lives is checked in at least one dense and one sparse model sharing data and architecture, and the difference is attributed to routing explicitly - because "the circuit for this task is X" is a per-model empirical fact in these panels, and the only cross-model regularity found so far is that sparse models build on a positional substrate the dense ones do not use.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's cleanest case where the *proposal* and the *verdict* are separate stages with independent evidence, and the proposal's own statistical null can pass while the verdict fails in the wrong direction - which makes the two stages non-substitutable in a way no accuracy number can express.** The MoE story is staged deliberately: the marginal Ising collapses on natural text (ARI 0.006), route-conditional stratification restores a signal that beats a ten-partition null (+3.05 sigma, 0/10 partitions reached it), and only then does closure fire - and it fires negatively, with the 22-head community *improving* loss on its own stratum (z -5.64) and more outside it (z -12.94), and target logit and accuracy flipping favourable off-stratum as well. Read against run 749 (a witness that is always checkable versus a universal that is co-NP-hard) and run 748 (a suppression table where the nulls sat in the same table as the wins), the distinction is that those entries separate checking from certifying and pass from fail, while this one separates *finding candidates* from *judging them* and shows a candidate can clear the finding stage's null and invert at the judging stage. Mechanical rule for us: **every structural claim we publish is split into a proposal stage with its own null and a verdict stage with its own null, the two nulls are different instruments, and the verdict may not be inferred from the proposal - because a signal that is real, statistically specific and above every reasonable null can still point the wrong way, and only a signed test against matched controls that is run *after* discovery can say so.**
- **Pattern:** **The metric divergence inside one ablation is presented as an architectural measurement rather than as noise, and the number makes the case: the same 9-head community shows delta-loss +0.050 at z +1.95 while the target-token logit falls 3.00 units at z -52.5 - a 25x spread between two z-scores from one intervention.** The paper's reading is downstream redundancy (the circuit is real and specific; later layers partially reconstruct its contribution, so the aggregate is insulated while the targeted quantity collapses), and it quantifies the coupling difference across models at roughly 18x (|z-target|/|loss| about 3.4 in OLMo, about 60 in Pythia). The reverse failure also appears: OLMo 1B's +36.4 sigma on loss rests on a control SD 16x tighter than another test's (0.043 vs 0.69), so the largest-looking z in the study is smaller in absolute nnats than the modest one. Read against run 747 (n_solved/n_total next to every mean) and run 743 (a count of cells beating a control with no magnitude), the recurrence is that **an aggregate and a targeted quantity measure different things and neither is the effect.** Mechanical rule for us: **we report the per-example target quantity and the aggregate side by side for every intervention, print each z-score next to the absolute delta and its control mean +/- SD, and treat a large between-metric gap as a hypothesis about *where the redundancy lives* rather than as a better or worse result - because a z-score's denominator is a property of the controls, and a delta's size is a property of the model.**
- **Pattern:** **Two cheap proxies each fail in both directions across training, which converts "this proxy is a less sensitive measure of function" into "these are different constructs" - and that distinction is only available because the paper measured across checkpoints rather than at the end state.** BOS function is load-bearing while selectivity is still 0.6 (OLMo 1B, 2B tokens, accuracy z -6.1), and previous-token heads at Pythia step 512 show selectivity 74 and participation ratio 32 yet ablation helps loss (z -5.4); participation ratio alone is high and null at Pythia step 256. Read against run 744 (a structural quantity flat at baseline until about 2.1B tokens, so architecture and learned computation were separable only by trajectory) and run 742 (a bulk-patching diagonal), the recurrence is that **a proxy validated as a correlate at one checkpoint is then read as a detector at another.** Mechanical rule for us: **when we propose a cheap structural proxy for function, we test it at no fewer than three checkpoints and require both orderings - proxy before function and function before proxy - and only if neither occurs may we call it the same quantity at lower sensitivity, because one ordering is a stale threshold and both orderings are two different objects.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry where the quantity being certified is not a decomposition at all but a *quantifier order*, and the cheap/hard boundary is anatomical rather than gradual: checking a supplied leaking execution is always polynomial, while universal non-leakage over the declared prompt domain is co-NP-complete and, at a threshold, co-NP^PP-complete at every fixed rational cutoff.** The paper makes the asymmetry independent of the model's size or expressiveness - it holds for a depth-two threshold circuit - so no amount of architectural reading changes which side of the quantifier you are on. Read against run 747 (a certificate whose *coverage* - 13 of 61 instances certified - was the real result, and a complexity class put on the wrong structural assumption) and run 746 (a pre-fit screen that predicts recoverability from the inputs), the distinction is that those entries locate hardness or tractability in the *data* or in the *architecture*, while this one locates it in the *statement being made*: same model, same judge, same prompts, and the answer's cost is set by whether the claim begins "there exists" or "for all". Mechanical rule for us: **we state every white-box claim we publish with its quantifier explicit - a witness, an average, or a universal over a named domain - and we do not let evidence for the first be read as evidence for the third, because the same observation set that refutes a universal establishes nothing about it when it comes back empty.**
- **Pattern:** **The paper's two execution modes are each perfectly reproducible and still disagree on a target decision, which is the folder's cleanest demonstration that reproducibility and validity are separate measurements.** Both maps are complete enumerations of the same 4,096-prompt domain under greedy decoding, the positive totals differ by only four (2,489 vs 2,485), a same-input same-order repeat reproduces every output digest and every label for all 64 targets - and yet 342 of 196,608 labels differ between modes (12.87% of all pairs positive in either map), 173 witnesses exist in one map only and 169 in the other, and one of the 48 adapted targets flips from UNSAFE to SAFE because its single batched witness is absent when the prompts are run one at a time. The base controls show the same thing without a label change: 22,159 of 65,536 outputs differ. Read against run 744 (a sign-consistent table hiding any magnitude; norm-matched control as the default) and run 746 (two seeds disagreeing while each looks confident), the recurrence is that **a second run under the same protocol is a reproducibility check and never a validity check.** Mechanical rule for us: **when we rerun an experiment to confirm it, we say explicitly what the rerun varies - seed, initialisation, batch schedule, execution path - and we report agreement per mode *and* across modes, because a same-protocol repeat that matches to the digest tells us the pipeline is deterministic and tells us nothing about whether the batching changed the model's behaviour, which is exactly what these maps found (batch size changed outputs on a third of the base-control prompts).**
- **Pattern:** **A no-hit audit is bounded above by the sampling law rather than by the absence, and the paper prints both bounds so the number cannot be read as a strength: 256 uniformly selected records with no leak still admits 46 of 4,096 at 95%, and a target with a single witness needs 3,892 of 4,096 records for 95% detection.** The sparsity is the real story - 13 of the 30 leaking targets had at most seven witnesses, and those 13 contribute 10.46 of the 12.32 expected uniform misses - so miss rates are set by the witness density of the *targets*, not by the budget alone. The paper also warns that the confidence bound is for the average risk under the sampling distribution and not for the worst prompt, which is the claim an auditor actually wants. Read against run 748 (a suppression table with targeted against matched-size random null, and rows that failed printed) and run 747 (a denominator of 61 reported next to every mean), the recurrence is that **the honest form of a null result names what it rules out and what it does not.** Mechanical rule for us: **every negative result we publish carries (records examined, total domain, sparsity of the positives we would have detected, the resulting detection probability for the rarest class we care about) - and where the rarest class would need more than the whole budget, we say the audit cannot speak to it rather than reporting zero.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's cleanest case that a decomposition whose units are architectural is legitimate on a ground the learned decompositions were denied - the unit is monosemantic by construction, and the cost of that construction is scope, not fidelity.** Rank-1 experts read one direction and write another, so each expert's weight vector can be pushed through the unembedding for a name with no reconstruction error to report at all; the paper states the trade explicitly (experts are tied to the MoE architecture, sparse autoencoders apply to anything) and reports its own received numbers for the alternative (SAE features shared across runs at 30-40%, absorption, predictable reconstruction error). Read against run 745 (a decomposition whose own authors list three non-identifiability mechanisms and say the factors are one admissible representation) and run 744 (a rank-cap that buys tractability at an unprinted accuracy cost), the distinction is that those entries pay for their decomposition in fidelity or accuracy, while this one pays in generality - and it pays nothing in fidelity because the architecture did the factorization during training. Mechanical rule for us: **when we read a unit that is rank-1 or otherwise structurally factorised by the architecture, we say so in the entry header and we do not re-derive its identifiability from data; when we read units we fitted ourselves, we carry run 745's two-seed alignment requirement - because the same word "monosemantic" is a theorem about one and a hypothesis about the other, and only the provenance tells them apart.**
- **Pattern:** **The suppression table is the folder's best-designed null and also its own counter-evidence, and both facts come from the same four rows: targeted effect, matched-size random effect and KL for each, with the failures printed.** Temporal -33.7% at KL 0.139 against exactly 0.000 for the random null, geographic -23.2% at KL 0.512 against 0.000, and then cardinal and discourse at -0.0% and -0.4% with KL 0.000 in *both* columns. The authors explain the zeros by content-addressed routing - the expert is rarely top-4 for those prompts - which is plausible and is also an admission that the causal claim holds only where the router cooperates; 2 of 4 categories therefore fail the test as run, and the paper's headline is carried by the 2 that pass plus the broad steering sweep. Read against run 747 (a denominator that showed a strong predicate certifying on 13 of 61) and run 743 (a count of cells beating a control with no magnitude attached): the recurrence is that **the cells that did not work are the ones that tell you what the intervention is conditional on.** Mechanical rule for us: **every intervention table we publish keeps the null rows in the same table as the wins, with the null's own value rather than a dash, and a zero-effect row is diagnosed in the text (was the unit inactive? was it not routed? was the quantity absent?) rather than dropped - because a table of only-passing rows is indistinguishable from a table produced by a model where every unit were special, and the conditional that explains the failures is usually the real scope of the claim.**
- **Pattern:** **The composition result is a mechanism rather than a mystery, and the mechanism is routing geometry: same-layer steering pairs interfere because centroid addition perturbs every similarity in that layer, while cross-layer pairs compose almost additively - so the interference is a property of the *operator* and not of the experts' representations.** The pairs make it concrete: same-layer solo +567% and +44% collapse to +41% and +35%, while L4-plus-L7 gives +513% and +18%, with measured cross-talk under 10% in both cases. Read against run 743 (a cross-layer remainder the same-block sum does not predict, measured on a real pretrained model) and run 744 (a cross-layer coordination component that only appears after ~2.1B tokens), the distinction is that those entries find cross-layer structure that a within-layer accounting misses, while this one finds *within-layer* interference that a third-party operator introduces and a cross-layer additivity that no one imposed. Mechanical rule for us: **before we interpret a multi-component interference effect as representational overlap, we check whether the intervention operator itself is shared across the components, and we state which one we perturbed - the component, the gate that selects it, or the space both are read in - because "these two features conflict" and "this steering operator cannot address two centroids in one layer" are different findings and only the operator's shape distinguishes them.**

Provable Explanations for Any-Order Neural Additive Models (read full v1 HTML: 116,587 chars / 2,556 lines, abstract through §9 including Definition 1 (any-order NAM), Definitions 2-3 (sufficiency, cardinal vs subset minimality), §4 Theorem 4 with the MSE-k-NAM decision problem, §5 Definition 5, Proposition 6, the two-component worked example, Proposition 7, the MILP of Eq. 2, Proposition 8, Algorithm 1 with its bisection loop, Theorem 9 and the complexity paragraph, §6 Lemma 10, Definition 11 (-sparse), Theorem 12, §7.1-§7.3 with **Tables 1 and 2 read cell by cell** (nine datasets, three methods, all size/time cells) and the order-2/3/4 Bike Sharing sweep, §8 Related Work, §9 Conclusion, **Appendix A Limitations in full**, Appendix G.1-G.6 including the dataset split/feature table read cell by cell, and **Appendix H.1-H.8 in full** with **Tables 3-8 read cell by cell** (unrestricted domain, first-vs-second-order, gap-vs-margin correlations, the order-4 wall at 1,096/3,450, VeriX+/brute-force/MHS baselines, SIAN-discovered scopes, and the Heart Disease case study); Appendices B-F read only as headings and proof statements; Figures 1-15 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**. This HTML build rendered the prose cleanly but **stripped every inline formula** (Theorem 4's constant, the MILP constraints, the complexity classes appear only as gaps), so no formula is quoted anywhere in the entry and the hardness result is described in words; no table cell was lost.
- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry where the *interaction structure of the model itself* is the variable that flips explainability between cheap and intractable — and the flip is total, not gradual: a first-order additive model admits cardinally-minimal sufficient explanations in a linear number of verification queries, and adding scopes of bounded constant size restores the hardness of a fully general network.** The authors then recover tractability twice, once by strengthening the notion of explanation (letting each component choose its own completion, which decomposes the worst-case margin loss into a sum) and once by exploiting sparsity (merging overlapping scopes into disjoint components so the same solver returns genuine cardinal minima for the original model). Read against run 746 (a pre-fit certificate that predicts *after* the fact whether a decomposition is recoverable) and run 745 (identifiability of a fitted decomposition across seeds), the distinction is that those entries ask whether the *learned* components are real, while this one asks whether the model's structural assumptions make a *certified* statement about it computable at all — and puts the cost of the wrong assumption into a complexity class rather than a seed spread. Mechanical rule for us: **before we promise that a white-box reading of our network is exact or minimal, we name the structural assumption that licenses it (additivity, bounded scope, sparse interaction graph), state whether our model satisfies it, and say what the guarantee degrades to when it does not — because "we can compute the minimal explanation" is a theorem about the architecture, and the architecture is the thing we chose.**
- **Pattern:** **The measure to steal is "solved inside budget", reported as a numerator and denominator next to every mean — because two of the paper's own tables show that a mean computed only over converged instances is a statement about which instances converged.** At interaction order 4 the mean size and mean time are computed on the 1,096 of 3,450 examples (31.8%) that finished inside an hour, and the authors flag the subset as probably biased toward easier inputs; under an unrestricted admissible domain the sensitivity-guided baseline drops to 13 of 61 solved while its reported mean size stays competitive with 51 of 61, so the mean would read as a fair loss and the denominator as a rout. The exact-baseline appendix applies the same discipline in the other direction: brute force and minimum-hitting-set are reported only where they are affordable (8 features), which is what makes the claim "close to the true optimum where an optimum exists" a checkable statement rather than an assertion. Read against run 739 (a Spearman that dropped to 0.70 on the ViT, where the aggregate hid a regime change) and run 743 (a record of how many cells beat a control without the magnitude): the recurrence is that **the aggregate is always honest about the instances that entered it and never about the ones that did not.** Mechanical rule for us: **every aggregate number we publish carries `n_solved/n_total` in the same cell, and where instances were dropped for timeout or convergence we state the drop rate and say which direction the surviving subset is biased — because "mean error over the runs that completed" and "mean error over the runs we attempted" are different measurements, and only the denominator distinguishes them.**
- **Pattern:** **Tightening the definition to buy tractability is legitimate here only because the paper refuses to sell the tightened thing as the thing — strong sufficiency is a strictly stronger property, so every strongly-sufficient explanation is genuinely sufficient, but it carries no size guarantee against a plain subset-minimal baseline, and the size table shows that: the method wins on a minority of the strong-sufficiency benchmarks and loses on Breast Cancer and California Housing.** The paper states this in the same paragraph that introduces the relaxation, and then introduces a *separate* setting (sparsity) where the guarantee is for the original model and where it wins on all nine. Read against run 744 (a rank-capped surrogate whose accuracy cost against the uncapped version is the price the headline omits) and run 742 (a decomposition made exact by construction so the sum and the joint effect coincide): those entries are about paying for exactness, this one is about **buying tractability with a stronger predicate and then keeping the two settings in separate tables.** Mechanical rule for us: **when we substitute a stricter, cheaper-to-verify predicate for the quantity we actually claim to measure, we print (a) the implication direction that makes it sound, (b) a head-to-head against a baseline on the original quantity, and (c) the cases where the substitute loses — because a conservative approximation can be certified and still be worse, and the certification is what makes it tempting to report only that.**

Identifiability, Recoverability, and a Pre-Fit Diagnostic (read full v1 HTML: 55,621 chars / 406 lines plain-stripped, plus the raw 304,950-char HTML re-read and its 436 `<math alttext>` annotations substituted back in with `/tmp/lit/math8390.py` into a 464-line / 63,575-char text file, because this build strips every formula and every table cell exactly as in run 744; read: abstract, §I Introduction with all four contributions, §II Definitions 1-2, §III Lemmas 3-4 and Assumptions 5-6, §IV Proposition 7 with proof, §V-A Assumption 8, Proposition 9 with proof and Remark 10, §V-B Assumptions 11-12 and Lemma 13 with proof, §V-C Assumptions 14/17/20, Propositions 15/18/21 with proofs, Definitions 16/19 and Remark 22, §V-D Lemma 23, Proposition 24 and Theorem 25 (all three claims) with proofs, Corollary 26 with proof, §VI on the participation-ratio effective rank including the explicit rejection of condition number and thresholded rank, §VI-A workflow and Figure 1, §VI-B failure modes F1-F4, §VII setup / recovery at scale / additive baseline / capacity-matched comparison with Table I read cell by cell, the rho-sweep and Figure 3, §VIII-A-VIII-D with Tables II, III and IV read cell by cell, §IX Related Work, §X Discussion, §XI Conclusion, and the references for every work cited; Figures 1-4 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**). **Note:** §VIII-A's *Setup B* paragraph and the Figure 4 caption are **truncated in the HTML build itself** (they break mid-sentence at "r_eff" and "Pairwise"), so the PM2.5-to-PM10 numbers are described as the paper's 6-of-8 win count only, and nothing is quoted from the missing tail.
- **Pattern:** **This is the first entry in the folder to give an *input-side* certificate of non-identifiability, and the certificate is cheap: participation-ratio effective rank of the joint lag-block covariance, computed from one eigendecomposition of the inputs before any training, predicts whether an interaction decomposition can be recovered at all.** The correlation sweep makes it a curve rather than a sign - effective rank falls from 3.00 to 1.54 as the two true modulators become collinear and modulator-set recovery collapses from noisy mid-range values to 0/10 seeds at the top, and the paper plots gate error against rank rather than against the correlation knob it swept, because rank is the quantity the theorem names. Read against run 745 (non-identifiability from non-rectangular support, named as a limitation after the fact) and run 744 (cost as the barrier to an exact decomposition), the distinction is that those entries discover non-identifiability *after* fitting or price it *in compute*, while this one predicts it from the inputs alone and locates the failure in the data rather than in the optimiser. Mechanical rule for us: **before we report any decomposition of the network's internals, we compute a pre-fit spread statistic on the inputs or activations it will be fitted on - effective rank or participation ratio over the relevant covariance - and print it next to the result, because a decomposition measured on collapsed support is a statement about our parameterisation, and the rank says so before we have spent the compute to find out.**
- **Pattern:** **The sharpest finding here inverts the folder's reading of magnitudes: under collapsed support the within-fit margins were *large* (1.9x to 5.8x) while the models disagreed with each other across seeds, and the paper calls this "confidently arbitrary" and argues it is a *stronger* signature of non-identifiability than small margins would be.** Its four pre-registered predictions for the volatility panel were stated before the fit; three held, and the one that failed - the prediction of small margins - failed in the dimension the theory does not require, which the authors say outright rather than reframing. On the development panel the failure is quieter and more instructive: effective rank is 4.47 of a possible 4, so the pre-fit screen says feasible, but the two seeds disagree on the top modulator, the gate slopes flip sign, and the model does not beat the additive baseline (1.26 vs 1.19) - the property that the screen cannot see by construction. Read against run 736 (a norm gap manufactured by a term in the loss) and run 737 (a coefficient sweep whose optimum the prose denied): those entries show a statistic being *produced* by a design choice, this one shows a statistic being *unreproducible* while looking strong. Mechanical rule for us: **a large effect size from a single fit is not evidence of anything until a second independent fit reproduces it, and we make the two-run comparison part of the report rather than a robustness footnote - because "confidently arbitrary" is the failure mode that survives every within-run check and dies only at the second seed.**
- **Pattern:** **The paper pushes the identifiability question up one level, from the components to the *order* of the interaction, and states the synthesis that makes it crisp: a three-way product restricted to a support where one input equals another is exactly a two-way function, so the order cannot be recovered from that data.** On full-dimensional product support a genuinely k-way function cannot be written as a sum of lower-order terms and the order is identified; under rank-collapsed support it can, and between the extremes strong correlation degrades order identifiability well before complete collapse. The paper also makes its own headroom explicit in a way that matters for us: a hierarchical functional-ANOVA decomposition gives "the order of a dependence" a representation-independent definition, but only under extra regularity assumptions that full-dimensional support does not imply - and the gated model's listed modulators need a separate faithfulness assumption to bridge the structure to the parameters. Read against run 740 (26% of the target preserved, so the experiment was a boundary case) and run 742 (a partition exact by construction, so sum-of-parts and joint effect coincide): the recurrence is that **every "this is what the component means" claim rests on a bridge assumption that the headline theorem does not carry.** Mechanical rule for us: **whenever we claim an interaction of a particular order, we state the support condition that makes the order identifiable and the bridge assumption that makes the fitted parameters mean that structure, and we say which of the two is doing the work - because "the decomposition is unique" and "the fitted parameters are that decomposition" are separate theorems and the first is routinely reported as if it were the second.**
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The Priority block being empty, run 745's carried candidate #1 was taken - a paper that **compares an exact subset-based interaction decomposition against a tractable approximation on the same trained model and reports the gap as a function of order**. - **Pattern:** **This is the folder's cleanest demonstration that a net signed number has an *arbitrary zero*, and it arrives with a construction rather than an argument: on a synthetic function whose interesting coefficient sits behind a third-order interaction with zero expectation, the population one-dimensional partial dependence for that feature is identically zero, so every model's standard chart is flat while the effect is strong.** Only the paper's magnitude summary - the shared positive backbone, which cannot cancel by construction - retains the quadratic shape. Worse for the signed reading, in the unconstrained rank-1 alternative each individual factor is only identifiable up to a joint sign flip, so a fit exists in which the features' own curves look negative while the product is positive because four signs cancel. Read against run 742 (the signed gross/net budget, where a net of 0.05 could be a mechanism or an absence and only the pair distinguished them) and run 741 (attribution as a pair of estimands), the distinction is that those entries say a net hides its components, while this one shows a marginal operation erases a component outright and shows a quantity that survives. Mechanical rule for us: **every component score we publish carries a magnitude summary that is non-negative by construction alongside the signed one, the two are reported together, and we state which gauge fixed the sign - because a signed number whose zero point is set by a gauge choice is not a measurement, and a signed net whose components cancel to zero reads identically to a component that is absent.**
- **Pattern:** **The paper's principal stated limitation is that its own model is not identifiable, and it lists three distinct mechanisms - scaling and permutation, non-rectangular support, and noisy fitting - then shows that positivity fixes only the first two partially and that the third causes whole bags to converge to different factorisations of the same function.** The consequence is mechanical: some fitted bags are discarded rather than averaged, so only a fraction of the ensemble contributes, across-seed variance is high, and the recommended fix (aligning all bags on the positive rank-1 manifold) is left as future work. The paper states plainly that the latent factors should not be read as uniquely recoverable objects but as one admissible representation chosen by the fitting procedure. Read against run 744 (a decomposition whose *cost* is exponential in order) and run 743 (a remainder that is only ever measured, never bounded), the recurrence is that **every exact-looking decomposition in this folder arrives with a caveat about which of its parts are real** - and this is the first entry to catalogue the caveats as separate mechanisms with different fixes. Mechanical rule for us: **before we report any decomposed component as a fact about the model, we run the decomposition at no fewer than two seeds or two initialisations, align them in a named gauge, and report the between-run disagreement in the component values themselves - because two runs that fit equally well and disagree on a factor have produced two admissible representations, and reporting either of them alone as the structure is a statement about our optimiser, not about the network.** Cross-reference run 740 (a decomposition that preserved only 26% of the target and was labelled a boundary case).
- **Pattern:** **The cost boundary carried out of run 744 is crossed here, but in the direction the folder had not considered: the escape from exponential subset enumeration is not a cheaper exact decomposition, it is a *different function class* with a hard rank cap - and the paper's own benchmark shows the cap costing accuracy.** Training cost is stated as linear in stages, bagged grids, split iterations, samples and features, with the bagged grids embarrassingly parallel and histogram binning cutting the per-iteration sample dependence; but every stage contributes at most two products, so the model's separation rank is bounded at twice the stage count, and across 27 datasets the family's unconstrained version wins more aggregate rows than the positivity-constrained one the paper advocates. The approximation rate carries its own caveat: it is dimension-free only for a target class whose required order of mixed differentiability grows with the dimension. Read against run 742 (completeness by construction, where the sum of parts *is* the joint effect) and run 744 (exponential subset cost, left open), the distinction is that those entries ask whether a decomposition is exact and how much it costs, this one shows a cheap decomposition is available only by restricting the hypothesis class. Mechanical rule for us: **when we trade an exponential exact decomposition for a tractable one, we publish the rank or order cap that makes it tractable, the accuracy lost against the uncapped version at matched budget, and a sentence naming which function class the guarantee is for - because "scalable decomposition" and "exact decomposition" are answers to different questions, and the cap is the price that is never in the headline.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the first entry in the folder where the "interaction term" of run 743 is computed EXACTLY and then used as a *developmental* statistic - and the developmental claim is the sharpest thing in it: the normalised cross-layer component is flat at its baseline (ratio ~1) until roughly 2.1 billion tokens of pretraining, and only then separates upward with bootstrap bands that no longer contain 1.** Across all seven models the ratio is above 1 at every order (weakest Pythia-1B order 2 at 1.62, strongest Pythia-1.4B 60.8 and Pythia-6.9B 64.4 at order 6), and the baseline itself halves per order (0.30 / 0.11 / 0.05 / 0.03 / 0.02 in four of the seven families) while the final checkpoint stops decaying. Read against run 743 (a cross-layer remainder measured pointwise on a frozen checkpoint and shown to be as large as the same-block sum) and run 739 (a spectral statistic whose onset leads behavioural degradation), the distinction is that 743 measures *how big* the cross-layer term is on one trained model while this one measures *when it appears* across a training trajectory. Mechanical rule for us: **any structural quantity our white-box model reports is measured at no fewer than three checkpoints of the same run and reported as a ratio to the earliest checkpoint, with the episode at which the ratio stops straddling 1 named - because a quantity that is already present at initialisation is a property of the architecture, and only the trajectory separates architecture from learned computation.** Cross-reference run 738 (head geometry vs feature geometry under one knob).
- **Pattern:** **The control here is the folder's best-designed intervention control and should become our default: a norm-matched random direction deleted at the same site, so the comparison isolates direction and nothing else.** Component deletion beats the random control in all 35 cells (Pythia-1.4B order 6: 224x vs 19.8x; Pythia-6.9B order 5: 1472x vs 162x; OLMo-1B order 2: 218x vs 18.6x), but Amber-7B's margins are 1.1x vs 1.04x at order 4 and 1.8x vs 1.20x at order 6, and Pythia-6.9B order 6 is 1253x vs 898x - a factor of 1.4 - in the same table where the effect is three orders of magnitude elsewhere. The paper reports the cell count as the result and does not comment on the size spread. Read against run 736 (a seen/unseen split that carried the whole claim) and run 733 (a headline 0.0117 against a 0.0085 seed spread): the recurrence is that **a sign-consistent table can hide an effect of any magnitude, and the count of consistent cells is not the effect size.** Mechanical rule for us: **every intervention experiment we run pairs a deletion with a norm-matched random direction at the same site, and we publish the ratio-of-ratios (component damage divided by control damage) next to each cell rather than only the count of cells where the sign holds - because "beats the control in 35 of 35" and "beats the control by 11x median and 1.4x in the worst cell" are different claims and the second is the one a reader can act on.**
- **Pattern:** **Representation similarity is negatively associated with joint computation in all seven models, yet it is not a substitute for it, and the paper's block-removal table shows the cheap proxy pointing at the wrong block in the strongest model.** Pairwise CKA correlates negatively with the pairwise connected component everywhere (Pythia-410M about -0.52 to -0.71 by order; Pythia-6.9B only -0.19 at order 2), and the CKA-based block ranking correlation vanishes or flips on Amber-7B (+0.18, +0.01) while the coordination-based one stays positive (+0.57, +0.49); concretely, CKA's "similar, therefore redundant" pick on Amber order 2 costs 55.7 perplexity against 26.9 for its "dissimilar" pick, while the low-coordination pick costs 23.8. In the Pythia family the two criteria agrees within 8% in 10 of 15 cells, so the failure is model-specific rather than general. Read against run 734 (eight collapse scalars used as covariates to pick a detector family - a proxy used as a selector) and run 728 (a near/mid/far stratification where the winner changes by regime): the recurrence is that **a proxy validated as a correlate is then used as a decision rule, and it is the decision rule that breaks.** Mechanical rule for us: **when we use a cheap structural proxy (similarity, norm, rank) to decide what to remove, patch or prune, we validate it by actually performing the removal and scoring the outcome, per model, and we report the cells where the proxy and the direct measure disagree - because a correlation with the true quantity does not license the ranking, and the ranking is what the proxy is for.**

- **Done:** arxiv-2608.03629 - Cross-Layer Interaction under Weight-Space Ablation: A Closed-Form Attention Jacobian Bound and a Test on a Real Pretrained Model (read full v2 HTML, 84,977 chars / 1,374 lines: abstract, §1-§9 including the complete §8 Discussion "What is established" / "What remains open" and §9, §2 Related Work in full, §3's Facts 1-5, Fact 3's normalization Jacobian and Fact 4's first-order interaction formula with Eq. 1-5, §4 Proposition 1 with its complete proof and Remarks 2-3, §5 Propositions 2 and 3 with proofs, Eq. 3-5, Remarks 4-6 including the numerical-check looseness ratios and the "no identifiable sign law" negative finding, §6 Lemma 1 and Proposition 4 with proofs, Eq. 6, Remark 7's 1,200-sample refutation test, §7's same-block-vs-cross-layer measurement and §7.1's Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct IOI section with Table 1 read cell by cell, Figures 1-2 read from captions and reporting prose (**not inspected as images**), Appendix A in full (task, architecture, carrier selection, ablation subspaces, probe seeds, code list); reference list read for all six cited works. The HTML build rendered the maths inline this time, so Eq. 4-6 and the Table 1 cells were readable without a PDF fallback.
- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first exact, complete accounting of a *multi-layer* interaction, and its verdict overturns the assumption the folder has been operating on since run 742: the same-block sum is not a proxy for the joint effect once components sit in different layers.** Pooled over 1,440 measurements the median same-block sum (0.31 logits) sits right next to the median total interaction (0.30), which reads as confirmation - but the median cross-layer remainder is 0.59, roughly twice either, and the same-block sum exceeds the total on 57% of pairs while the remainder exceeds it on 63%, so the two routinely cancel; one configuration shows a same-block sum of 3.85 logits against a total of 0.11. Read against run 742 (a decomposition made complete by construction, so sum-of-parts and joint effect coincide by theorem) and run 741 (the cross-interaction invisible to per-head measurement, group term dwarfing the pairwise), the distinction is the sharpest in the arc: those entries establish that *within* a partition the total is the sum, this one establishes that across layers the naive partition misses a term of the same order or larger. Mechanical rule for us: **every multi-component ablation we publish prints three columns - the sum of per-component effects, the measured joint effect, and the residual as its own number with its own magnitude - and we never report the sum as a prediction of the joint effect unless the residual has been measured to be small on our own model rather than assumed small by analogy to a single-block theorem.**
- **Pattern:** **Two rigorous, pointwise-verified, never-violated bounds are looser than the truth by 71x and by 10^5-10^6x, and the paper reports the ratio rather than the pass - which makes this the folder's cleanest case that a bound is a certificate of order, not an estimate.** The attention Jacobian bound's p-weighted form exceeds the finite-difference estimate by 12x to 111x (median 71x) on Qwen's real weights over 12 sites and 24 random unit perturbations each, with 0 violations; its weight-only normalization-closed form is finite and also never violated but looser by four to six orders of magnitude (median 1.7e5, max 4.2e6), and is described as "not a numerically useful estimate as stated". The curvature bound has 0 violations in 1,200 samples but exceeds the largest sampled curvature by 6.5e2 to 2.4e3, and the paper localises the slack: the normalization lemma alone is loose by only 12.9-15.7x, so the slack accumulates in the product-of-norms composition, not in the derivative. Read against run 731 (a per-sample bound repaired so unsupported inputs stay in the ranking) and run 725 (a bound whose failing constant was estimated rather than argued away): the recurrence is that **a bound's job and an estimate's job are different, and only one of them can carry a quantitative claim.** Mechanical rule for us: **every closed-form bound our white-box model asserts is published with (bound value, measured value at the observation point, ratio) side by side, and the ratio - not the absence of violations - decides whether it may be used to predict; where the ratio is above two orders of magnitude the bound is labelled a certificate of order and quoted no further.**
- **Pattern:** **The paper names a negative result with a structural reason rather than an empirical failure: the cross-layer remainder is a bilinear form, so no function of the two marginal magnitudes can predict its sign, and it records this as a closed question rather than an unexplored one.** Differentiating the interpolation twice at the origin shows the mixed derivative is a pairing between how much of the earlier layer's removed mass reaches the later layer's input and how sensitive the later layer's removed-mass map is there; neither the product nor the sum of the two single-site effects is such a pairing, so neither should be expected to give the sign "as a rule". The check is then explicit about its own worthlessness - the sign matches on two of three instances and fails on the third, and the paper says three points settle nothing and the check was free. Read against run 740 (a decomposition that preserved 26% of the target and was reported as a boundary case) and run 738 (an ablation that confirmed a mechanism and then showed the mechanism was not load-bearing): those entries report a measurement that failed to support a claim; this one reports a claim that was *shown unpromising before* the measurement and then measured anyway. Mechanical rule for us: **when a structural argument tells us a simple relation cannot hold, we write it into the notes as a negative result with the reason, and we do not spend a run searching for the relation empirically - and when we do run the check anyway because it is free, we print the sample size next to the result so a 2-of-3 match cannot be read as a pattern.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the answer to the compositionality carry, and the answer is an arithmetic guarantee rather than a learned correction: a contribution defined on full input-to-output path terms sums exactly to the output change at every granularity, so "sum of the parts" and "joint effect" are the same quantity by construction rather than by fit.** Hierarchical completeness holds for any family of subnetworks whose path sets partition a larger one, which makes neurons, edges, convolutional channels and arbitrary circuits all readable as marginals of a single path-level matrix, and recovers Integrated Gradients, neuron conductance and CODEC as special cases of one definition. Read against run 741 (which proves the cross-interaction is invisible to per-head measurement and that the group term dwarfs any pairwise term) and run 740 (a decomposition that preserved only 26% of the target signal), the distinction is that those two entries measure how badly a decomposition approximates a behavioural quantity while this one builds the decomposition so that it cannot. Mechanical rule for us: **we do not fit a correction to close the gap between a sum of component scores and the measured group effect; we define our scores on units whose partition is exact, verify the partition sums to the measured output difference to within the printed numerical error, and report that error - because a decomposition that is complete by construction makes the sum-of-parts-versus-joint question disappear, and one that is incomplete by 74% cannot be repaired by any downstream model.** Cross-reference run 733 (a theorem predicting an ablation it then measured).
- **Pattern:** **The signed gross/net budget makes cancellation measurable and shows it is the norm, not the exception - so a small net contribution is not evidence of a weak component, and the folder has been reading small numbers as small effects.** At the path level, gross excitatory and inhibitory sums are much larger than the residual that neuron-level methods report, the cancellation factor roughly triples from edges to paths, and the distribution coincides across three random seeds; the "silent" neuron that motivated the analysis turns out to be strongly driven in both directions on exactly the inputs where it looks inert. In the retina the gross drive exceeds net output by a median factor of 6.2 and inhibition supplies 45% of it, with the strongest inhibitory paths converging on the same channels as the strongest excitatory ones. Read against run 741 (two estimands whose difference is itself the measurement) and run 736 (a norm gap manufactured by a loss term): the recurrence is that **a quantity defined as a difference silently discards one of its two terms, and the discarded term is often as large as the retained one.** Mechanical rule for us: **every intervention result we publish carries (gross positive, gross negative, net) at the finest granularity we can afford, and a component whose net is near zero is described as cancelling rather than as unimportant until the gross budget says otherwise - because a net of 0.05 from +3.0 and -2.95 is a mechanism, and the same 0.05 from +0.05 and 0.00 is an absence, and only the pair distinguishes them.** Cross-reference run 739 (a ratio that saturates while its numerator grows threefold - normalisation deciding what a number means).
- **Pattern:** **Bulk patching lands on the diagonal against summed contributions while single-context patching does not sum correctly, and that asymmetry is the cleanest statement in the folder of why group interventions need group scores.** Averaged over 3,001 simulated contexts the path contribution tracks the patching effect closely, and the difference between them is much smaller than the spread across contexts, so the contribution behaves as an efficient summary of a distribution that is expensive to sample; but remove-one and keep-one patching each fail to sum to the output (the XOR toy model counts the product term twice under one convention and never under the other), and only complete contributions recover it. The paper also shows the search must be exact rather than heuristic: a beam search over the most important edges has to enumerate about 100 times more paths than requested to find the true top ones, and 100 times more again over neurons. Read against run 734 (a random shortlist of matched size matching a fitted predictor) and run 728 (a stratification where the winner changes by regime): those entries establish that selectors are only as good as their shortlist, this one establishes that a best-first search with a provable bound can be cheaper than the heuristic it replaces while returning the exact answer. Mechanical rule for us: **any group intervention we report is paired with the summed per-component score for the same group, the comparison is printed as a scatter against the diagonal rather than as a correlation, and where a cheap heuristic stands in for an exact search we report how much extra enumeration it costs to reach the exact answer - because a diagonal scatter says the score predicts the intervention and a correlation only says the two move together.**
