- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's sharpest statement that an attribution is a *pair* of estimands, not a number, and that the gap between them is the measurement - arriving with a closed-form reason rather than a correlation.** Denoising and noising are not two recipes for one quantity: the paper proves the first estimates the pure indirect effect (bypass edges blocked) while the second estimates that effect *plus* a mediated interaction equal to the mixed second directional derivative of the downstream map in the two perturbation directions. So the gap is not estimator error to be reduced; it is a property of the model at that operating point. Read against run 740 (probe readout moved 0.24 while behaviour moved on ~6% of items) and run 739 (an exactly-scored influence whose Spearman dropped from 0.96 to 0.70 on the ViT), the recurrence is now three deep and the shape is identical: **two instruments aimed at the "same" causal quantity disagree, and the disagreement is the finding.** Mechanical rule for us: **every causal attribution we publish is reported as an ordered pair from both intervention directions on the same component, with the difference reported as its own quantity and the perturbation distance named - because a single-direction attribution silently adds a term whose size we do not know, and a term that is invisible at small patch distance becomes dominant at larger distances without any change to the model.** Cross-reference run 733 (an ablation that moved the measured geometry without moving AUROC in the predicted direction).
- **Pattern:** **The paper converts a nuisance into a free functional label, and that conversion is directly reusable: the *sign* of the interaction term sorts heads into three mechanistically distinct roles that neither estimator recovers alone.** Negative interaction with positive pure effect is backup compensation (the head's marginal contribution shrinks because a peer is doing the same job); positive interaction with near-zero pure effect is a context-specific mechanism whose whole contribution is realised only when the downstream circuit is intact; negative pure effect with positive interaction is a harmful mechanism partially opposed by its peers. The signs are consistent within functional groups, so clustering on the pair recovers the published labels without re-reading the circuit paper. Read against run 734 (eight collapse scalars used as covariates to pick a detector family) and run 737 (a coefficient swept to move collapse and behaviour together): those entries use a scalar as a *predictor*; this one uses a *signed difference* as a *classifier*, and the classifier's categories are causal roles rather than scores. Mechanical rule for us: **when a white-box statistic has two natural reference points (two directions, two baselines, two ablations), we compute the signed contrast and report it as a categorical variable with its role interpretation attached, rather than collapsing it into a magnitude - because the magnitude answers "how much" and the sign answers "what kind", and downstream analysis almost always needs the second.**
- **Pattern:** **The measured quantity depends on the experimental design choices the researcher makes, and three of the four sufficient conditions for it to vanish are choices - which makes "clean single-component attribution" a statement about the experiment, not the model.** The interaction term is provably zero when the component is unaffected by the input change, when the downstream map is locally affine, or when the two perturbations have no shared component in the curved directions; and empirically it is small exactly for semantically close prompt pairs, for single-word counterfactuals, and for piecewise-affine compiled models. The paper states the consequence plainly: the magnitude of the phenomenon is set by the evaluation conditions rather than by the circuit, and benchmark suites built on compiled transformers cannot exhibit it at all. Read against run 739 (a threshold grid was the only thing that showed a claimed timing lead survives its own free parameters) and run 737 (a coefficient swept to locate an interior optimum its own prose denied): the recurrence is that **a free parameter in the experimental design silently determines the sign of the result, and the paper that names it is the exception.** Mechanical rule for us: **any conclusion of the form "component X is responsible for behaviour Y" is printed alongside the corruption scheme, the prompt distance and the ablation baseline that produced it, and where more than one of those was varied we report the conclusion per setting - because the same component ranks first under one corruption scheme and one hundred and forty-second under another, and neither number is wrong.**

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's sharpest case that the instrument and the behaviour are two different measurements of the same intervention, and the dissociation is the finding: probe-only features moved the probe's own readout by 0.24 while moving behaviour on about 6 percent of statements, and shared features did the reverse (0.05 readout shift, 13-27 percent behaviour).** Read against run 736 (a norm gap manufactured by a mask in the loss), run 734 (eight geometric scalars predicting which detector family wins) and run 739 (a spectral statistic whose onset leads behaviour), the difference is that those entries measure a *property* of a state and this one measures two *outcomes* of one ablation. Mechanical rule for us: **every intervention we report is scored twice - on the model's output and on the statistic we are validating - and where the two disagree we report the pair as the result rather than picking the favourable one, because a feature set that moves the probe and not the output is proof the probe is a readout, and that is information we would otherwise discard.** Cross-reference run 733 (a compaction ablation that moved the measured geometry without moving AUROC in the predicted direction) - the same table shape, drawn out explicitly.
- **Pattern:** **The cheapest attribution signal in the folder - gradient sensitivity - earns nothing here, and the reason it earns nothing is stated: a gradient is a first-order local quantity, so it can name features that carry the wrong sign, that never fire, or that sit off the manifold the model actually visits.** Gradient-top features flip behaviour on about 6 percent, statistically indistinguishable from the activation-free geometric top-16's 6.1 percent, while the *intersection* of the two rankings flips 13-27 percent and an activation-aware selection flips 17.6. Read against run 731 (a bound repaired so unsupported inputs stay in the ranking) and run 739 (an exactly-scored per-example influence with a stated approximation error), the recurrence is that a first-order object is used as if it were the effect: one entry repairs the first-order account, this entry shows it cannot stand alone. Mechanical rule for us: **a feature-importance or attribution score is never reported as a predictor until it has been paired with a second, structurally different signal (an activation statistic, an ablation, or a downstream readout) and the pair is scored against the single signals - because a first-order score that ranks identically to a static geometric one is not measuring sensitivity, it is measuring which direction is largest.** Cross-reference run 728 (a near/mid/far stratification where the winner changes by regime).
- **Pattern:** **The paper's auxiliary experiment fails its own precondition and is reported as a boundary case rather than a null result - the folder's best example of gating a causal claim behind a decomposition check.** Only about 26 percent of the Qwen3-8B reward-hacking probe's margin survived the SAE reconstruction, with 74 percent in the reconstruction error, so ablating "probe-aligned" SAE features flipped roughly a third of predictions *in reconstruction space* and almost none on raw activations. The lesson is mechanical: an SAE-based intervention is a statement about the SAE's basis, and it says nothing about the model until you check that the quantity you are trying to explain lives inside that basis. Read against run 738 (a mechanism ablation that barely moved behaviour, reported honestly) and run 730 (a negative result repaired by measuring whether the phenomenon was expressible): three different ways of asking the same question - *was the thing we tried to manipulate even there?* Mechanical rule for us: **before any decomposition-based causal claim we print the share of the target quantity that the decomposition preserves, and if that share is small the experiment is labelled a boundary case with the number attached rather than reported as evidence either way - because a 26-percent-preserved signal guarantees a null on raw activations and a positive result on the reconstruction, and only the printed fraction tells a reader which of those two tables they are looking at.** Cross-reference run 719-style "was the effect expressible" checks generally.

- **Done:** arxiv-2607.12438 - Fisher Rank Inflation: A Spectral Signature of Memorization under Label Noise (read full v1 HTML, 157,534 chars / 2,207 lines: abstract, §1-§6 including the complete §5 Discussion with all four limitations and §6 Conclusion, §2 Related Work, §3.1-§3.9 with Lemma 1-5, Theorem 1 (both cases), Theorem 2's condition, Theorem 3, Proposition 1 and the majorization/NIR distinction, §4.1 setup with the subsampling protocol and metric definitions, §4.2-§4.7 including Table 1 (clean-difficulty control), Table 2 (cross-architecture), Table 3 (direct spectral diagnostics) and Table 4 (CIFAR-10N), Appendix A.6 with Tables 9, A.9 with Table 10 and the nine-cell onset-threshold grid, A.10 with Table 11 (centred vs uncentred Fisher); the deferred proofs and Appendix sections A.1-A.5, A.7-A.8 were **not** read beyond the statements they are cited from; Figures 1-12 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; reference list not read beyond works cited in §1-§2)
- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry where a purely weight- and gradient-side statistic visibly moves while the behavioural one on the same network has not yet moved, and it arrives with a timing claim rather than a correlation - which is the shape a predictive white-box model needs.** Rank inflation onset precedes test degradation under every one of nine onset-threshold pairs for both CIFAR-10 convolutions and CIFAR-100 ResNet18 (mean lead 22.0 to 35.7 epochs, positive fraction 1.00), and only becomes threshold-dependent for the ViT (6/9, range including negative values). The authors deliberately downgrade this to a retrospective comparison and refuse to sell it as an online predictor, which is the discipline. Read against run 737 (a coefficient swept with an error next to it, but no timing) and run 738 (shallow and deep forgetting measures plotted against one knob), the difference is that those two measure two statistics of the *same* moment while this one measures two statistics at *different* moments and reports the lag. Mechanical rule for us: **when our white-box model produces a statistic that is meant to lead behaviour, we report the onset of the statistic and the onset of the behaviour as two separately-defined epochs with the thresholds named, and we state the fraction of threshold settings on which the lead is positive - because a single default threshold pair will produce a lead time whether or not one exists, and the threshold grid is the only thing that shows whether the claim survives its own free parameters.** Cross-reference run 730 (a null repaired by measuring whether the phenomenon was expressible).
- **Pattern:** **The leave-one-out-with-re-centring closure makes an exactly scored per-example influence on any spectral statistic of a scatter matrix cheap, and that is the single most reusable technical object in this entry for us.** Deleting one example from a centred scatter has the closed form "remove a rank-one term and rescale", so `er(S) - er(S_{-i})` needs no retraining; the derivative of effective rank with respect to the eigenvalues is written out as a signed sensitivity that is positive exactly on eigendirections carrying less than the reciprocal of the effective rank in normalised mass, and the per-example score is then the centred gradient's quadratic form against that sensitivity matrix. The paper then validates the cheap score against the exact recomputation: Spearman 0.9795 (SmallCNN) and 0.9603 (ResNet18), degrading to 0.7036 (ViT) and 0.6514 (CIFAR-100), and says plainly that higher-order deletion effects explain the degradation. Read against run 728 (a near/mid/far stratification where the winner changes by regime) and run 734 (eight scalars used as covariates predicting a detector clique): those entries use checkpoint scalars only in the aggregate, whereas this one converts a scalar into a per-example quantity with a stated approximation error, which is the granularity our paper's "prediction under intervention" claim actually needs. Mechanical rule for us: **any white-box statistic we report at the model level is accompanied by its per-input or per-example decomposition and by the correlation between that cheap decomposition and the exact recomputed version, with the regimes where the correlation drops called out as the regimes where the first-order account fails - because a statistic that ranks examples well in one architecture family and near chance in another (0.96 vs 0.70 here) has told us the approximation, not the phenomenon, is architecture-dependent.** Cross-reference run 736 (a closed-form gradient account of where a geometry comes from) - here the closed form is for the *influence*, not for the geometry.
- **Pattern:** **The same sweep produces one monotone statistic and one saturating-then-falling statistic, and the difference is trace normalisation - which makes this the folder's cleanest demonstration that how you normalise a spectral quantity decides whether it carries a trend at all.** Peak effective rank rises monotonically over the corruption sweep, 28.88 -> 62.69 -> 70.37 -> 78.98 -> 87.90 -> 97.09, while the noisy-to-clean effective-rank ratio sits near 2.00 across the low end and *declines* to 1.87 at the highest corruption, which the paper reports in a single sentence and does not explain. The paper is careful elsewhere about exactly this distinction - §3.7 spends a full subsection arguing that the ratio (computed after centring each group separately) is logically distinct from the globally centred additive decomposition, and Proposition 1 gives only a one-sided majorisation guarantee `NIR >= 1` that cannot license a monotone claim. Read against run 735 (per-class vs per-sample loss normalisation as the load-bearing variable, where the same architecture with a different objective produced a completely different similarity matrix) and run 731 (a deficit average hiding the losing cells): the recurrence is now three entries deep and the lesson has generalised from losses to statistics - **a normalisation is a modelling choice with a directional consequence, and it is almost never the one the paper's headline is about.** Mechanical rule for us: **every spectral or geometric statistic we publish is printed in raw and trace-normalised form side by side, any monotonicity claim names which of the two it holds for, and where the two disagree we treat the raw one as the sweep's signal and the normalised one as a measure of shape - because trace normalisation divides out exactly the magnitude that grows with the knob, and a ratio that saturates near 2 while its numerator grows threefold is measuring shape, not amount.** Cross-reference run 737 (a coefficient sweep whose optimum was interior and whose flatness was misread as insensitivity).

- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's cleanest single-knob experiment on a two-part decomposition, and it delivers the shape our white-box model needs: one control variable, two statistics of the same network, and a theory that says which of them moves first.** Buffer size is swept over ten settings; deep forgetting (a logistic probe on frozen features) saturates at the smallest non-zero buffer while shallow forgetting (the network's own accuracy on past tasks) keeps degrading until near-full replay, so the gap between the two curves is the finding and the sweep is the whole table. Read against run 737 (a collapse coefficient swept on a ten-class appendix with an interior optimum) and run 734 (eight NC scalars used as covariates predicting a detector clique), the methodological advance is small but real: the knob here is *data availability*, not a loss coefficient, so the manipulation is a change in support rather than a change in the objective, and that makes it the closest experiment in the folder to our question of what a model does on inputs whose support it has lost. Mechanical rule for us: **when we claim a white-box statistic tracks behaviour, we report it alongside a matched statistic computed on the *other* half of the model's decomposition (features vs readout), both plotted against the same controlled knob, because a statistic that saturates at a tenth of the knob's range while the behaviour does not is a statistic about the representation, and calling it a predictor of the behaviour is the mistake this paper avoids by printing both curves.** Cross-reference run 736 (a training term that manufactures the geometry) and run 735 (head geometry generated by class support).
- **Pattern:** **The paper's own ablation confirms a mechanism and then reports that removing it barely changes the behaviour - the inverse of the folder's usual defect, and the more useful version, because it kills the theory's practical claim in one figure.** Appendix A.3.3 attributes the monotone growth of the centred class-mean norm to initialisation shock (new heads created with smaller norms than converged ones), shows that norm-matching initialisation suppresses the growth, and then states that final forgetting and test accuracy barely move. The theory says this norm sits in the denominator of the separability bound, so suppressing it should have helped; the paper prints the contradiction rather than burying it. Against run 737 (a mechanism ablation where every row moved something, in opposite directions) and run 736 (a term whose only stake was the sign of the effect), the difference is the kind of ablation: those perturbed the *objective*, this one perturbs an *artefact* of the training schedule and shows the artefact was not load-bearing. Mechanical rule for us: **we require every causal claim in our write-up to name the ablation that would falsify it, and when that ablation is run and brings a negligible change we record the claim as failing even though the mechanism is real - because "the mechanism exists" and "the mechanism is what moves the output" are separate claims, and only the second one supports a prediction.**

- **Done:** arxiv-2502.10691 - Controlling Neural Collapse Enhances Out-of-Distribution Detection and Transfer Learning (read full v3 HTML, 148,765 chars / 5,848 lines: abstract, §1-§8 including the complete §7 Discussion, §2.1-§2.3, §3 with the three error definitions and the energy score, §4.1 with Proposition 4.1 and the Kozachenko-Leonenko nearest-neighbour entropy estimate, §4.2 with the rank-(D-1) simplex ETF formula, §5 setup, §6.1-§6.6 with Tables 1-9 reconstructed from the column-major cells, Appendix A-E, Appendix G with Tables 13-14 including every per-dataset cell, Appendix H.1 Table 15; Figures 1-9 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; reference list not read beyond works cited in §1-§2)
- **Pattern:** **This is the causal test the folder has been carrying since run 735, and it arrives in a cheaper form than anyone expected: the collapse level of a layer is a training knob, and the cleanest controlled comparison needs only ONE training run because the encoder and the projector in the same network sit at opposite ends of that knob.** VGG17 trained with an entropy penalty on the encoder and a frozen simplex-ETF projector gives NC1 0.393 in the projector and 2.175 in the encoder, with detection error 65.10 against 87.62 and transfer error 66.36 against 41.85 — same weights, two embeddings, two behavioural errors, and the sign is exactly what the geometry predicts. Against runs 735 and 736, which established that geometry is an *output* of the objective, this entry supplies the opposite arrow: the objective is a dial, and moving the dial moves the behaviour. Mechanical rule for us: **when our white-box model reports a geometric statistic and attributes a behavioural effect to it, we first ask whether that statistic can be moved by a training term we control, and if it can we report the manipulation rather than the correlation — because a statistic that responds to a single coefficient is a knob, and a knob is a better claim than a slope.** Cross-reference run 736 (a loss term that manufactures the norm gap in closed form), run 734 (self-duality of the head carrying the detector prediction).
- **Pattern:** **The one place the axis is swept properly is buried in an appendix on a ten-class dataset, and it is the only evidence in the paper that the relation is a curve rather than a sign — which is the same defect as run 733's single compaction point and run 731's single bound value, now in its sixth appearance.** Table 14 on ImageNet-10 VGG17 with the entropy coefficient at 0 / 0.1 / 0.2 / 0.6 / 1.0: effective rank 2211.99 -> 4815.32, transfer error 77.78 -> 64.96 and ID error 9.20 -> 12.80. Most of the transfer gain is in the first 0.1 (77.78 -> 68.87), after which the curve is nearly flat, while the ID cost keeps rising roughly linearly — and transfer at 1.0 is *worse* than at 0.6 (64.96 against 65.15), so the optimum is interior and the paper's own sentence that the method is "not sensitive to coefficient" is a claim its own table contradicts at the top of the range. Note also that the main trade-off claim rests on one point per configuration with no seeds and no variance. Read against run 733 (an ablation inside the seed spread), run 729 (a correlation over surviving seeds) and run 731 (a deficit table whose summary hid the losing cells): **the folder now has six entries where the number that would settle the claim exists but is either unpublished, unswept, or averaged away.** Mechanical rule for us: **any claim that a mechanism improves a behaviour is accompanied by that behaviour plotted against the mechanism's own strength at no fewer than three settings, with the optimum located on the curve rather than assumed at the setting we chose — because a monotone-sounding method claim and an interior optimum are different claims, and a table that sweeps is the only thing that tells them apart.** Cross-reference run 732 (a tuned k whose optimum degenerated to 1).
- **Pattern:** **Every mechanism ablation moves two or three behaviours in different directions, and the paper prints all of them, which makes it the folder's clearest case that "improves X" needs X named per row — including one pair where the more collapsed model is also the more accurate one.** The frozen ETF projector beats a trainable projector by 8.9 detection points, loses 2.47 on transfer and *wins* 2.48 on ID error; group norm beats batch norm by 10.11 transfer and 4.37 detection while raising NC1 from 1.401 to 2.175; CE beats MSE by 6.74 detection and 17.71 transfer; and the encoder's weaker collapse costs 3.34 points of ID error on VGG17 and 0.06 on ViT-Tiny. So collapse is not a single axis for the model's behaviour — it is rewarded by detection, punished by transfer, and rewarded by in-distribution accuracy in at least one pairing. Read against run 728 (a manipulation that moved a phenomenon in opposite directions across two architectures, reported honestly) and run 734 (near/mid/far strata where the detector winner changes by regime): **the shape claim survives every sweep while the direction claim flips by task, architecture or pair.** Mechanical rule for us: **a geometry change we publish is reported against every behavioural metric in the study simultaneously — including the in-distribution one — and where two metrics move in opposite directions we print the pair rather than a summary, because a mechanism that helps detection, hurts transfer and helps accuracy is not "beneficial" or "harmful" and cannot be reported as either.** Cross-reference run 731 (an average that was best while a third of the cells were losses).

- **Done:** arxiv-2504.12569 - Let the Void Be Void: Robust Open-Set Semi-Supervised Learning via Selective Non-Alignment (read full v4 HTML, 82,424 chars / 1,925 lines: abstract, sec.1-5, Eq. 1-9, Tables 1-6 reconstructed from the column-major cells, App.A.1-A.7 including the four gradient derivations, the combined-dynamics section and both Rademacher bounds with Theorem 1 and Theorem 2, App.B prototype blending, App.C.1-C.2, App.D.1-D.2 with Tables 5-6, App.E.1 Algorithm 1 and E.2 training details; Figures 1-9 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; reference list not read beyond works cited in sec.1-2)
- **Pattern:** **This is the first entry in the arc where the known-vs-unknown separation statistic is something the training objective MANUFACTURES, and the paper prints the accounting for it in closed form - which converts run 734's "geometry measures the loss" warning into an explicit mechanism.** Appendix A writes the norm derivative for every loss in the stack (cross-entropy, consistency, OVA) as a cosine against the relevant weight vector times the gradient magnitude, so norm growth is confidence-times-alignment; the non-alignment term multiplies the positive cosine term by zero and leaves only the tangent-plane projection, so uncertain and OOD items accumulate norm along a direction no prototype occupies. The visible consequence is a clean norm gap between known and unknown (their Figure 2b) plus a low-cosine/low-norm void (Figure 2c). Read against run 734 (collapsed-and-self-dual representation predicts which detector family suits a model), run 733 (a score hurt by over-compaction at a spread ratio of 7.5) and run 732 (a density score whose tuned k collapsed to 1): four entries now, and all four say the same thing from different sides - **the geometric quantity a probe reads off a checkpoint is an output of the objective that trained it, and its sign is known in advance once you know what the loss asked for.** Mechanical rule for us: **when our white-box model scores an input by norm, prototype distance, or margin, we state which training term created that quantity and its expected sign, and where that term is present the score is reported as a readout of the objective rather than as evidence about the input - because a gap that a one-line mask in the loss produced will be found by any probe, and finding it tells us nothing about the model's uncertainty.**
- **Pattern:** **The paper's only honest table is the one that separates seen from unseen, and it shows the generalisation term earning its keep exactly where its name says it should and losing slightly where it does not - the seen/unseen split is the cheap version of run 730's "was the phenomenon expressible" check.** Table 3: no SNA losses gives seen 88.6 / unseen 76.2; supervised alignment alone (IA+PA) gives seen 91.5 / unseen 77.7; adding the unlabelled non-alignment term takes unseen to 79.1 but seen *down* to 91.2. That single split carries the paper's entire claim, and it is also the only place where a term is allowed to cost something. Everywhere else the metric is "overall AUC", an unweighted mean over six to nine OOD sets, which cannot show a trade because a trade averaged against itself looks additive. Note against run 735 (a paper with no task metric at all, where the absence had to be stated outright), run 725 (a bound whose failing constant was estimated rather than argued away) and run 730 (a negative result repaired by measuring base rates): **the folder now has four consecutive entries where the useful number is a two-column split rather than a headline average.** Mechanical rule for us: **any claim about generalising to a held-out or unseen family is reported as a seen column and an unseen column with the trade visible, never as their mean - because a mean over the two is the one summary in which a term that helps only on new data and hurts slightly on old data cannot be distinguished from a term that does nothing.**
- **Pattern:** **The ablation tables are point estimates in a paper whose main table prints a standard deviation larger than the ablation differences, so the three ablations that carry the method's story are all inside the paper's own noise - and this run's own caution applies to its own entry, which is why the numbers above are quoted as measured rather than as findings.** Table 1 reports CIFAR-100 80/20-25 AUC at 82.8 with +/-3.2; Table 2 compares the pull-only cosine variant to the full loss at 79.4 vs 80.8 in the same configuration with no variance given; Table 5's nonlinearity gain is 1.7 overall / 2.0 unseen; Table 3's three comparisons are 0.3 to 1.4 points with no variance. The one effect far outside all of that is the detector half of the gate (threshold 0 costs 14.4 AUC points on CIFAR-10 6/4-50), and it is the least discussed number in the paper. Read against run 733 (a headline 0.0117 against a seed spread of 0.0085, with the five-seed study run on the losing variant), run 731 (a deficit table whose summary hid the losing cells) and run 729 (a correlation over surviving seeds): **five entries now, and the defect has migrated from "which seeds survived" to "which differences anyone would believe".** Mechanical rule for us: **an ablation difference we publish carries the variance of the configuration it is a difference between, drawn from the paper's own table where one exists, and if no variance is printed for that cell the difference is labelled unresolved rather than reported as a gain - because a 0.7-point gap inside a +/-3.2 seed spread is not a result, and treating it as one is the single most common way this literature produces findings that do not replicate.**

The paper has **no Limitations section** and no task metric - no accuracy, no AUROC, no detection number - so the entry says so outright rather than implying an evaluation exists)
- **Pattern:** **The folder now has the answer to the open-set half of the collapse question, and it is a warning rather than a tool: a classifier vector with zero training samples behind it is not idle, it converges to a specific direction with the same confidence and the same tiny variance as a trained one.** Theorems 2 and 3 say that with K₁ populated classes and K−K₁ empty ones, the empty vectors all coincide and point along minus the global feature mean, orthogonal to the trained weights; empirically they reach about 0.999-1.000 mutual similarity by epoch 50, i.e. *before* the known classes have settled. Read against run 734 (self-duality and weight equiangularity carry the OOD-detector prediction, so the head rather than the feature cloud is the object of interest) and run 733 (compaction moved the spread ratio without moving AUROC in the direction theory wanted), the distinction is now usable: those entries argued that head geometry matters; this one says head geometry is *generated* by support, and where support is zero the geometry is generated by something else entirely. Mechanical rule for us: **when we read prototypes or classifier rows out of a model and treat a low-support or zero-support direction as "unused", we first measure the gram matrix among such directions and their angle to the global feature mean - because the theory here says empty directions collapse onto one another and look as crisp as trained ones, and a crispness statistic computed without checking for that collapse cannot tell a real class from a placeholder.**
- **Pattern:** **The load-bearing variable is the loss's normalisation, and the paper's whole result is that changing it from per-sample to per-class is what converts a distorted geometry into the standard one - which makes normalisation a confound in every cross-model geometry comparison the folder has run.** Per-class averaging is algebraically inverse-frequency weighting, and at IF=100 the globally averaged objective prints class frequency straight into the similarity matrix (off-diagonals grading from about −0.3 in the head classes to about 0.1 in the tail, a span of roughly 0.4) while the class-wise version stays inside a band under 0.024. The BCE bias is the cleanest confirmation available in this literature: 4.663-4.669 measured against 4.6698 predicted on CIFAR10-LT across four imbalance factors, and 6.328-6.332 against 6.3437 on CIFAR100-LT, on two architectures - agreement that a free parameter cannot fake. Compare run 731 (a bound repaired by adding a reciprocal-density term) and run 732 (a score whose tuned k degenerated to 1): three entries now in which the difference between two configurations is a single algebraic term in the objective rather than an architectural or data choice. Mechanical rule for us: **any claim we make that two checkpoints differ in geometry, collapse, or prototype quality carries the loss normalisation of each, and where the normalisations differ we treat the geometry difference as attributable to the loss until a matched-normalisation comparison says otherwise - because per-class and per-sample averaging are the same architecture with different objectives, and no measured similarity can distinguish them afterwards.**
- **Pattern:** **The one prediction the paper reports failing is a conditioning failure rather than a refutation, and it is the folder's cleanest example of a theorem whose residual is numerical - the score you want is a direction of a vector whose norm is going to zero.** The empty-class vector should sit exactly antiparallel to the global feature mean, and at the theoretical minimum it does; but as the mean norm collapses toward zero any perturbation reorients it, so the measured cosine wanders (−0.6181 for BCE at epoch 600, and −0.2504 → −0.0492 → −0.0442 across CE stages in Table 3) while every other statistic in the same table is within noise of its constant. The paper states the cause plainly and leaves the quantity in the table rather than dropping it. Read against run 729 (a correlation computed on surviving seeds) and run 727 (a score whose invariance group was proved before its diagnostic value was claimed): the difference is that those papers' numbers were compromised by which samples survived, while this number is compromised by the arithmetic of a normalised angle near the origin. Mechanical rule for us: **a normalised direction or cosine statistic we publish is accompanied by the norm of the vector it was computed from, and where that norm falls below the scale at which the direction is stable we report the instability rather than the angle - because a ratio of two small quantities is a measurement of noise, and an error term that is a conditioning problem is fixed by changing the statistic, not by adding data.**

- **Done:** arxiv-2511.11934 - A Systematic Analysis of Out-of-Distribution Detection Under Representation and Training Paradigm Shifts (read full v4 HTML, 302,325 chars / 8,773 lines: abstract, §1-§6 including the complete five-paragraph §5 Limitations, §2, §3.1-§3.6 (the eight NC metric definitions, the three PCA projection variants, the CLIP near/mid/far protocol with its robustness checks, the Friedman/Conover/Bron-Kerbosch pipeline, and the per-CSF logistic predictor with its set-regret evaluation and three baselines), §4.1-§4.4 with the Mantel numbers and the VGG-13→ResNet-18 regret table in prose, Appendix A (hyperparameter selection, the 5-seeds-per-cell statement), Appendix B in full including all eight metric formulas, the note on how NC1 is normalised, Table 5(a)(b)(c) reconstructed from header column order, and §B.1.1-B.1.4 plus the ViT note, Appendix C checkpoint counts, Appendix D, Appendix E (the Friedman and Iman-Davenport statistics), Appendix G.1 (the three-way partial Mantel result incl. the ViT learning-rate collapse), Appendix H (coefficient heatmap reading, Table 26 configurations), Appendix I (the worked regret row), Appendix J, Appendix K (CNN→ViT, Table 31), Appendix L (self-supervised probes), Appendix M (ImageNet, Table 34 and per-family text); Figures 1, 2, 5 and 6 read from captions and the coefficient prose, **not inspected as images**; reference list not read beyond the works cited in §1-§3)
- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry where a purely checkpoint-derived geometric measurement predicts a held-out outcome with no fitted behavioural coefficient - and the paper's own random-shortlist control removes most of the credit at exactly the scale where the folder has been asking for prediction.** The instrument is the eight Neural Collapse scalars, all computable from training data alone, and the outcome is which of 22 detectors lands in a statistically indistinguishable top clique; inside the CNN family it works (55-80% regret reduction, 65 of 66 comparisons), and on 47 ImageNet models a **random shortlist of the same cardinality reaches 1.25 against the predictor's 1.05**, significant under the rank test and not under the mean test, while on 40 self-supervised probes it loses outright at far shift (8.08 against 0.85 for Maha++). Read against run 731 (a per-sample bound scored on held-out data, with validation-only hyperparameters) and run 730 (a negative result repaired by measuring whether the phenomenon was expressible), the distinction is now sharp and reusable: **a representation-side statistic can be validated in the aggregate — does it rank models the way behaviour ranks them — without being validated as a cause, and the control that separates those two is a random draw of matched size, not a stronger baseline.** Mechanical rule for us: **every selector, router or probe-set we publish that is derived from white-box statistics is scored against a random draw of the same cardinality in the same table, and we report which of the two tests it passes (rank test or mean-difference test), because a predictor that contains the oracle 57% of the time against 37% expected by chance can still be beaten by chance once eight near-tied candidates exist.**
- **Pattern:** **The collapse geometry that carries the prediction is not the spread ratio the folder has been chasing — self-duality and the equiangularity of the classifier weights are, which moves the object of interest from the features to the linear head.** The within/between covariance ratio is measured and printed (VGG-13 around 0.002-0.010 against roughly 0.012-0.037 for the ViT) but it is one of eight covariates, and the strongest single metric is the maximal equiangularity of the classifier weights in three of four strata (0.68-0.77, and 0.88 for the equiangularity of the weights in the weakest stratum). The coefficient signs say the same thing from the other direction: CTM is favoured when the representation is collapsed *and* self-dual (negative on both), while fDBD is favoured precisely when the weights do **not** align with the class means (positive on self-duality), i.e. the two most-recommended feature-side detectors divide on a property of the head, not of the feature cloud. Note the ViT explanation the paper offers in Appendix B.4 — pretrained residuals mean OOD inputs land inside subspaces created during pretraining, so class-conditional PCA filtering is the only intervention that helps ViTs, and global projection is the only one that helps CNNs. Read against run 733 (a compaction ablation that moved the spread ratio without moving the AUROC in the direction the theory wanted) and run 732 (a density score whose tuned optimum degenerated to a single neighbour): **the folder's geometric covariates are turning out to be properties of the readout rather than of the representation.** Mechanical rule for us: **when we attribute a reliability or interpretability effect to feature geometry we regress it against the head-geometry terms (self-duality, weight equiangularity) as well, and if those absorb the effect we say the finding is about the classifier rather than the representation — because a collapsed feature cloud with a misaligned head behaves nothing like a collapsed cloud with a self-dual one, and only one of those is a claim about the model's inner state.**
- **Pattern:** **The prior-shift result this entry supplies closes the folder's near-shift thread in the negative: geometry-derived scores carry no information about near distribution shift, and the failure is now replicated across three independent instruments with chance-level numbers in the same table.** Run 732's embedding-density score sat at 0.5867 AUROC on its internal near-shift set with Energy 0.5858, Mahalanobis 0.5850 and ODIN 0.5878 — all at chance — and here the whole detector-family analysis is built on near/mid/far stratification because the near regime is where the transitions are least decisive (near-OOD on CNNs is dominated by Energy and MLS, which read the logits; the geometry-aware detectors only take over at mid and far). Note also that the paper's own three-group stratification is argued for on the grounds that a binary near/far split hides the mid-OOD winners (a ViT far clique of 8 CSFs collapsing into a focused KPCA-RecError mid clique and a distinct Residual/ViM far clique). Read against run 725 (a bound whose failing constant was estimated rather than argued away) and run 723 (a rank at a fixed variance cut measuring the measurement): **the reason near-shift geometry fails is that near-shift OOD inputs sit in the same region of feature space as in-distribution inputs, so every statistic that summarises that region — density, distance, boundary count, NC metric — returns the same value for both.** Mechanical rule for us: **a near-shift claim is our test set's hardest case and gets its own row in every table, reported at chance when it is at chance, and we never average a near-shift cell into a headline that is dominated by far-shift rows — because the aggregate that includes far shift is the aggregate where geometry-derived scores look useful.**
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The Priority block is empty, so run 733's carried candidate #1 was taken - the spread-ratio instrument (does any paper report the intra-class to inter-class feature-spread ratio as a measured quantity and tie it to a per-sample reliability outcome), with the paper's own §2.3 line ("Liu and Qin 2025", "Momeni et al. 2026 / AnaNC") named as the place to resolve first. One web search on neural-collapse OOD geometry returned four candidates: `2605.14413` (MahaVar, a class-wise Mahalanobis-variance detector derived under weak NC1/NC2), ENCORE at WACV 2026 (feature scaling for energy scores under an added NC5 about OOD means being orthogonal to class means), `2610.24844` (an ETF impossibility result, not a reliability paper) and **`2511.11934`**, which was chosen over MahaVar and ENCORE because both of those *derive a new score from NC assumptions* while this one uses NC metrics as **covariates and predicts an existing detector's competitiveness** - the predictive shape run 733's carry called for, rather than a third post-hoc score. The missing measurement is therefore no longer "a predictive score at matched seeds" (run 733's framing) but **a statistic that separates the near-shift regime from the in-distribution one at all**; nothing we have read does, and three entries now report chance-level near-shift AUROC in their own tables.

- **Pattern:** **The folder's per-sample-reliability line now has both ends of a U, and the quantity that moves along it is the ratio of within-class spread to between-class separation: run 732's density score degenerated to a single nearest neighbour because proximity was all that mattered, and this paper's covariance-aware score gets *worse* when the features are deliberately compacted past a spread ratio of 2.5.** CenterLoss raises that ratio to 7.5 (the manipulation is shown to have happened, which is the one piece of ablation hygiene run 732 lacked when it reported k=1 without a curve) and costs 0.0117 average out-of-distribution AUROC while buying about 0.003 in-distribution accuracy. Read against run 731 (a bound repaired by adding a reciprocal-density term, so no sample is ever dropped) and run 725 (a bound repaired by estimating its failing constant), the recurrence is now three deep: **every reliability score in this folder is a function of two spreads, and the entries differ only in which spread they fix and which they let move.** Mechanical rule for us: **a reliability score fitted to class-conditional geometry reports the intra/inter spread ratio it was fitted on, and any "tighter" or "wider" claim is made as a two-point comparison at matched seeds - because the effect here (0.0117) is smaller than the seed spread of the configuration being compared (0.0085 standard deviation, range 0.9209-0.9448), and an ablation on one seed against a stability study on the other variant cannot tell the two apart.**
- **Pattern:** **The cleanest result in the paper is the one its own appendix theorem predicts, and it is not the headline: dropping the early-layer tap costs 0.0096 average and 0.0175 on the domain-shift set, exactly where the likelihood-ratio factorisation says a texture-level shift must be invisible to semantic features.** Theorem 1 says that if the two distributions agree on the conditional given both layers, the density ratio depends on the pair, and a shift living only in the early layer's marginal is undetectable from the late layer alone - so the ablation is a forced consequence, not a discovery. The headline (CenterLoss hurting) is the part the theory only sketches: Theorem 4's proof argues simultaneously that tight clusters make Mahalanobis *more* sensitive (large inverse eigenvalues) and that they collapse the distance to a midpoint between two class means, and the paper does not reconcile the two directions. Note against run 727 (a folding score whose invariances were proved and whose diagnostic value was never tested) and run 731 (a bound whose repair was argued, then scored): the discipline that separates these is whether the theorem's prediction is the thing that gets measured. Mechanical rule for us: **when we publish both a proposition and an ablation, we state which ablation the proposition forces and which it merely permits, and we do not cite a permitting proposition as an explanation of a measured effect - because a proof that covers half of a claim reads, in the conclusion, as covering all of it.**
- **Pattern:** **The winning model is trained on one seed, the losing model is the one given the five-seed study, and the paper's own variance numbers therefore bracket its entire effect - the survivorship shape of run 729, arriving here from the opposite direction.** The five-seed table belongs to GOEN-Default (with CenterLoss, mean 0.9337, standard deviation 0.0085, per-seed 0.9209 to 0.9448), while GOEN-NoCenterLoss is reported at seed 42 only, with the paper stating it is "expected" to be stable because it shares an architecture. Meanwhile the calibration head is fitted on half of SVHN's test set, so the single-set gain that carries the average sits on the shift whose shape the head was tuned on; the defence is disjointness, which rules out leakage of samples but not of distribution shape. Read against run 730 (a negative result repaired by measuring the base rate of the phenomenon) and run 729 (a depth correlation computed on surviving seeds): the folder now has four consecutive instances of the same defect - **the comparison the reader needs is not the comparison the paper ran.** Mechanical rule for us: **every variant we compare is run on the same number of seeds as its competitor and the per-seed values are printed, and any component fitted on data resembling an evaluation set is reported in that set's row as "tuned on this shift", because a one-seed winner against an N-seed loser is not a comparison, and a calibration set drawn from the evaluation distribution is a prior, not a control.**

- **Pattern:** **The folder's long-running "what does a bound buy" question now has its control condition: keep the density, drop the bound, and the score becomes cheap, model-agnostic and — on the near-shift set — exactly at chance.** This paper measures per-sample reliability as a neighbour count in a frozen embedding space, which is the continuous-representation instrument `arxiv-2106.08365` said it could not supply without a new derivation; the derivation does not exist here, and in its place is an empirical curve. The cost is visible in the numbers: on the internal ImageNet-V2 test set the embedding-density score reaches 0.5867 AUROC while Energy (0.5858), Mahalanobis (0.5850) and ODIN (0.5878) are all equally at chance, and only on ObjectNet, where base accuracy has already collapsed to ~0.30, does it reach 0.851 against Energy's ~0.90. Read against the partition paper's theorem-bound construction, the lesson is that the *bound* was doing work the density alone does not: it gave a reason for the ranking that survives when the ranking itself is uninformative. Mechanical rule for us: **when we swap a reasoned bound for an empirical score, we report the score on the regime where the behaviour of interest is common as well as the regime where it is rare - because the near-shift cells here are exactly where all four methods tie at chance, and an aggregate that is dominated by the easy regime will hide that.** Cross-reference run 731 (the bound plus the reciprocal-density term), run 725 (a bound whose failing constant was estimated rather than argued away), run 726 (a headline measured with a different instrument than the bound written for it).
- **Pattern:** **The tuned hyperparameter is the real finding: five of six classifiers want exactly one nearest neighbour, so what is installed is nearest-neighbour identity, not local density — and the paper reports the number without drawing the conclusion.** For ResNet-101 and ResNet-50 the best (N, L) pair is N = 1; only the strongest embedding (DINO-v2 ViT-B/14) prefers 7 to 60 neighbours depending on classifier. Every sentence about "the local geometry of the representation space" therefore rests on a filter that is, for most pairs, "is the single closest ImageNet image close enough". Read against run 730 (a composite score that had to be factored into rate-of-application and rate-of-correctness before it could be judged) and run 723 (a metric evaluated at a fixed variance cut), the recurrence is that a k-NN or kernel-density statistic silently degenerates to k = 1 and the degenerate case reads as success. Mechanical rule for us: **every k-NN, kernel-density or neighbourhood reliability score we publish reports its tuned k, the full curve over k, and the sentence "at k = 1 this reduces to nearest-neighbour identity" whenever the optimum is 1 - because a density claim whose optimum is a single neighbour has not demonstrated that density matters, only that proximity does.**
- **Pattern:** **The controlled comparison in the Discussion is the folder's cleanest separation of "capacity" from "pretraining distribution", and it is done by holding dimensionality fixed across two model generations rather than by ablating anything.** DINO-v1 ViT-B/16 and DINO-v2 ViT-B/14 are both 768-dimensional, and DINO-v2 wins by 0.04-0.07 normalised gain across every classifier and test split even at matched input resolution; adding dimensions *within* a family (S/16 -> B/16 -> B/8) buys only 0.01-0.03. MobileNet-V2, with the widest embedding of all at 1000 dimensions but a narrow supervised pretraining set, is last on every row (0.169-0.244), which is the same fact stated as a negative. Note this against run 731 (a density argument whose partition was defined by one layer's activations, so "representation" meant a layer not a corpus) and run 724 (an exact object with no diagnostic content): the recurring shape is that "better representation" is usually a bundle, and the only way to attribute the gain is to hold one factor fixed and change the other. Mechanical rule for us: **when we credit a representation with better reliability or interpretability, we name which factor changed - dimensionality, resolution, or training distribution - and we cite a comparison in which exactly one of those moved, because a bundled "more sophisticated model" claim cannot be reproduced and cannot be told apart from the effect of a larger unlabelled corpus.** Cross-reference run 720 (a drift multiplier given as 2,000x and 1,300x against identical endpoints - a bundled quantity reported as a single scalar).

(5) **Carried candidates for run 733 (SINGLE), in order**: first, **the k=1 result is now the sharpest open question in the folder** - does any 2024-2026 paper show a *density* statistic beating a *nearest-neighbour-distance* statistic on per-sample reliability with k tuned well above 1, i.e. does local density matter at all beyond proximity? Search on "rare cluster" / "low-density region generalisation" / "k-NN reliability k ablation" and gate before naming; second, **the model-selection thread run 731 raised and this paper half-answers** - that reliability may scale with the share of inputs landing in high-density representation regions is now supported only by the *corpus-diversity* comparison (DINO-v2 vs DINO-v1 at matched 768 dimensions), which does not test the share of test inputs, so a paper that measures a held-out behavioural quantity against a measured high-density share would close it; third, if both fail, the **alignment / SAE-side head of the queue** in SEED_QUEUE.md, untouched for many runs. (6) **What the head wants next**: the two density entries (731, 732) now bracket the question - one has a bound and a partition, one has neither - and the missing piece is a paper that has *both* a cheap density statistic and a reasoned per-sample error estimate, i.e. a calibrated interval rather than a ranking. No entry in the folder supplies a per-sample reliability claim that comes with a stated error bar.

- **Done:** arxiv-2106.08365 - Test Sample Accuracy Scales with Training Sample Density in Neural Networks (read full v7 HTML, 61,855 chars / 1,453 lines: abstract, §1-§5, §2.1-§2.3 with Eq. 1-16, Theorem 2.1 plus its full Chernoff/Hoeffding proof in Appendix A, Appendix B (the complexity reduction, Eq. 32-40 and the binomial shell argument), Appendix C (Algorithm 1, lines 1-14), Appendix D (Tables 3-7), Appendix E (metric formulas Eq. 41-42), Appendix F (hyperparameter grids, dataset sizes, compute), Appendix G (Table 8 and the baseline boxplots); Figures 1-5 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; reference list read only as far as the works cited in §1-§3)
- **Pattern:** **This is the first paper in the folder whose "explanation" is a per-sample predictive bound that is actually scored against a held-out behaviour - and it gets there by repairing a bound instead of replacing it, which is the cheapest move in the arc.** The textbook per-region Hoeffding bound (their Eq. 4) is finite only when a region holds training samples; in a deep net almost every test input lands in a region holding none, so the bound is literally infinite and unusable for ranking. Instead of abandoning the partition, they smooth the empirical error across neighbouring subfunctions with a Gaussian kernel in Hamming distance between activation patterns, which makes the bound finite everywhere and leaves a two-term score: weighted neighbour error plus 1/P(h_i), the reciprocal of local training density. Read against run 730 (where the negative result was repaired by measuring whether the phenomenon was expressible at all) and run 725 (a bound whose failing constant was estimated rather than argued away), the discipline is now explicit in three consecutive runs: **when a published bound is infinite, undefined or inconsistent in the regime that matters, the repair is the contribution, and the repair has to be visible in the score's algebra.** Mechanical rule for us: **every bound or score we publish is written in the form (fit term) + (uncertainty term) with the uncertainty term named as a function of local sample density, and we report its value at the specific test inputs where our evaluation set has no support - because a bound that returns infinity on five percent of the data is a bound that has silently decided those samples do not exist, and the density reciprocal is the term that keeps them in the ranking.** Cross-reference run 726 (a control curve that separated reorganisation from flattening), run 722 (an invariance number whose arithmetic, not its geometry, produced the finding).
- **Pattern:** **A metric that is the best on average and best in only two of four categories is reported here as a per-category *difference from the winner*, which is the honest table shape - and it also removes the reader's ability to see the cells where the method loses, which the appendix then quietly contains.** Table 1 gives only the deficit to the top method per architecture x dataset (-0.007 for theirs, -0.014 for entropy, -0.181 for the GP), so the headline reads as dominance; the absolute tables are in Appendix C-D, where ResNet50-CIFAR100 in-distribution AUROC is 0.827 against entropy's 0.859 and cluster distance edges theirs 0.754 to 0.750 on one OOD cell. The abstract's "up to 20% absolute" is the CIFAR100 effective-accuracy best case (70-73% -> 90-92%) standing next to a roughly 7-point CIFAR10 gain that the same sentence does not distinguish. Read against run 721 (six prose/table disagreements in one paper), run 720 (a multiplier quoted as 2,000x and 1,300x), run 722 (PC1 given as 68-83% in one section and 70-94% in another): the recurring shape is a summary statistic whose aggregation level hides the dispersion. Mechanical rule for us: **a comparison table we publish carries the absolute numbers for every method in every cell, and any "difference from the best" summary is printed beside the absolute table rather than instead of it - because a deficit average is a mean over cells, and a mean over four cells can be negative while a third of the cells are losses, which is exactly the margin that decides whether a practitioner adopts the tool.** Cross-reference run 729 (a correlation computed on the surviving seed subset), run 723 (five contributions, four unmeasured).
- **Pattern:** **The theoretical machinery generalises past its own paper: completeness of the bit space turns two exponential sums over activation patterns into O(M) and O(N^2 + M^3) with binomial shell counts, and that is the same Hamming-shell identity every walk-based geometric statistic in this arc needs to be tractable.** Because every pattern at Hamming distance b from a fixed pattern contributes the same kernel value, the normaliser does not depend on which pattern you are normalising (Eq. 35-36), and the double sum over the full bit space collapses to a look-up table indexed by the Hamming distance between two observed patterns, built by iterating shells and choosing which flips move toward versus away from the target (Eq. 40). Deep networks are saved from an intractable partition only by assuming the *full* bit space - which is precisely the idealisation that assigns density to unreachable regions and that the authors list as a limitation. Read against run 728 (LC computed by one forward pass over a sampled hull) and run 729 (folding by a walk whose bit gaps exceed one in deep layers), the shared constraint is now clear: every cheap partition statistic in this folder is bought by pretending the feasible pattern set is the whole bit cube. Mechanical rule for us: **when we adopt a cheap partition or walk statistic we state which idealisation purchased the cheapness - full bit space instead of the feasible set, last layer only, sampled hull instead of the region - and we measure the error that idealisation introduces at least once, because the exponential-to-polynomial trick here is valid exactly when the reachable set is the cube, and the paper's own numbers are then a lower bound rather than an estimate.** Cross-reference run 727 (an extension whose lost structure the authors named in the same paragraph), run 725 (bias-only noise as the perturbation that keeps the geometry).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The Priority block is empty, so run 730's top carried candidate was taken. **One correction to the carried description, worth recording:** run 730's note called `arxiv-2106.08365` "Shattering a Neural Network", but the `/abs/` page returned the real title, **"Test Sample Accuracy Scales with Training Sample Density in Neural Networks"** (v1 2021-06-15, v7 2022-07-28, CoLLAs 2022 oral) - the word "shattering" survives only in the figure caption describing the decomposition. The fetched abs page was therefore trusted over the carried slug, and the title-substring gate was re-read against the real title. Gate-tested FREE before the fetch: `/tmp/lit/gate.sh 2106.08365 2509.15441` -> both `aether:0 core:0 idx_a:0 idx_c:0`; `grep -ic 'shattering'` on both INDEX bodies returned 1 / 0 and the single hit was **another paper's title** (`goodfire-com-research-can-saes-capture-neural-geometry`), which only an inspection of the matched line revealed - the same lesson as run 730's `model diffing` count. The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:1 core:0 idx_a:0 idx_c:0`) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the thirty-third consecutive run. **Discovery and tooling:** the `/abs/` page was fetched first (5,053 chars: five authors, DeepMind plus Oxford VGG plus Mila, CoLLAs 2022 oral, seven revisions, no code link in the text), then the **v7 HTML fetched clean on the first attempt**, 61,855 chars / 1,453 lines. - **Done:** arxiv-2602.10371 - Simple LLM Baselines are Competitive for Model Diffing (read full v1 HTML, 63,180 chars / 1,117 lines: abstract, §1-§5 including Limitations and Outlook, §3.1-§3.4 with Eq. 1-2, §4.1-§4.3, Table 1, Appendix A.1-A.4 (both pipelines and the judge protocol), Appendix B.1-B.4 with Eq. 3-4 and the 0.1/0.1 worked example, Appendix C.1-C.2 with Tables 2-4 in full, Appendix D (the 57-prompt and 513-prompt analyses and the judge-inconsistency case), Appendix E with Eq. 5 and Listings 5-7; Figures 1-10 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; reference list read in full)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's real instrument is an arithmetic identity that dismantles a composite score everyone had been quoting, and it is the folder's cheapest example of a score whose two factors must travel together - judge-verified frequency difference factors exactly as frequency times (twice accuracy minus one), so a hypothesis that fires on a tenth of inputs and is always right and one that fires on all inputs and is right 55% of the time both read 0.1.** Appendix B.1 derives it and then makes the point with those two hypotheses side by side. Read against run 723 (r90 at a fixed variance cut measuring the measurement), run 725 (rank_eps monotone in eps by construction) and run 731-to-come, the recurrence is that the folder keeps adopting scalars whose hidden denominator is a different quantity; here the authors show the hidden factor in closed form and then report both factors in every table (accuracy AND frequency, four columns per hypothesis). Mechanical rule for us: **before we publish any aggregate score for a probe, feature or explanation, we factor it into a rate of application and a rate of correctness and show the algebra that produced it - and where the product cannot be split, we say so - because a single number that multiplies how often a claim applies by how well it discriminates cannot be used to choose between a narrow true claim and a broad useless one, and the choice between those two is the entire design question for a white-box model that has to say when it applies.** Cross-reference run 722 (an invariance number whose absolute size said nothing and whose ratio held the finding), run 728 (a control curve that separated "reorganised" from "flattened" by computing the same statistic at three probe sets).
- **Pattern:** **The paper's own negative result is the strongest evidence in it, and the negative result is repaired not by a better method but by measuring whether the phenomenon was even expressible in the test set - which is the precondition check the folder has never once run.** Neither method recovers the trained-in gender assumption; instead of blaming the methods, Appendix D asks how many of the 1,000 WildChat prompts could elicit it, finds 5.7% (57 prompts rated at least 4 of 5 by an autorater), resamples 200 responses per model on those 57 and then repeats on 513 prompts drawn from 19,855, and shows the assumption rate sits below 7% in both models with no significant difference and the base model sometimes higher - so the null is about the prompt distribution, not the feature machinery, and the paper states that conclusion outright. Note the contrast with run 729 (a seed and depth sweep correlated on the configurations that survived), run 727 (a theory paper whose every empirical fact lives in a cited companion) and run 726 (a headline measured with a different instrument than the bound written for it): those papers report a relation without establishing that the data could have shown the opposite, and this one does. Mechanical rule for us: **before any probe or direction is declared a failure, we report the base rate of the target behaviour in our own evaluation set and the number of samples that could have expressed it - and if that count is small or the effect size is below what the sample can resolve, the result is recorded as uninformative rather than as a null - because a detector cannot be acquitted or convicted on a set where the signal appears five percent of the time, and an uninformative set reported as a null poisons every later meta-analysis of the tool.**
- **Pattern:** **The most confident finding of the feature-based arm was a token artefact, and the authors let it stand as the representative example of what that arm finds - the sharpest demonstration in the folder that a difference in representation is not yet a difference in behaviour.** The SAE pipeline described the Gemini revision as using "more Markdown table syntax, specifically the pipe and hyphen characters" at 95.7% accuracy and an abstraction rating of 1.33, while the LLM pipeline described the same change as "uses tables to present organized information" at 3.0 - same phenomenon, same data, two very different readings; and the KL fork-token method, which locates disagreement positions perfectly, produced LaTeX delimiter styles and heading markup as its showcase examples. The one behaviour only the language-level arm caught (a response-length collapse from 495.8 to 68.1 tokens on the misaligned Qwen) is confirmed by a character count, i.e. by an instrument outside both pipelines. Compare run 718 (a centroid attribution validated by recovering one known neuron on one prompt), run 723 (SAE latents correlating with markers while zeroing them barely moved accuracy), run 724 (an exact object with no diagnostic content): four entries now, and every one of them has a cheap non-mechanistic explanation available for its headline. Mechanical rule for us: **every feature, direction or probe we credit with a behavioural effect is compared against a stylometric or surface-statistic baseline computed directly on raw inputs and outputs, and the baseline is reported in the same table - because if counting punctuation reproduces the finding, the internal object is a rendering of the surface difference rather than a computation behind it, and a white-box claim that loses to a regex is not a white-box claim.**

- **Done:** arxiv-2502.09954 - On Space Folds of ReLU Neural Networks (read full v1 HTML, 68,631 chars / 926 lines: abstract, §1 Introduction, §2 Related work, §3 Preliminaries, §4 Convexity with Def. 1, Example 1, Example 2 and Lemma 1 with its full two-direction proof, §5 with the range measures, Eq. 1-5, Lemma 2 with proof, Algorithm 1 and the O(n(N+C)|C1||C2|) complexity bound and the N-step ceiling, §6.1-§6.3 including the Beyond MLPs remarks, §7 Future Work with the question/answer framing, §8 Conclusions, Appendix A (CantorNet generating function, decision manifold Eq. 7, the explicit W1/b1/W2/WL/bL weights, recursion Eq. 9-10), Appendix B (MAD definition Eq. 11, Algorithm 2, the seed disclosure), Appendix C (clustering sensitivity, 16 digit pairs, k in {1,2,5,10,20,50,100}), Appendix D (larger networks); Figures 1-9 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; **the paper contains no tables**, so every number in the entry comes from prose or a figure caption)
- **Pattern:** **The headline correlation is computed on the one seed out of five where the experiment worked, and the authors print the failure rate in the same paragraph - this is the folder's cleanest case of a depth correlation that is really a survivorship correlation.** Box 1 of `arxiv-2503.08502` (run 727) quoted this paper as the empirical authority for folding rising with depth and for folding tracking generalisation, and it turns out the support is a single Pearson 0.987 on seed 4, with seeds 3 and 4 the only ones of {0,1,2,3,4} to hit target accuracy and the 6- and 10-layer nets the ones that fall to about 0.85 - so the depth sweep is correlated with depth using only the depths that trained, on the only seeds that trained them. Read against run 722 (a defect magnitude that separated two groups retrospectively, with the author saying so), run 720 (an optimiser contrast confounded by loss level), and run 713 (numbers inherited from a survey): the recurring failure mode is not a wrong number but a number whose denominator is the subset that survived. Mechanical rule for us: **every correlation we publish across an architectural or training variable ships with the seed count attempted, the seed count that reached the criterion, and the swept values of both quantities - and where the criterion is itself accuracy we state the criterion - because a correlation over surviving seeds and surviving depths is a statement about which configurations trained, and the sweep variable and the survivor filter are not independent.** Cross-reference run 721 (a stated conclusion re-derived from its own table and found reversed), run 716 (a boundary chosen against the outcome it explains).
- **Pattern:** **The paper's one genuinely exact result is a theorem that confines folding to depth two or more, and its proof doubles as the cleanest re-derivation of why one hidden layer cannot separate XOR - geometry from a theorem rather than from a dataset.** Lemma 1 asserts convexity of a union of activation regions is equivalent to Hamming convexity of the bit patterns, and the forward direction is just the observation that crossing a whole-space hyperplane flips exactly one bit, so a straight segment is a shortest Hamming path; the reverse direction says a convex bit set forces the corresponding region union to be convex. The load-bearing hypothesis is *whole-space hyperplane* - first-layer neurons only - and the authors state the cost in the same section: deeper layers produce adjacent regions whose patterns differ by more than one bit, which is precisely what Example 2 exhibits on CantorNet. Compare run 727 (an extension whose lost structure the authors named in the same paragraph: the same discipline), run 718 (a framework whose five contributions included one theorem and four claims), run 724 (an exact re-description yielding zero measured numbers - the benign version of the same shape). Mechanical rule for us: **a theorem we lean on is cited together with its hypothesis, and where that hypothesis names a layer - first layer, whole-space hyperplane, single linear piece - we check before use whether the statistic we are computing lives in a deeper layer and say so in the entry, because the equivalence that justifies a walk statistic on layer one is exactly the one that fails by layer two, and a theorem's scope is part of its statement.** Cross-reference run 725 (a result valid only for the perturbation family it was proved for - bias noise), run 723 (a rank statistic whose threshold silently decided the reading).
- **Pattern:** **The measure is bounded and the bound is offered as its fitness for comparison, which is the second paper in three runs to sell a statistic on its invariance group rather than on its diagnostic value - and here the bound is proved while the diagnostic content is a hypothesis the authors label as such.** Lemma 2 gives 0 <= chi <= 1 for any path crossing more than one region, and the Discussion turns that into the programme's pitch: boundedness makes the score a reference point usable across models. What it is *not* shown to do is predict anything - the generalisation link is written as a hypothesis under an explicit "The question / The answer" heading, and the tests that would earn it (random labels in 10% steps, adversarial perturbations, augmentation, learning-rate and optimiser variation, neuron subsets) are listed in §7 as work for others. Read against run 727 (where the same group proved invariances before promising diagnostic value, and were honest that the claim was untested) and run 726 (a control that made a claim falsifiable), the arc's discipline is now clear and reusable. Mechanical rule for us: **we separate a statistic's range and invariances from its predictive content in separate sentences, we let boundedness license comparison across models and nothing else, and we never let a normalised score stand in for a mechanism - because [0,1] is a scale, not a finding, and the denominator that produces it (total Hamming distance walked) moves whenever the walk's step count or step rule moves.** Cross-reference run 722 (a residual fraction whose invariance followed from dimension, not dynamics), run 723 (rho_exec/rho_rand, where the ratio was the finding and the invariance was noise).
