- **Done:** arxiv-2310.12977 - Training Dynamics of Deep Network Linear Regions (read full v1 HTML, 43,010 chars / 689 lines: abstract, §1 Introduction, §2 Background, §3.1-§3.3 with the three-step LC algorithm, §4.1 setup, §4.2 depth and width sweeps, §4.3 batch norm and weight decay, §4.4 grokking and the two dataset-size sweeps, §5 Conclusion, Appendix A, Appendix B Figures 12-14 captions; Figures 1-14 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; the reference list read in full; **the paper has no tables**, so every quantitative statement in the entry comes from prose or a figure caption)
- **Pattern:** **The cheapest partition statistic in this folder's reach is a hyperplane-cut count over a sampled hull, and this paper is where the random-probe control was introduced - which makes it the origin of the control that runs 723-727 each re-derived independently.** Around a probe point the authors draw P orthonormal offsets, push the hull's vertices through in one forward pass, and count per layer how many pre-activations change sign across the vertices; no labels, no loss, no task, and it reproduces the doubly-descending curve that an exact region-count method (SplineCAM) gives on the same MLP. Read against run 726 (the three-probe-set rule credited to a 2024 paper) and run 725 (bias-perturbed boundary density averaged over the data distribution), the lineage is now visible: the random-point curve is not an innovation of the later papers, it is already here, plotted as a third colour beside train and test and doing exactly the job it does later - separating "the partition reorganised near the data" from "the partition changed everywhere", since the train-side ascent does not appear at random points. Mechanical rule for us: **when we adopt a control from a paper we cite the paper that introduced it rather than the one that restated it, and any locality claim we make is accompanied by the random-point version of the same statistic computed on the same checkpoints - because the random curve is what turns "the model uses dense regions near the data" into a falsifiable statement about where the boundaries are.** Cross-reference run 726 (LC at data, shifted and random probes), run 727 (invariance group stated before a statistic is used).
- **Pattern:** **The same manipulation moves the phenomenon in opposite directions across two architectures inside this one paper, and the authors report both - so the trajectory is the finding and the direction of every effect is a per-setting fact.** Depth reduces the ascent peak and the train-test LC gap on MNIST MLPs, but under induced grokking depth raises the ascent peak and delays generalisation; on CIFAR10 CNNs depth raises the ascent peak and train-test LC are nearly indistinguishable throughout, where on MNIST the ascent is almost entirely a training-set effect. Width is non-monotone even within one setting: climbing 20 to 1000 starts the ascent earlier and higher, and 2000 lowers the peak again. Two regularisers that both "remove the final descent" do so with opposite side effects - batch norm raises LC on train, test and random points, weight decay lowers the ascent peak and shortens it. This is the honest shape of a phenomenon paper and it is citable precisely because the disagreement is printed: the three-phase shape survives every sweep while every specific direction flips at least once. Mechanical rule for us: **we distinguish, in our own write-up and in every entry we cite, a shape claim from a direction claim - a shape that survives N settings is evidence, a direction observed in one setting is a hypothesis - and where an architecture family changes the direction we report the two families side by side rather than averaging them, because a mechanism whose sign depends on the architecture is a mechanism that depends on something not yet named.** Cross-reference run 722 (three spectral objects in one paper telling three different stories), run 721 (six prose/table disagreements in one paper), run 727 (a score whose invariance group decided what it could be compared with).
- **Pattern:** **The migration story is carried by pictures plus a handful of slice counts, and the paper's own conclusion concedes there is no theory - which places the whole arc's central object on the demonstration side of the line, again.** Exact partition visualisations on 2-D slices through three training samples show boundaries crowding onto training points during the ascent and moving to the decision boundary during the second descent; the quantitative support is the LC curve and exact counts on select slices, never a boundary-distance distribution over the input distribution. The Conclusion states that the phases remain unexplained and that the work rebuts earlier stationary-partition assumptions without offering a replacement model. Compare run 724 (an exact re-description that measured nothing), run 726 (region counts on four selected slices standing in for a distribution), run 723 (four of five contributions claimed rather than measured): four papers in this arc now describe the same migration and none measures where the boundaries go as a distribution. Mechanical rule for us: **a geometric mechanism we claim is carried by a distribution-level statistic - mean distance from probe points to the nearest region boundary, or the share of sampled points whose neighbourhood has any boundary at all - measured at every checkpoint, so that "boundaries migrated" is a number that could have come out flat; slice pictures are illustration, and an entry that cites migration without such a statistic says so.** Cross-reference run 725 (boundary density made local and data-weighted - the closest existing instrument), run 724 (exactness is not diagnostic evidence).

Gate re-confirmed FREE before the fetch: `/tmp/lit/gate.sh 2310.12977 2503.08502` -> `2310.12977 aether:0 core:0 idx_a:0 idx_c:0` (and `2503.08502` now correctly at `aether:1 idx_a:1`, the run-727 entry, which is the positive control that the gate reads). A lowercased title-substring count of `training dynamics of deep network linear regions` on both INDEX bodies returned **0 / 0**. The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:1 core:0 idx_a:1 idx_c:0`) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the thirtieth consecutive run. **Discovery and tooling:** `/tmp/lit/sl.py` and `/tmp/lit/gate.sh` were both written fresh at the start of the run per the standing rule, and the INDEX append ran as a script file (`/tmp/lit/app12977.py`), never `python3 -c`. The **`/abs/` page** was fetched first (5,039 chars: three authors, Rice plus Balestriero independent, v1 only 2023-10-19, 14 pages / 14 figures, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, **no journal reference, no venue - this is a 2023 preprint never matched to a conference version in the metadata**, and it states the code will be released after review, which it is not); then the **v1 HTML fetched clean on the first attempt**, 43,010 chars / 689 lines. **Rendering quirks:** inline maths arrives **doubled** in the now-familiar pattern (`P P`, `25 25`, `10 3 10^{-3}`), so every number was read as the first of its pair; display equations arrive twice (flattened then raw LaTeX); **there are no tables at all**, so no header-column cross-check was possible and every number in the entry traces to a prose sentence or a figure caption; **Appendix A is empty in this build** (heading, then straight to Appendix B), so nothing is lost but nothing is there; **the References section is complete and is the source for two facts the entry uses** - the SplineCAM validation target (Humayun et al. 2023, CVPR) and the batch-norm boundary-position result the paper cites rather than proves (Balestriero & Baraniuk 2022, "Batch normalization explained", arXiv 2209.14778), which is named as a citation in the entry and not as a measurement of this paper.

- **Done:** arxiv-2503.08502 - The Space Between: On Folding, Symmetries and Sampling (read full v1 HTML, 40,452 chars / 616 lines: abstract, §1 Introduction, §2 Related Work, §3 Preliminaries, §4.1 Construction with Eq. 1-3, §4.2 Properties with Props. 4.1/4.2 in full including both additivity counterexamples (Eq. 4 and Eq. 5) and the interaction term Eq. 6, §4.3 Remark 4.3 and Cor. 4.4, §4.4 global measure Eq. 7-8 and the Mann-Whitney footnote, §4.5 Def. 4.5 and the extension argument, §5 with **Algorithm 1 in full** (lines 1-21) plus the complexity paragraph, §6 the lambda/(Phi+1)^2 loss and the log-sum-exp surrogate, §7 Final Remarks with all three future-work items, the reference list read in full; Figures 1-3 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; **the paper contains no tables and no experimental section at all**, so there is not a single measured number in it)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's real instrument is not the folding score - it is the order in which it checks that score: invariances first, diagnostic value never - and that order is the cheapest control in the folder's reach.** Before anyone is invited to use chi, they prove four things about it: extra samples inside one region do not move it (Prop. 4.1), so it depends on which regions a walk visits and not on the sampling density; chi = 0 has an exact iff-characterisation (Prop. 4.2); the score is direction-sensitive with a three-bit counterexample (Remark 4.3) while flatness is not (Cor. 4.4); and the score is neither sub- nor super-additive (Eq. 4, Eq. 5). Read against the folder's standing complaint - run 722's projection fraction whose absolute value was tiny and whose finding lived entirely in the ratio, run 723's r90 at one variance cut with no spectrum, run 725's rank_eps monotone in eps by construction - this is the same discipline applied one step earlier: those papers established what their numbers were *relative to* only after reporting them. A scalar does not get to be an explanation until its invariance group is known, because an invariant quantity is comparable across models and a non-invariant one is comparable only across the samples that produced it. Mechanical rule for us: **for every statistic we publish in the white-box model we state its invariance group explicitly - under repeated sampling inside one region, under reversal of the path or order, under decomposition into sub-paths - and where we cannot prove invariance we measure it on our own data and report the spread, because a statistic is only a comparison device and a statistic whose invariances are unknown cannot be compared with anything.** Cross-reference run 724 (an exact re-description whose authors measured nothing - the same shape, differently resolved), run 719 (a correction term that loses to the simple score at the window sizes practitioners use - an invariance question asked about a correction rather than about a base statistic).
- **Pattern:** **The extension beyond ReLU is argued from a definition rather than tested, and the argument is the part worth keeping: a linear region is nothing but an equivalence class of inputs with identical binarised activations, so the partition survives any monotone activation even after it stops being a partition into affine pieces.** Def. 4.5 defines x ~ y iff their binarised patterns are Hamming-identical, checks the three equivalence axioms in three sentences, and then notes the honest cost of the move in the same breath: "we lose the geometrical interpretation of equivalence classes as linear regions." What survives is exactly what the folding measure needs - a discrete walk through a set of classes with distances between them - and what is lost is every geometric claim made about those classes, so the beta-parameter and the region-count literature silently do not transfer. Compare run 723 (five contributions, four unmeasured), run 721 (six prose/table disagreements): this paper is the clean case where the authors state what the generalisation costs them instead of carrying the old vocabulary across. Mechanical rule for us: **when we extend an object to a wider class of models we name, in the same paragraph, the structure that the extension does not preserve - because a definition that survives a generalisation is not the same object, and an invariant we computed on the narrower class is not automatically an invariant of the wider one.**
- **Pattern:** **Every empirical fact in this paper is inherited from a companion it cites without an identifier, and the paper says so plainly - which makes it the folder's clearest example of a theory paper whose theory rests on someone else's measurement.** The depth-dependence, the low-error/high-folding association and the near-invariance of folding to width (60- vs 600-neuron nets at fixed depth) all trace to "On Space Folds of ReLU Neural Networks" (TMLR 2025), cited by author-year with no arXiv id; the only experiment run here is a Mann-Whitney comparison of intra- vs inter-class medians, reported as significant with the threshold deferred to Cohen's conventions and no number given. The regulariser proposed in §6 is described as "untested" in §7. This is the benign version of run 726's shape (a paper whose headline was measured with a different instrument than the theory described): here the authors segregate what they proved from what they borrowed. Mechanical rule for us: **before quoting a paper's empirical claim we trace it one hop - if the number lives in a cited companion, the citation is to the companion and the entry says "reported in <paper>, not re-measured here" - because a two-hop citation chain is where the instrument, the sample size and the caveats all quietly drop out.** Cross-reference run 713 (numbers inherited from a survey and never re-measured), run 726 (a drop measured with a different instrument than the bound written for it).

- **Pattern:** **The control that makes this paper's headline falsifiable is a curve nobody else in the folder plots - local complexity probed at uniformly random points - and it is the reason a "complexity dropped" claim here cannot be confused with a global decay.** During the second descent, LC around training, test *and* random points all fall; if only the data curves moved, the paper's story would be indistinguishable from the network simply smoothing everywhere, which is exactly the failure mode that makes run 723's r90 and run 722's rho≈1.000 unreadable. The random-probe curve forces the claim into its real form: boundaries leave the data neighbourhoods and concentrate somewhere the random probes do not sample, which is then verified on the slice. Note the cost - 1,000 ResNet18 probes in about 28 s on an RTX 8000 - so this control is cheap enough to be mandatory rather than aspirational. Mechanical rule for us: **every locality statistic we report (boundary density, region count, curvature, rank) is reported at three probe sets - the data, a distribution-shifted set, and uniformly random points - because a statistic computed only at the data cannot distinguish "the model reorganised" from "the model flattened", and the random set is the null that separates them.** Cross-reference run 725 (bias-perturbed boundary density weighted by the data distribution), run 722 (a residual fraction whose only informative form was its ratio to a random basis), run 714 (a spread statistic whose value was set by the random-initialisation baseline).
- **Pattern:** **The paper's own diagnostic for whether its measure is valid is the most portable instrument in it, and it is a test of the neighbourhood rather than of the network: push the probe polytope through the layers and measure how much its shape deforms.** Graph eccentricity and diameter of the polytope rise roughly exponentially with depth for larger radii and stay flat below about 0.014 on CIFAR10 and for the ResNet18, which is why the radii used are 0.005 and 1e-4 rather than something more local; and even then the authors report that the LC *dynamics* are similar across large and small radii, so the qualitative curve survives the approximation even where the absolute value does not. This is the first entry in the folder that pays for a hyperparameter with a measurement instead of asserting it, and the same operation is what ordinary probing lacks: a linear probe never checks whether the representation neighbourhood it is reading still corresponds to an input neighbourhood. Mechanical rule for us: **any statistic computed on a pushed-forward neighbourhood (a polytope, a ball, a patch of activation space) ships with the deformation of that neighbourhood measured at the depth it was taken, and the radius is chosen as the largest one where deformation is still flat - because a local statistic taken through a deep network is a statement about an object that may no longer be local by the time you read it.**
- **Pattern:** **Region counting and circuit counting are the same measurement here, and the paper says the identity out loud - a region is one active-neuron configuration, adjacent regions differ by one neuron, so boundary density around a point *is* circuit-transition density around that point.** That single sentence collapses two literatures this folder has been reading separately (the spline/partition line from runs 718, 723-725 and the mechanistic circuit line), and it comes with the empirical claim that circuits are exhausted rather than accumulated late in training: the partition coarsens around data and concentrates on the boundary, so the number of distinct subgraphs traversed in a data locality collapses, most sharply in the deeper layers. The load-bearing caveat is that the paper's own theory stops before this: it offers no account of why the migration happens only after interpolation, and the association between migration and robustness is never tested by moving boundaries artificially. Mechanical rule for us: **when we write that a feature or circuit "is used", we state the measure as a density over an input locality and report how that density changes over training, and we treat a dense-region count and a circuit count as one statistic rather than two corroborations - because counting regions and counting circuits from the same partition is one measurement, and two names for one number is not evidence.**

- **Done:** arxiv-2412.18283 - On the Local Complexity of Linear Regions in Deep ReLU Networks (read full v3 HTML, 134,483 chars / 2,591 lines: abstract, §1-§2, §3 with Def. 1 / Thm. 2 / Cor. 3, §4 with Def. 4 / Thm. 5, §5 with Prop. 6 / Thm. 7, §6 with Prop. 8 / Prop. 9 / Cor. 10, §7 including the complete Limitations and future research section, the reference list in full, Appendix A.1-A.12 (notation, the single-neuron and two-hidden-layer worked computations, every proof), Appendix B.1-B.4 (estimation, sigma ablation, init-scale ablation, bound tightness), Appendix C.1-C.4; Figures 1-14 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; **the paper contains no tables at all**, so every number in the entry comes from prose or a figure caption)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's real contribution is a definition that makes the partition object answerable to the data distribution, and the trick that buys it is perturbing biases only - which is the missing half of every partition statistic this folder has been computing.** Counting regions at fixed weights needs a parametric solve; averaging the boundary density over Gaussian *bias* noise needs only a co-area formula, and bias noise translates boundaries without rotating them, so the averaged object is still the same geometry (their sigma ablation at 0.025/0.05/0.1 and the bias-vs-bias+weight comparison in Appendix B.1 both leave the picture qualitatively unchanged). Theorem 2 then factorises that density into a per-neuron term, ‖∇z_i(x)‖ × ρ_b(z_i(x)) × 1{z_i good at x}, i.e. gradient norm times a threshold-crossing weight times a liveness indicator. Read against run 724 (the radius dropped from the power-diagram object, so a centroid can name a tile the input does not occupy) and run 723 (a rank at a fixed 90% variance cut measuring the measurement), this is the third consecutive run arriving at the same structural point from a different direction: a geometric quantity computed in ambient space is not a statement about the network until it is weighted by where the data is. Mechanical rule for us: **every partition, region, curvature or boundary statistic we report is averaged over a data distribution (or an explicit input sample) rather than over input space, is computed under a perturbation that moves boundaries without re-orienting them (bias noise), and is reported per neuron as gradient-norm times threshold-crossing so the contribution can be attributed - because a region count in ℝ^D upper-bounds nothing about behaviour, and a boundary density near the data is the only version of the object that a mechanism could act through.**
- **Pattern:** **The one place the paper's own bound fails, it diagnoses the failure by measuring a constant inside the bound instead of softening the claim - and calls the result unexpected rather than rescued; this is the template the folder should copy for every relation it publishes.** Total variation and local complexity drop together on MNIST at 2× He initialization, which is the setting they chose; at standard He initialization the correlation "seems to break down", and at 3× the picture changes again (Appendix B.3, Figure 8). Rather than hedge, they estimate the bound's own Lipschitz term as the max over layers of the suffix matrix-product operator norm, show the corrected lower bound resumes tracking LC across 88 repeated runs (Figure 9), and then state that the late-training TV *spike* is attributable to growing Lipschitz constants and "merits further investigation" - an inconclusive diagnosis reported as inconclusive. Note against run 721 (six prose/table disagreements where the reversed ordering supported the thesis) and run 722 (an invariance number the author himself called nearly contentless): those authors reported the doubtful number; this one reported the ablation that broke it. Mechanical rule for us: **when a predicted relation fails under our own ablation, we do not drop the ablation from the write-up - we estimate the constant that could absorb the discrepancy, re-test with that estimate, and state plainly whether the estimate rescued the bound or whether the residual is unexplained; because a bound with unmeasured constants is untestable in the regime where it matters, and the only honest outcome is a named residual.**
- **Pattern:** **Local rank is defined here as `rank_ε` - a count of singular values above a threshold - and the theory's relation to local complexity runs through that ε twice, which is an independent theoretical confirmation of the folder's empirical complaint about r90.** Theorem 5 bounds LC above by Σ_l √(C²·LR^ε_l + ε²n_l) and bounds Σ_l LR^ε_l above by (LC + error terms)/(c·ε²); both directions carry the threshold explicitly, and the exact-rank corollary only survives in the limit ε→0 through a weaker √LR form. Also worth holding: the paper's own Figure 12 shows local-rank dynamics on MNIST can be considerably more complex than on the synthetic task, and their Figure 2 correlation numbers (0.852 / 0.957 / 0.985 at layers 2/3/4) come from a 100-dimensional Gaussian-to-Gaussian regression with a randomly drawn cross-covariance, i.e. from the one setting where a low-rank target is known to exist. Read against run 723 (r90 at a fixed 90% variance cut with no null and no spectrum) and run 719 (a null the same paper had already shown to be wrong by orders of magnitude), this closes the loop from the theory side. Mechanical rule for us: **when we quote a rank we state ε (or the variance cut) and the spectrum it was cut from, we report the rank at two thresholds, and we never present a rank correlation obtained on a synthetic low-rank target as evidence about the rank of a trained network's features - because rank_ε is monotone in ε by definition, and a correlation between two thresholded quantities is partly a correlation between two thresholds.**

(5) **What the head wants next**: three consecutive runs (723, 724, 725) have now landed on "weight the geometric statistic by the data distribution", which suggests the folder's remaining gap is not more partition theory but an **experiment**: compute local complexity and local rank for a trained model, and check on held-out data whether they predict robustness or behaviour better than a matched-random geometric statistic of the same dimension. That is a design note for our own paper, not a read, and it should be carried forward as such.

- **Pattern:** **The radius is the half of the power-diagram object that decides whether a centroid is even inside the region it names, and it is dropped by everyone - starting here, in the paper that defines the object.** Lemma 3 says changing a cell's radius shrinks or expands it relative to its neighbours, and this paper's own Figure 4 caption states that as depth increases the radius overtakes the centroid and pushes it away from the region. So the centroid (the object this folder has measured since run 714, and the object the 2026 Linear Centroids Hypothesis is built on) is a two-parameter object and only one parameter is ever reported. Note the provenance: the "radii left unstudied" limitation that arxiv-2604.11962 names as its own is 2019-original, inherited from this paper rather than introduced later, which means the folder's whole centroid line inherits a caveat none of its recent entries priced in. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever we report a centroid, a linear-region direction or a tile-centre statistic, we report the radius or radius proxy beside it and state whether the centroid lies inside its own cell at the depth we took it - because a centroid that has been pushed outside its region by accumulated depth is a direction pointed at a tile the input may not occupy, and a direction that names the wrong tile is the exact failure mode the folder keeps re-discovering under other names.** Cross-reference run 718 (a centroid-vs-latent dissociation demonstrated in kind, not magnitude), run 722 (a residual fraction that is a statement about dimension, not dynamics), run 719 (the basis is not the subspace - here the centre is not the cell).
- **Pattern:** **This is the origin of the one formula the whole centroid line runs on, and the formula's own paper is honest that it returns a saliency map - which is the least faithful attribution the folder holds, offered as the definition rather than as a validated feature.** Proposition 3 and eq. 20 give the composed region's centroid as the composed Jacobian transpose times the all-ones vector, computable in one backward pass with no region enumeration, and the paper says outright that at a given layer this is the sum of the per-unit saliency maps. That claim is load-bearing twice: it is why the centroid is cheap, and it is why a centroid direction can be expected to inherit the well-documented unreliability of gradient saliency - which the later entries in this folder then treat as a validated representation. Read against run 717 (the strongest parameter-space direction turned out to be the instantaneous gradient, with the whole 648x coupling ratio an artefact of the degenerate estimator) and run 718 (a centroid attribution score validated by recovering one known neuron on one prompt), the folder now has three independent arrivals at the same place: every cheap interpretability object is a Jacobian product, and Jacobian products are exactly the objects that need a matched-random control. Mechanical rule for us: **any white-box quantity we compute as a single Jacobian-vector product is described as a saliency-type quantity until it has beaten a matched-random direction on a held-out set, and the fact that it is cheap is recorded as a reason to run the control more often, not as a reason to trust it - because a definition being exact does not make the definition's value diagnostic, and this paper's exactness is geometric while its use downstream is statistical.** Cross-reference run 715 (a circuit necessary in one model, dispensable across a family), run 704 (a forward-computable path-product bound).
- **Pattern:** **The paper re-describes the entire network exactly and completely, obtains zero measured numbers from the re-description, and still supplies the folder's cleanest argument for organising a white-box model as a partition - which is the distinction between a model being exact and a model being useful that we should keep permanent.** Theorems 5 and 6 give the subdivision parameters and the feedforward cost, the decision boundary is a level set of a product of pre-activations with a dihedral-angle curvature, and the consequences recovered are conceptual (regions cannot be edited in isolation = weight tying; curvature is invariant to parameter rescaling, so gradient norm alone cannot characterise boundary regularity; ReLU curvature is larger where fewer ReLUs fire). Meanwhile the empirics are figures without quantities: no model names, sizes, seed counts or realised region counts for the two prose claims, and the non-CIFAR network is described only in an appendix cross-reference the submitted text leaves as "appendix ??". Note against run 721 (a paper whose six prose/table disagreements were all load-bearing) and run 723 (a framework whose five contributions included four never measured): this is the benign version of the same shape, because it does not claim to have measured anything. Mechanical rule for us: **we separate, in every entry and in our own write-up, "the object is exactly defined" from "the object has been shown to be diagnostic", and we cite an exact re-description as a design commitment and never as evidence - because an exact description of a network as a partition is available for every network ever trained and therefore distinguishes none of them, and the diagnostic content has to be earned by a comparison that this class of paper structurally cannot supply.** Cross-reference run 723 (four of five contributions claimed, not measured), run 718 (the surrogate graded against itself), run 716 (a boundary selected against the outcome it explains).

- **Done:** arxiv-2602.10496 - Low-Dimensional Execution Manifolds in Transformer Learning Dynamics: Evidence from Modular Arithmetic Tasks (read full text v2 HTML, 39,235 chars / 470 lines: abstract, §1-§6 including all of §5.3 Limitations and Future Directions, §3.1-§3.4, §4.1-§4.6, all six figure captions; Figures 1-6 read from captions and the reporting prose only, not inspected as images; **the paper contains no tables at all**, so every number in the entry comes from prose or a figure caption; reference list read only as far as the works cited in §2 and the acknowledgments)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's headline number - a collapse to a 3-4 dimensional execution manifold - has no null, no spectrum and no table behind it, and is measured at a fixed 90% variance cut that determines the answer arithmetically; this is the cleanest case in the folder of a rank statistic that measures the measurement rather than the computation.** d^2 = 16,384 parameters per attention matrix and an effective rank r90 of 3-4 within the first 20-30% of training sounds like severe dimensional collapse, but r90 at a fixed 90% threshold is a monotone function of how much variance the leading components already hold and of how many checkpoints the PCA ran on, and the paper reports neither the eigenvalue spectrum nor the checkpoint count nor the same statistic for an untrained model or a random walk of matched length. Note this against run 721 (PC1 at 70-90% of trajectory variance turned out to describe the scaffold, not the solution) and run 714 (participation ratio near 60 at random initialisation collapsing to 2-30 - the same statistic being high by default): here the number is reported alone, so a reader cannot tell which regime it is in. Mechanical rule for us: **every \"the model computes in k dimensions\" claim ships with the eigenvalue spectrum, the number of snapshots the PCA used, the same statistic computed on an untrained model and on a random walk of matched length, and the r90 value at a second threshold (say 80% and 95%) - because a rank quoted at one variance cut is a statement about the tail of a spectrum, and the spectrum is what a mechanism could live in.** Cross-reference run 716 (a boundary selected as the argmax of a ratio over a candidate family), run 719 (a null that the same paper had already shown to be wrong by orders of magnitude).
- **Pattern:** **The one genuinely comparative result is also the one the author states correctly - a raw projection fraction is meaningless without a rank-matched random denominator, and the finding lives in the ratio's decay rather than its peak - which makes this the earliest member of the arc that runs 717 and 722 later completed, and the student paper gets the framing right where the later ones got the arithmetic wrong.** rho_exec is 0.02-0.13 throughout, i.e. roughly 2-13% of commutator energy inside the execution subspace; the paper says outright that any K-subspace of a P-dimensional space captures about sqrt(K/P) of a generic vector, so the fraction proves nothing on its own, and reports rho_exec/rho_rand instead: 9.7x median in the first 20% of training decaying to 2.1x in the last 20%, with one panel peaking near 14x. Now compare run 722, the same author's later paper, where the same projection quantity is reported as an invariance reading rho near 1.000 with the ratio demoted to a footnote - and where the 1.8-2.9x ratio (not the 1.000) was the only informative number. Here, in the earlier paper, the ratio is the headline and the invariance is not claimed. Mechanical rule for us: **when a projection or coupling fraction appears in a paper, we record the raw fraction, the random-baseline fraction, their ratio, and the point in training at which each was taken - and we read the decay curve as the finding, because a ratio that starts near 10x and ends near 2x is describing non-commutativity leaving a subspace, whereas a peak ratio alone is describing one moment.** Cross-reference run 717 (a 648x coupling ratio decaying to 140 as the estimator was made less degenerate), run 722 (the invariance number the author himself calls nearly contentless).
- **Pattern:** **Three of the paper's five contributions are asserted, inherited or demonstrated only in prose, and the two negative results are more informative than the positive ones - the folder's standing reminder that a framework paper's value is in its falsifiable residue, not its headline.** The MLP result is a single sentence: standard transformers show \"higher-dimensional training dynamics, with no clear collapse\" and worse generalisation, with no rank values, no seeds, no number at all. The curriculum result is likewise prose-only (mixed training generalises, progressive curriculum forgets) and neither is tabulated, despite the paper having 20 seeds. The modular-multiplication extension gives 4-6 versus 3-4 dimensions with no table and is explicitly labelled preliminary. The SAE result is the one clean negative: 3-5 latents of several hundred correlate with marker count or marked positions and zeroing them barely moves accuracy at any of three training stages. Read against run 718 (a centroid-vs-latent dissociation demonstrated in kind, not in magnitude, in a left-hand figure panel), this is the third entry in a row where the decisive comparison exists as a picture or a sentence rather than a number. Mechanical rule for us: **we do not adopt an architectural or curriculum claim from a paper that reports it without a rank value, a seed count and a baseline - we note it as \"claimed, not measured\" and design the measurement ourselves, because a framework that unifies five phenomena on one measured number and four unmeasured ones is a hypothesis with a worked example, and the unmeasured four are exactly the ones readers will quote.** Cross-reference run 713 (numbers inherited from a survey and never re-measured), run 715 (a circuit necessary in one seed, inert in another, only visible because both were reported).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The top carried candidate from run 722 - `arxiv-2602.10496` (Xu 2026a, \"Low-dimensional execution manifolds\") - was **free**, with all four checks printed before any fetch: `2602.10496 aether:0 core:0`, `ls docs/literature/aether/arxiv-2602.10496.md` -> no such file, and a lowercased title-substring count of \"execution manifold\" on both INDEX bodies returned 0/0. The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:0 core:0` -> `aether:1 core:0`) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the twenty-fifth consecutive run. This was the right read and it closes the provenance question run 722 opened: this is the programme's founding paper, the one that first reports the PCA-of-trajectory machinery, the random-subspace baseline and the commutator defect, and reading it after 717/719/720/721/722 shows that the random-baseline discipline was present from the start and *weakened* in the later papers rather than being added by a correction. **Discovery and tooling:** the `/abs/` page was fetched first (5,254 clean chars: single author, gmail contact, 15 pages / 6 figures, v1 2026-02-11 / v2 2026-02-13, CC BY 4.0, full abstract, code URL in the PDF body), then the **v2 HTML fetched clean on the first attempt** - 154,915 raw bytes, **39,235 chars** after tag strip, **470 lines**, by far the shortest document in this arc. `/tmp/lit/sl.py` was written fresh at the start of the run per the standing rule and every slice went through it; the heading map was one `grep -nE '^ ?[0-9]+(\.[0-9]+)? +[A-Z]'` pass. **Rendering quirks:** inline maths arrives **doubled** in the familiar pattern (`d = 128 d=128`, `3 3`-`4 4`, `> 92 % >92\%`) so every number was read as the first of its pair; **display equations arrive twice**; and there are **no tables in this paper at all**, which is itself the most notable rendering fact of the run - the 9.7x/2.1x ratios, the 92%/98% perpendicular fractions and the 3-4/4-6 dimension comparison exist only in prose and figure captions, so they could not be cross-checked against a header column order the way runs 719-722 all did.

- **Done:** arxiv-2602.16746 - Low-Dimensional and Transversely Curved Optimization Dynamics in Grokking (read full text v3 HTML, 86,506 chars / 1,250 lines: abstract, §1-§8 including §6.7 Limitations and Open Problems and the Reproducibility note, §2.1-§2.3, §3.1-§3.5, §4.1-§4.7.4, §5.1-§5.5, Tables 1-3 reconstructed from header column order and cross-checked against prose restatements, appendix Figure 22-26 captions; Figures 1-26 read from captions and reporting prose, not inspected as images; reference list read only as far as resolving works cited in §§1, 4, 5 and 6)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's headline invariance number is arithmetically almost contentless, and the author is the one who says so - which makes this the folder's cleanest worked example of why a residual fraction in a huge space needs a matched-random denominator before it means anything.** rho ≈ 1.000 "within numerical precision" across all 36 conditions sounds like a strong claim that curvature never deflects the trajectory; in fact both the execution-basis and the random-basis projections of the commutator are under 0.05 of the vector's norm, because a 24-dimensional subspace of 290,000 dimensions captures almost nothing from a generic vector, and the invariance follows from the arithmetic rather than from the geometry. The only informative statistic is the ratio between them - 1.8-2.9x in favour of the learned basis, stable when K is moved to 16 and 32 - and even that ratio is measured on the small parallel component. Note this against runs 717, 721 and 714, each of which reported a projection or variance-share number whose absolute value was tiny and where the finding lived entirely in a comparison: this paper is the version where the author states the reasoning explicitly. Mechanical rule for us: **any projection, variance-share or residual fraction we report ships with the matched-random denominator *and* the absolute share printed beside it, and when the absolute share is below a few percent the sentence is written about the ratio and never about the invariance - because in a parameter space of hundreds of thousands of dimensions every low-dimensional subspace is approximately orthogonal to everything, and \"the trajectory stayed inside its subspace\" can be a statement about dimension rather than about dynamics.** Cross-reference run 717 (a coupling ratio that decayed 648x as the estimator was made less degenerate - the same percentage-of-a-big-space problem met from the other side), run 721 (PC1's 70-90% share describing the scaffold, not the solution), run 714 (a spread statistic whose value was set by the random-initialisation baseline).
- **Pattern:** **Necessity and sufficiency came out asymmetric in a way that should be the default expectation for direction-level interventions: suppressing the component along a learned direction damaged behaviour with a clean monotone dose-response, while injecting perturbation along the curvature direction at up to 500x the gradient step norm did nothing at all.** The suppression curve is the good kind of causal evidence - 20-25% delay at strength 0.75 in all four operations, 0/12 successes at 1.0, and a rank-matched random projection that is inert at intermediate strengths - and the kick experiment is a true null: 27/27 runs generalise at the same step whether the kicks are commutator-aligned or random-orthogonal. The author reads this correctly as curvature being a *signature* of a barrier rather than a manipulable cause. Two bounds have to travel with the result and one is easy to miss: at full strength the random projection also kills grokking, so specificity is established only in the partial-strength regime where the intervention is a nudge rather than a cage. Mechanical rule for us: **we separately report whether removing a direction hurts and whether adding to it helps, and we treat \"removal hurts, addition is inert\" as the signature of a route the model must travel rather than a variable of the mechanism - and any specificity claim from a graded intervention states the strength range over which the control is inert, because at the hard end of the sweep every subspace is fatal and the comparison collapses.** Cross-reference run 721 (subspace-deletion dose-response with a non-monotone recovery point at ten times the dose), run 715 (a control matched on the axis of the claim), run 717 (a diagnostic subspace that matched a random one at its own rank).
- **Pattern:** **The one number in the paper that separates the two groups never appears alone at the same strength in both groups, and three different spectral objects in the same paper tell three different stories - which is the folder's most compact illustration that \"the leading structure\" is not a stable object across a decomposition.** Defect magnitude reaches 500-2000x baseline for the grokking operations against 30-50x for the two that never generalise, with zero overlap in totals but complete overlap early in training, so the separation is retrospective - the author says so plainly and refuses to sell it as a predictor. Meanwhile the update-Gram gap ratio is *higher* in memorising runs (2.83 against 1.40), the weight-SVD sub-leading gap declines 15-111x in 12/12 grokking runs against 1/12 controls, and the displacement-PCA PC1 that the whole manifold story rests on is quoted as 68-83% in §4.1 and as 70-94% in §5.4 for the same quantity. The author's explanation for the inversion - memorisation gradients are themselves strongly rank-1 - is plausible and untested. Mechanical rule for us: **before calling any direction \"the leading one\" we name which matrix it is leading in (updates, displacements, or the learned operator) and report the other two, because the same model at the same step ranks them differently and the one that matters to a mechanism is not the one that dominates the spectrum; and a quantity quoted at two different values inside one paper is recorded with both.** Cross-reference run 720 (a drift multiplier given as 2,000x and 1,300x against identical endpoints), run 721 (six prose/table disagreements in one 42-page paper), run 716 (a boundary selected against the outcome it explains, at a different scale).

- **Pattern:** **The scaffold-solution split is the folder's clearest statement that a variance-ordered decomposition ranks the thing you do not want first - and it makes every PCA/SVD summary we have been quoting suspect by construction.** PC1's share of trajectory variance is 70-90% in these runs and reconstruction with the leading components alone is at chance; the capability appears only once k* directions (5-10 dual-task, 4-6 tri-task) are included, and the accuracy flips from chance at k*-1 to 99%+ at k* with nothing in between. The author's reading is that the dominant direction is the memorisation path, not the destination. Note this against runs 714 and 717-720, all of which read a dominant trajectory direction or a top-PC concentration as a substantive finding: here the same statistic is measured against a functional criterion and turns out to be a description of the scaffold. Mechanical rule for us: **no rank, concentration or cumulative-variance figure is ever reported alone - the rank we report is the smallest k at which a *reconstruction or behavioural test* passes, and PC1's share is printed beside it as the scaffold fraction, because a decomposition ordered by variance places the path first and the solution last, and only a functional criterion can tell which is which.** Cross-reference run 721's own §10.2 ("variance explained is not a reliable proxy for functional relevance"), run 714 (participation ratio is high at random initialisation and collapses to a specialism - the same measurement inverted), run 720 (drift direction integrated by momentum is 25x better aligned with accumulated updates than with any single gradient - an ordering that depends on which object you sum).
- **Pattern:** **This is the causal experiment the last four runs kept naming as missing - delete a fraction of the update's component orthogonal to a measured subspace, sweep the dose, and match a random-subspace control - and its most valuable output is the part that does not fit.** The dose-response is monotone and steep: 1% deletion costs about 29% more steps, 7% costs about 203%, 10% and above fails outright, while a random projection at 0.25 strength does nothing at all, so the effect is about *which* directions were removed and not about the size of the perturbation. Then at 50% deletion the dual-task model groks again at a 163% delay and the tri-task model never does. The author reads this as redundant centre manifolds - overparameterisation supplying alternative escape routes - and concedes the alternative (implicit low-rank reconfiguration) cannot be separated with one seed. Mechanical rule for us: **a subspace deletion sweep reports its non-monotonic points as findings rather than smoothing them into a threshold, and every threshold we publish carries the recovery behaviour beyond it, because a "critical deletion fraction" that becomes passable again at ten times the dose is evidence about escape routes and not about an information bottleneck - and only the full curve distinguishes a wall from a door you cannot reach.** Cross-reference run 715 (a mitigation that relocates rather than removes), run 713 (ablation damage is architecture-dependent because families differ in what else can carry the behaviour), run 710's carried question (direction-level intervention during training with a predicted effect size - this paper is the closest thing in the folder to that).
- **Pattern:** **A control with the right sign and the wrong magnitude is the most instructive kind: the no-decay runs have negative curvature comparable to grokking runs (about -50 against -63) and never generalise, so the instrument that grades severity inside the regime has no relation to the regime itself.** The same paper's own phase diagram shows grok time and curvature depth covarying across lambda and then breaking at lambda = 0 - the point where the covariate reads "nearly as extreme" and the outcome is absent. Read against run 719's honest admission (24/24 grokking runs with decay against 1/24 without, with no direct manipulation of the mediating quantity) and run 720's confounded SGD control, this is the third run in a row where the causal quantity is a covariate of weight decay rather than a manipulated variable. Mechanical rule for us: **when we claim a quantity is the mechanism rather than a correlate, we exhibit at least one condition where the quantity is held high and the outcome is absent - and if we cannot, we write "covaries with" and never "drives", because every measurement of a training-time quantity is measured under some regulariser, and the regulariser is the thing that moved.** Cross-reference run 719 (weight decay as a hyperparameter contrast dressed as a mechanism test), run 720 (optimiser substitution confounded by loss level), run 712 (a percentile threshold stable across training while a maximum swings two orders of magnitude).
- **Pattern:** **Six places in a 42-page paper with fifteen tables where prose and table disagree, every one of them load-bearing - and the file's own summary is the one that is wrong.** The tri-task condition is add+mul+sq everywhere except Table 13's label, "add + mul + sub"; §12.5 says k* rises from about 5 dual-task to about 7 tri-task while §10.5 and the Conclusion say the reverse and use the reverse as evidence for constraint-induced compression; §11.3 gives dual-task a wider k* range than tri-task in the same sentence arguing tri-task is more tightly packed; the tri-task lead fraction is called monotonic when it runs 82% then 77% then 97%; the orthogonal complement is quoted as about 1% of trajectory variance in one figure caption and as under 1% of *gradient* variance in §8.3 where the causal claim rests; and one tri-task probe table's internal spread (35,000 at 3% against 36,200 at 2%) sits unmotivated inside an otherwise monotone dose-response. Mechanical rule for us: **before quoting a paper's stated conclusion number we re-derive it from the table it came from, and where a paper gives the same quantity in two directions we record both and cite neither - because the errors that survive into abstracts are the ones where a reversed ordering supports a thesis, and a coherent narrative is exactly what makes them invisible.** Cross-reference run 720 (the drift multiplier given as 2,000x and 1,300x against identical endpoints), run 713 (a survey quoting its own study count as three different numbers in three sections), run 716 (an argmax boundary selected against the outcome it explains).

- **Done:** arxiv-2602.23696 - Optimizer-Induced Low-Dimensional Drift and Transverse Dynamics in Transformer Training (read full text v3 HTML, 72,522 chars / 1,950 lines: abstract, §1-§11 including all subsections, §10.7 scope and limitations, §11.1-§11.5 methods, Tables 1-6 reconstructed from header column order and cross-checked against prose restatements, Figures 1-4 read from captions and the prose that reports them but not inspected as images, reference list read in full - 16 entries, four of them the author's own 2026 preprints with no arXiv ids in the text)
- **Pattern:** **This paper supplies the causal control the whole spectral-edge arc (runs 710, 716-719) was missing, and it answers the question in the direction nobody expected - the structure is in the *optimiser*, not the loss surface - but the control is confounded at exactly the point that matters, and the confound is invisible in the headline.** Swap AdamW for SGD family with model, data, seed and schedule fixed: SGD without momentum never trains at all (validation above 8, probe at chance), both momentum variants crawl to validation near 5.1 with probe accuracy under 0.015, and geometrically every SGD run is essentially collinear - 100% of drift in one component, `k95 = 1` - while AdamW over the same window shows 61.5% and `k95 = 9`. Read that as "the edge is a property of the optimiser" and you have read a null result about training as a null result about geometry. The paper's own matched-loss attempt does not fix it: the AdamW windows it compares against sit at validation below 3.4 versus the SGD plateau at 5.1, and the author states outright that AdamW's pass through 5.2 happens inside the first 200 steps where there are no checkpoints. Mechanical rule for us: **an optimiser-substitution experiment counts as evidence about geometry only if the substitutes reach the control's loss inside the analysis window; where they do not, the result is reported as "this optimiser did not produce this training outcome" and never as "this geometry requires this optimiser" - because a trajectory that ends 3.5 nats away from the control is a different experiment, not a different optimiser on the same experiment.** Cross-reference run 719 (a hyperparameter contrast - weight decay - dressed as a mechanism test, with the same structure one level up), run 713 (numbers inherited from a survey and never re-measured here; these numbers are the author's own), run 715 (necessity in one model, dispensability across a family).
- **Pattern:** **The single most portable instrument in the whole arc is a pair of alignment numbers taken on the same direction, and the gap between them is 25x - which means every gradient-derived attribution we have been quoting is measuring an object the optimiser is not moving along.** Per-batch gradients align with the dominant drift direction at about 0.008-0.012, near the isotropic floor; the 200-step cumulative parameter change aligns at 0.15-0.32, twenty to thirty times higher; and the *signed* alignment flips from negative to positive near step 5100, exactly where the global direction rotates. The author's mechanism is simple and checkable - momentum integrates a weak persistent sign bias, and second-moment normalisation downscales high-variance coordinates so the low-variance coherent ones survive - and the β-2 sweep grades it smoothly: concentration 68.8% to 52.5%, early alignment 0.37 to 0.27, probe accuracy 0.951 to 0.005 as the second-moment estimator is turned off. Note what this does to run 717's finding that the strongest parameter direction *is* the instantaneous gradient: that was measured inside a rolling-SVD estimator at W=1, and here the same family of question asked with accumulated updates instead of a smoothed gradient gives the opposite ordering. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever we say a direction "matters" we report its alignment with instantaneous gradients and with accumulated updates side by side, and we trust the accumulated number - because the object that shapes a trajectory is twenty times better aligned with the sum of the steps than with any one of them, and a single-batch attribution inherits all of the noise and none of the coherence.** Cross-reference run 717 (the 648x coupling ratio at W=1 falling to 140 at W=40 - the degenerate-estimator limit of exactly this measurement), run 704 (a path-product bound computed forward from weights rather than from gradients).
- **Pattern:** **A slow direction can be simultaneously the most stable thing in the model and the least rotatable, and the paper measures both with the same spectrum - this is the cleanest statement the folder has of why stability and controllability are different axes.** Adjacent rolling windows align at a mean cosine of 0.80 and never drop below 0.70, so the direction is locally smooth; yet early and late phase directions agree at only 0.32, a rotation of about 71 degrees, and the trajectory passes through a dead zone near step 3500 where it aligns 0.02 with the early direction and 0.16 with the late one. Meanwhile curvature along the direction rises roughly 3,000x over training and the anisotropy ratio spikes to 12.4 at step 4800, precisely when the rotation is under way. Then reheating from the final checkpoint drives probe accuracy back to 0.782 (above the training peak of 0.777) in a thousand steps and it decays to 0.279 by two thousand: the capability was never erased, it became expensive to reach. Mechanical rule for us: **a direction we report as "stable" ships with its rotation over the full run and its curvature at the checkpoints we used, and any claim that a model "cannot" do something is checked by a re-heating or re-weighting probe rather than by an ablation - because a locally smooth tangent can accumulate 71 degrees of global rotation, and a capability suppressed by stiffness is still present, just unreachable at the learning rate you were using.** Cross-reference run 715 (a mitigation that relocates the sink rather than deleting it), run 712 (a percentile threshold stable across training while a maximum swings two orders of magnitude - here stability and drifting are separated by which window you take), run 719 (a stability coefficient that loses to its own simplest component at the window sizes practitioners use).
- **Pattern:** **Two arithmetic slips sit in the prose around numbers that the paper repeats elsewhere correctly, and both are in the load-bearing ablation - which is the folder's standing reminder that a repeated number is not a checked number.** The β-2 ablation's drift multiplier is given as about 2,000x in the mechanism section and the discussion but about 1,300x in the ablation section, against identical endpoints (120 to 1.6x10^5, which is about 1,300x), and the SGD control's "AdamW with the same architecture achieves PC1 around 81%" is quoted against a Table 4 whose AdamW row reads 61.5 - two windows, correctly, but the prose does not say so where the comparison is made. Compare run 713 (a survey giving its own study count as "over 210", "over 200" and "over 180" in three sections) and run 716 (an argmax boundary selected against the outcome it explains). - **Done:** arxiv-2603.28964 - Spectral Edge Dynamics: An Analytical-Empirical Study of Phase Transitions in Neural Network Training (read full text v3 HTML, 63 pages: abstract, Part I §1-§3 the objects and the three assumptions, Part II §4-§5 noise vacuity and the Hessian/Krylov argument, Part III §6-§7 the definition of k* and its null, Part IV §8-§10 order parameter, Davis–Kahan and level crossings, Part V §11-§15 the flow equations, Part VI §16-§18 stability coefficient, loss decomposition and the Gap Maximality Principle, Part VII-VIII §19-§29 the full system and all eight empirical tests including the causal test, Part IX §30-§44 connections, open problems, closing remark, and the reference list; all ten tables reconstructed from header column order and cross-checked against prose; Figures 2-5 not inspected as images)
- **Pattern:** **This is the analytical parent of run 710's empirical paper, and reading it dissolves the distinction that four earlier runs were groping for: the "spectral edge" and the optimiser's coherent direction are not the same object, they are the *subspace* and its *basis*.** Run 717 found that a parameter-space diagnostic subspace was interchangeable with random directions of the same rank; run 718 found the general geometric object (a centroid of an affine tile) that the whole programme is a probe of; this paper supplies the skeleton both were standing inside - a dominant block of k* modes separated from a subdominant block by one gap, with the entire dynamic content in the gap size. The load-bearing move is that "signal versus noise" is the wrong axis in this regime. With p/W around ten million the classical Baik–Ben Arous–Péché threshold sits orders of magnitude below the weakest mode, so every mode is signal and the interesting boundary is between committed and wandering signal. For us this is the correct frame for reading weights: our problem is not detecting directions, it is telling the directions the network has settled on from the ones it is merely passing through. Mechanical rule for us: **report any spectrum-derived statement with its aspect ratio and with the position index at which it was taken, and never quote a signal/noise separation that came from an isotropic null without also reporting the empirical spread of the noise modes in the same window** - because when the null is off by two orders of magnitude, as run 710 already measured, the null is describing a different problem.
- **Pattern:** **The paper's most portable instrument is a stability coefficient computed by splitting the window in half, and its most instructive result is that the instrument loses to its own simplest component at the window sizes a practitioner would actually use.** The coefficient is one minus the ratio of window perturbation to squared gap, floored at zero, and it needs no Hessians and no gradients - just the spectrum. It is what makes "how reliably is this direction held" a number. But the causal sweep (§28) reports rank correlation 0.21 and 0.42 at windows of 10 and 20 checkpoints against 0.76 and 0.72 for the plain gradient product, crossing over only at 30 (0.75 versus 0.63) and 40 (0.68 versus 0.35). The paper reads the crossover as the minimum window for resolving subdominant modes and that is probably right; the consequence it does not draw is that most published trajectory analyses use windows of ten to twenty. Compare run 711 (a percentile statistic stable where a maximum swung two orders of magnitude, visible only because both were tabulated) and run 718 (a surrogate graded by recovering a single known answer). Mechanical rule for us: **a correction term added to a simple score is reported with the simple score's value alongside it at every setting we actually used, and the claim "the correction helps" is only made above the crossover point where it demonstrably does - because a two-parameter refinement that wins at a window size nobody chose a priori is a refinement tuned by the sweep.**
- **Pattern:** **The control design here is a hyperparameter contrast dressed as a mechanism test, and the paper is honest enough in its phrasing that the gap shows.** The headline is 24/24 grokking runs with weight decay against 1/24 without, with the sub-leading gap compressing 33-43x at the grokking step. But weight decay simultaneously changes the spectrum, the loss trajectory, the weight norms and whether the model memorises at all; no run manipulates the gap directly. The author's own wording - weight decay as a driver "by which" the dynamics are associated with generalisation - concedes the association. Meanwhile the one test that does intervene, removing a signal direction from the updates and comparing against the predicted per-direction loss change (§28), is the one that came back window-limited, and the one prediction that came back null (gap-flow sign correlation r = +0.08, R² = 0.006) is excused by the equation having three terms rather than retested. Cross-reference run 713 (numbers inherited rather than measured) and run 715 (a pathway real in one seed, inert in another, only visible because both were reported). Mechanical rule for us: **when a treatment variable is a hyperparameter, the intermediate variable it is supposed to work through is named as an association until a direction-level intervention is run, and that intervention is the one we design first - because a co-occurrence between a spectrum and a behaviour is compatible with both of them following from a third cause that the hyperparameter also moves.**

The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:1 core:0 idx_a:1 idx_c:0`, i.e. the key now appears exactly once, in this folder only) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the twenty-first consecutive run. **Discovery and tooling:** the `/abs/` page was fetched first (5,353 chars: single author, gmail contact, no institution, v1 2026-03-30, v3 2026-05-07, 63 pages / 5 figures, CC BY 4.0, full abstract), then the full v3 text was fetched once - **215,561 chars after tag strip, 6,159 lines** (the report line printed 228,726 for the raw file). - **Done:** arxiv-2604.11962 - The Linear Centroids Hypothesis: How Deep Network Features Represent Data (read full text v1: abstract, §1-§5 including Discussion, Limitations, Impact Statement and Acknowledgments; Appendices A-H including the formal theory, the polygon/GELU replications, MNIST, the Platonic-representation check and the compute table; displayed maths read from the TeX pass; Figures 1-17 read from captions and reporting prose, not inspected as images; reference list read in full)
- **Pattern:** **This is the first entry in the folder that relocates the feature from the layer's output space to its *input* space and gets a falsifiable prediction out of the move - and the prediction is a dissociation, which is worth more to us than any accuracy gain it reports.** Because a centroid is the Jacobian of a sub-component summed over output dimensions (`J^T·1`), it describes what the local affine map *does*, whereas a latent activation describes only *where the input landed*. The paper's load-bearing experiment follows directly: inject colour into FashionMNIST at a swept correlation with the label, then probe colour linearly from latents and from centroids. Latents stay linearly decodable for colour even at random correlation; centroids are decodable only to the extent the label depends on colour. That is a claimed criterion for spuriousness - present-but-unused versus used - and it is the first operational test this folder holds for the distinction every "spurious feature" warning since run 690 has gestured at. Caveat that must travel with it: the evidence is a left-hand panel of a figure with no tabulated effect size and no seed spread, so the dissociation is demonstrated in kind, not in magnitude. Mechanical rule for us: **for every feature we report, we report both readings - the representation-side one (is it linearly decodable from activations?) and the action-side one (does removing or perturbing it move the output map?) - and we call a feature spurious only when the first is positive and the second is flat, because a direction being present is not the same as a direction being used, and only a pair of readings can separate them.** Cross-reference run 717 (a diagnostic subspace interchangeable with random of the same rank - the same "presence is not use" failure, met from the causal side), run 695 (a construction baseline making a score interpretable).
- **Pattern:** **An attribution score computed as a mean relative displacement over a neighbourhood is an intervention-shaped quantity obtained without running the intervention, and this folder now has the clean statement of what such a surrogate buys and what it does not.** Ablating one neuron and measuring the relative change in the sample's centroid, then averaging over a 256-point ball of radius 0.25 around the input embedding, ranks the previously published "an"-neuron of GPT2-Large in the top 99.8th percentile of its layer at layer 31 - a filter that avoids sweeping every neuron, arrived at from one prompt. Two properties are checked (low variance across draws, stable percentile across neighbourhood sizes) and one is not: the score is never validated against the ablation it stands for. So the claim "this ranking matches what ablation would find" rests on one recovered known answer, i.e. a test on the training case. Compare run 696 (a shuffled-community null beating every activation-only score) and run 717 (a 648x coupling ratio falling monotonically to 140 as the estimator is made less trivial) - both are cases where a surrogate was graded against itself. Mechanical rule for us: **any cheap surrogate for an expensive intervention (a gradient-based attribution, a centroid displacement, a Jacobian norm) is graded by rank correlation against the expensive intervention on a held-out set of at least dozens of cases, reported as a scatter and a Spearman value, and a surrogate validated only by recovering a single known answer is described as "consistent with" rather than "predicts".** Cross-reference run 694 (the one-forward-pass screen, first column only), run 696 (the shuffled null), run 717 (the degenerate-estimator limit).
- **Pattern:** **A paper whose every result is the shape of a plot is asking the reader to accept a method on one reading of its geometry - and the giveaway here is that the alternative hypothesis is criticised for exactly that.** The LRH is faulted for directions that are "linear even when the feature is spurious", yet the LCH evidence is PCA scatters, probe-accuracy curves, an 8-neighbour image grid and saliency images; the numbers that would settle it (probe accuracy, firing frequency, the cosine-similarity distributions whose bimodality at 0.4 carries the Platonic-representation argument) live only inside panels. The one quantity actually tabulated is compute cost - and it is not uniformly small: 8.7s versus 7.8s for DINO extraction, 881 versus 771 on MNIST, but 2329 versus 470 (about 5x) for the Llama-3.1-8B probes. Compare run 712 (a percentile threshold stable where a maximum swings two orders of magnitude, because both were tabulated) and run 715 (a circuit necessary in one seed, inert in another, only visible because both seeds were reported). Mechanical rule for us: **when we adopt a representation for our own probes we adopt it with a tabulated head-to-head on held-out data across seeds and a stated cost multiplier, and a new object that is cheaper on one experiment and 5x dearer on another is reported as a cost/benefit curve rather than a drop-in replacement - because "negligible in the grand scheme" is a claim about a budget, and budgets are where surrogates quietly stop being affordable exactly on the large models we care about.**
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The top carried candidate from run 717 - `arxiv-2604.11962`, the Linear Centroids Hypothesis paper cited as [1] by both runs 716 and 717 - was **free**, with all four checks printed before any fetch: `arxiv-2604.11962 aether:free core:free idx_a:0 idx_c:0` (and the same for the two backups, `arxiv-2603.28964` and `arxiv-2602.23696`, both still free). The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:YES core:free`) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the twentieth consecutive run. This was the right read and it closes a four-run arc: runs 714-717 repeatedly measured a "centroid" object in activation space, in parameter-delta space and in training trajectories, and this is the paper that (a) says what the object formally is (`J^T·1`, the centroid of a power-diagram tile) and (b) commits to a predictive claim about it. The honest summary for the head: the object's *definition* is well-founded and inherited from the spline/geometry literature rather than invented here, but its *predictive content* - the claim that centroid directions track use and activation directions track mere presence - is asserted and illustrated, not tabulated. **Discovery and tooling:** the `/abs/` page was fetched first (5,386 clean chars: title, four authors with affiliations, v1 2026-04-13, CC BY 4.0, full abstract), then the **v1 HTML fetched clean on the first attempt** - **89,534 chars after tag strip, 1,276 lines**, a compact document compared with the 144k-char papers of runs 711-712. The line slicer `/tmp/lit/sl.py` was **written fresh this run** per the standing rule that `/tmp/lit` helpers do not survive between runs, and all slicing went through it. **Rendering quirks:** every display equation arrives **twice** - once as flattened mathtext and once as raw LaTeX (`\left(\mathbf{J}_{\bm{x}}\left(f^{(\ell_1\leftarrow\ell_2)}\right)\right)^{\top}\mathbf{1}`) - so all formulas were read from the TeX pass; inline maths arrives **interleaved** (e.g. `DINOv2` printed as `DINOv2` then `2`), so every inline number was read as the base token and cross-checked against a visualisation axis label or a prose restatement; **table cells arrive one per line with the header row split**, so Table 1 (compute seconds) was reconstructed from its header column order; **appendix headings arrive as bare ` Appendix X Title` lines** (974-1234), making the section map a single grep. **Figures 1-17 are images and were not inspected**, so every qualitative result in the entry (PCA separation, the golf-ball neighbours, the saliency panels, the training-epoch centroid migration) is taken from its caption or from the prose sentence that describes it, and the entry says so in "Read from". **Provenance worth recording:** the reference list is large and the geometric backbone is the authors' own programme - Balestriero & Baraniuk 2018a/2018b/2018c, Balestriero, Cosentino, Aazhang & Baraniuk 2019 (the power-diagram subdivision paper that Proposition 2.2 comes from), Balestriero, Cosentino & Shekkizhar 2023, Cosentino & Shekkizhar 2024, Humayun et al. 2023 (SplineCam) and 2024 - so this is a manifesto for one research line as much as an empirical paper, and the LRH tools it borrows (TopK SAE, mass-mean probes, the "an"-neuron ablation) come from outside it. The "an"-neuron source is a 2023 single-post finding (Clement & Joseph), not a peer-reviewed paper, which weakens the one quantitative recovery claim.

- **Pattern:** **A feature-formation subspace can be maximally diagnostic and simultaneously non-causal and interchangeable with a random subspace of the same rank - and this paper is the folder's cleanest worked demonstration because both numbers come from the same intervention.** Keeping only a rank-3 attention subspace accelerates grokking 2.20x when the subspace is the gradient-SED basis and 2.36x when it is a freshly drawn random 3-subspace, statistically indistinguishable across three seeds and two operations; removing either one does nothing (1.02x and 1.00x). So the object that carries a 100x+ centroid-coupling ratio buys exactly the same speedup as a random subspace of its rank. This is one level sharper than the folder's previous warnings (run 715: necessary in one seed, inert in another; run 716: one implementation among several) because here the diagnostic and the null are evaluated in a single experiment on one model. Mechanical rule for us: **every time we report that a recovered direction, feature or subspace "matters", we also report the same intervention with a random subspace of the same dimension at the same site, and if the two are statistically indistinguishable we write "rank, not direction" - because a coupling ratio, however large and however reproducible, is a property of the measurement geometry, and only the matched-random intervention tells us whether the direction is doing the work.** Cross-reference run 714 (a circuit necessary in s42 and inert in s271), run 716 (every sink component individually dispensable), run 707 (magnitude and direction separated by three ratios).
- **Pattern:** **Most of a spectral coupling number can be inherited from shared Jacobians rather than from feature structure, and the window sweep here is the folder's best instrument for detecting it.** At W=1 the rolling SVD reduces to the normalised instantaneous gradient, and the coupling ratio is 648x; the author states outright that this is near-tautological because the parameter gradient and the embedding-space centroid are built from the same Jacobians, so any perturbation along the gradient moves centroids mechanically. The ratio then falls monotonically with the window - 648 at W=1, 617 at W=2, 394 at W=5, 212 at W=20, 140 at W=40 - which makes the canonical setting a chosen averaging timescale, not a discovery. The paper is honest about this (it says the "spectral edge" contribution is not load-bearing in single-task) and it is the reason its multitask result is the real one. Mechanical rule for us: **any sensitivity or attribution number produced by perturbing a model along a quantity derived from its own gradients is reported together with the degenerate-parameter limit of that estimate (W=1, instantaneous, single-batch) and the ratio between them - because a chain-rule-implied response is not evidence of a privileged feature direction, and only the decay curve as the estimator is made less trivial tells us how much of the number survives.** Cross-reference run 716 (the Marchenko-Pastur null rejected by 229-576x yet still used as the detection floor), run 706 (variance versus magnitude separated by a norm-matched control), run 708 (a scale fixed at initialisation decides what the fit finds later).
- **Pattern:** **Aggregating gradients across objectives before asking which directions matter to one objective destroys the answer, and this is the folder's first evidence that the destruction is attributable rather than mysterious.** In the shared-encoder multitask model the aggregated AdamW update gives a coupling ratio of 0.1-0.9, i.e. worse than random, for all four operations while all four still grok past 0.99 test accuracy; per-operation gradients recover 20-45x for every operation, larger than the strongest single-task number. The author lists four contaminants (momentum, per-coordinate adaptive scaling, weight decay drift, inter-task cancellation) and shows the fourth is the one that pushes the estimate across the random baseline. This matters for us because our own probes are computed on a model trained on a mixture, and every activation or parameter statistic we take is a statistic of the aggregate. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever a probe or spectral statistic is computed on weights trained on more than one objective, we say which objective's gradient the recovered direction is aligned with and report the per-objective version alongside the aggregate - because an aggregate direction is a compromise between objectives, and a compromise direction can sit below a random baseline for every one of them.** Cross-reference run 713 (ablation damage is architecture-dependent, the same quantity read differently by family), run 716 (one implementation across architectures), run 704 (the path-product bound, a forward-computable surrogate).
