- **Done:** arxiv-2603.15678 - Spectral Edge Dynamics of Training Trajectories: Signal-Noise Geometry Across Scales (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§9 including the Discussion's window-artifact rebuttal and the Limitations paragraph, §2.1-§2.4 with Definitions 1-5 and Properties 1-6, §3.1-§3.7, §4.1-§4.6 with the residualised and multivariate Granger tables, §5, §6's cross-scale table and §7; all tables reconstructed from header columns and cross-checked against prose restatements; Figures 1-4 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; reference list read **in full** because five of its 26 entries are same-author 2026 preprints and the provenance matters)
- **Pattern:** **A trajectory statistic chosen as an argmax over candidates, and then validated by its correlation with the very target it is supposed to predict, is a fit and not a discovery - and this paper is the folder's clearest case because the winner changes with scale.** The signal/noise boundary is defined as the k maximising the ratio of consecutive singular values. At 51M that is sigma2/sigma3 (peak 1.747 ± 0.120 across four seeds) and it correlates with validation loss at |r| = 0.738. At 124M the paper ranks all five candidates by their loss correlation, and sigma2/sigma3 - the boundary that WAS the edge at 51M - comes **last** at 0.211, while sigma3/sigma4 wins at 0.870. The author then writes that "the correct spectral edge automatically emerges as the ratio most strongly coupled to training dynamics", which is selection on the outcome stated as if it were a property of the object. There is no a-priori rule for which ratio is the edge at a new scale. Mechanical rule for us: **any boundary, threshold or rank we define as the argmax of a ratio over a candidate family ships with the full candidate table, the rank of the winner against the outcome it explains, and an explicit note when that ranking chose it - and the word "emerged" is reserved for cases where the winner was fixed before the outcome was measured; otherwise we write "selected", and the selected statistic has to be cross-validated on held-out windows before it counts as predictive.** Cross-reference run 714 (spread-versus-integral ranking: the naive summary ranks the random-init collapse at the top while the integral ranks the real specialists - the same trap inside the model's own features), run 712 (a percentile threshold is stable where a maximum swings two orders of magnitude).
- **Pattern:** **When the null model is rejected by two orders of magnitude it cannot also be the detection floor in the same paper - and here the paper knows both things and reports both anyway.** Marchenko-Pastur predicts a noise-eigenvalue coefficient of variation of about 0.001 at this aspect ratio; the observed value is 0.688 at 51M and 0.26-0.50 at 124M, a factor of roughly 229-576. The author's own diagnosis is that Adam's second-moment preconditioner structures the noise, so the isotropic theory does not apply, and a coloured-noise generalisation is named as needed. Then Property 1 still declares the edge "detected" as a departure from that same isotropic prediction, and the MP edge is drawn on the spectrum plot. This folder has met the same structural error from the other direction (run 706: an inflated norm will usually reproduce an effect you attributed to structure), but this is the cleanest statement of it at the level of a *null* rather than a control. Mechanical rule for us: **an empirical detection claim is stated against a noise band measured from the same data (shuffled windows, held-out seeds, permuted time order), not against a theoretical null the same paper has shown to be wrong by orders of magnitude - because a structured-noise distribution has fat tails, and a ratio threshold calibrated on the isotropic prediction sits inside the noise.** Cross-reference run 706 (variance is not magnitude, norm-matched control), run 708 (a scale at initialisation decides what the fit finds later), run 713 (survey-sourced numbers are inherited, never re-measured - here the null is inherited and never re-measured either).
- **Pattern:** **A rolling-window monitor's claimed lead time is bounded by its own integration horizon, and Property 2 measures exactly that artefact before interpreting it as physics.** The lag between the edge ratio and validation loss flips from -1 to +2 as W grows from 10 to 30 (GPT-2), and the paper's interpretation is temporal integration - a larger window averages more history so it becomes a slower, leading summary. That interpretation is almost certainly right, and it is also the reason the companion grokking claim needs scrutiny: a window of W deltas at 200-step checkpoint spacing spans 200W steps, so at W = 10 the horizon is 2,000 steps, and the 1,700-step grokking lead reported from companion work is *shorter than the integration horizon of the statistic that produced it*. A slow moving average leading a fast series by roughly its own averaging length is a mathematical certainty, not evidence of a precursor. Mechanical rule for us: **every monitoring statistic we propose that is a rolling-window functional states its integration horizon in the same sentence as its lead time, and if the lead is comparable to or shorter than that horizon the result is reported as a level threshold rather than as a precursor - because the only thing that distinguishes a genuine early warning from an artefact of smoothing is a lead time that survives shortening the window.** Cross-reference run 712 (a between-checkpoint gap is not a transition; two transitions separated 10-20x in tokens), run 708 (a stability claim is worth more as a curve over a sweep than as a point).

- **Done:** arxiv-2604.14722 - A Mechanistic Account of Attention Sinks in GPT-2: One Circuit, Broader Implications for Mitigation (read full text v1: abstract, §1-§5 including all four limitation subsections, §3.1's score expansion and §3.2.1-§3.2.6 in full, Appendix A.1-A.3, Appendix B and B.1's norm-preserving swap construction; Table 1 reconstructed from its header column order and cross-checked against the ten prose bullets that follow it; Figures 1-8 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; reference list read only as far as resolving works cited in §1, §3.1 and Appendix B)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's own conclusion is the opposite of what its title suggests, and it is the folder's cleanest statement of "necessary in this model, dispensable in the family" - every one of the three circuit members is individually absent from an architecture that still has sinks.** Query biases (nullified here at 44.7% of baseline) are missing entirely from many reported architectures; MLPs can be dropped from the block (50.3% here) and sinks persist in MLP-free transformers; positional encodings can be removed (17.5% here) and sinks persist in models trained without them. The authors read this as optimisation reconstructing an equivalent sink from whatever components are available, so the parameter-level pathway is an *implementation*, not *the mechanism*. Mechanical rule for us: **when we identify the pathway carrying a behaviour, we report beside it every known architecture in which the behaviour persists without that pathway, and if any exist we write "one implementation" and never "the cause" - because a necessity result in one model and a dispensability result across a family are both facts, and reporting only the first reads a property of a draw as a property of the phenomenon.** Cross-reference run 714 (a circuit necessary in s42 and absent in s271, the same structure at seed granularity), run 713 (ablation damage is architecture-dependent), run 705 (collapsing a selector reveals which half was the mechanism).
- **Pattern:** **A control must be matched on the *axis of the claim*, not on size - and these ten interventions are the folder's most explicit three-axis matching.** Each specific intervention is paired with a control that spends the same budget on the same axis where the objection lives: three outlier key columns zeroed (65.2%) against three *random* key columns zeroed (100.0%), so the claim is about which columns and not how many parameters; replacing the first positional embedding (3.0%) against nullifying the BOS token at the same position (99.7%), so the claim is about position rather than token identity; swapping the MLP-processed positional direction (6.2%) against swapping the raw one with identical swap machinery (99.0%), so the claim is about the processed object rather than the act of swapping. Mechanical rule for us: **every ablation or steering claim we publish names the alternative explanation it defeats and exhibits a control that differs from the treatment on that axis alone, holding size, position and machinery fixed - because a control matched on parameter count but not on the axis where the doubt lives tests nothing, and the three pairs here are the template for how to pick the axis.** Cross-reference run 706 (the norm-matched control that turned a correlation into a mechanism), run 707 (magnitude and direction separated by three ratios), run 700 (a gain needs a null that spends the same budget).
- **Pattern:** **A mitigation that moves a statistic is not a mitigation that removes the phenomenon - this paper's best intervention relocates the sink instead of deleting it, and says so.** Replacing the first positional encoding takes BOS attention from 0.563 to 0.017, which reads as a near-total fix; but the authors' own framing is that the circuit *controls sink location*, so what actually happened is that attention moved to a different position, and the residual after every pathway intervention is reported as unexplained secondary contributors rather than as zero. Read against runs 705-711 this is the same lesson one level up: success scored at the original address alone is compatible with the phenomenon having moved. Mechanical rule for us: **every sink-suppression or feature-ablation result we report carries the statistic at the treated address *and* the maximum of that statistic at any other address, because a 3%-of-baseline reading is compatible both with a real fix and with a sink that relocated to position 2, and only the pair distinguishes them.** Cross-reference run 711 (gating deletes NOP sinks and multiplies broadcast sinks fortyfold), run 713 (ablation damage varies by family because each family answers "what else could carry this" differently), run 705 (removal versus relocation at the component level).

- **Done:** arxiv-2605.24059 - Spectral Probe-Circuits: A Three-Step Recipe for Identifying Attention-Head Circuits in Pretrained Transformers (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§9 including the limitations list and the practical recommendation, Appendix A in full, and §3.5's threshold-calibration tables and §5.3's nine-signal comparison; Tables 1-14 as cells cross-checked against prose restatements; Figures 1-4 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; reference list read only as far as resolving the works cited in §§1-2)
- **Pattern:** **A trajectory feature must be an integral, not a peak and not a spread - and this entry is the sharpest demonstration in the folder of why, because the artifact it defeats is one the folder has been treating as a finding.** Pythia's layer-0 heads begin at a participation ratio near 60 (random attention spreads mass across all positions, so the effective rank is high at initialisation) and collapse to 2-30 by the end of training, so ranking heads by spread - max minus min - puts exactly those collapsing heads at the top; ranking by the time integral of the clipped positive part demotes them and surfaces heads that *gain* sustained rank. The head-to-head table makes the size of the error visible: precision-at-30 is 0.93 for spread against 0.97 for the integral, but mean selectivity in the top five is 155x against 5,791x. Note what this does to every entry in this folder that read a dominant direction or a high rank out of a final checkpoint: a high effective rank is the *default* state of a random head, so the interesting quantity is not how high the rank is but how long it stayed high relative to where it started. Mechanical rule for us: **every trajectory-derived feature we define is specified as a functional of the whole curve - monotone in sustained behaviour, clipped or baseline-subtracted against its value at initialisation, and normalised by fraction of training rather than raw step count - and we print the spread-based and peak-based alternatives beside it, because on this panel the naive alternatives rank the random-init collapse as the top result while the integral ranks the actual specialists.** Cross-reference run 712 (a percentile threshold is stable while the maximum swings two orders of magnitude - the same lesson, one statistic upstream), run 708 (a scale at initialisation decides what the fit finds later).
- **Pattern:** **A circuit can be causally necessary in one seed and causally irrelevant in another trained on identical data with identical hyperparameters - the six-seed cross-ablation matrix is the cleanest negative in the folder and it constrains every "the circuit for X" claim we will ever make.** s42 implements the retrieval task with four layer-0 heads; s271 uses L6H{1,10} plus L7H{9,15} with no layer-0 head above a spread of 11. Ablating s271's circuit on s42 changes probe accuracy by nothing at any checkpoint (0.982 / 0.838 / 0.995 against baselines 0.982 / 0.843 / 0.995), and removing all 32 heads in L6+L7 from s42 still leaves 0.94 - so s271's implementation is not merely redundant on s42, it is absent. The reverse direction is not symmetric (s42's heads do hurt s271), and the seed with the broader late-layer circuit generalises better out of distribution. Note the mechanism the author draws from it: s42's circuit is an *instance* and s271's is a *class*. Mechanical rule for us: **no circuit or feature set is reported as "the" implementation of a capability without a same-architecture, different-seed ablation matrix, and where only one run exists we write "one implementation observed" rather than the definite article - because a causal necessity result on a single seed is a statement about one draw from a distribution over implementations, and this panel measured that distribution and found it spans layer 0 to layer 7.** Cross-reference run 712 (the formation circuit and the final circuit share only a third of their heads), run 704 (the head that fires on apostrophes and is otherwise silent).
- **Pattern:** **Screening quality and causal adequacy are different axes, and the gap between them is filled by distribution rather than dilution - the 43% / 77% / 100% ladder on one model is the measurement.** On Pythia 410M, top-6 spectral picks drop induction by 43%, a mech-interp-plus-second-class set by 77%, and the all-head screen at 50x by 100% with eleven heads, only three of which were in the spectral top-30. The unattractive explanation (dilution: we captured less of the circuit) and the attractive one (distribution: the circuit is spread over more heads at lower per-head selectivity) are separated by the fact that the full set of induction-selective heads exists and restores the whole effect. Mechanical rule for us: **when a screened set recovers only part of a causal effect we do not report the shortfall as noise or as a smaller mechanism - we run the exhaustive screen at a permissive threshold and check whether the missing effect is spread across heads the ranked screen did not surface, because a pre-filter's recall is bounded by the ranking it uses and the residual effect is exactly where that bound bites; and any circuit-size claim we publish carries its ablation-floor curve across thresholds, not a single point.** Cross-reference run 696 (a per-unit worth is a property of the ablated set), run 712 (a head that stopped showing the screened pattern was still inside the circuit).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The carried candidate from run 713 was the author's own companion methodology paper, named there only as `[ Xu, 2026d ]` with the instruction that its id be resolved from the reference list before any id was written down; this run did exactly that first: grep of `/tmp/lit/02378.txt` for the Xu entries returned four, and `Xu (2026d)` is *Spectral probe-circuits* with the arXiv id **2605.24059** (the other three are 2604.25143, an unnumbered composed-task companion, and 2603.15678). - **Done:** arxiv-2604.10098 - Attention Sink in Transformers: A Survey on Utilization, Interpretation, and Mitigation (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§9 including the conclusion and the §9 limitations paragraph, with §2.1-§2.6 architecture-by-architecture, §3.1-§3.4, §4.1-§4.5 and the five-level synthesis table, §5.1-§5.5, §6.1-§6.9 and §7.1-§7.2 read in full; Appendix A Table 1, the per-paper summary of surveyed work, located but not read row by row; Figures 1-42 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; reference list read only as far as resolving works named in the text)
- **Pattern:** **The survey's one structural claim worth carrying is that its eleven accounts are arranged by the *kind* of thing each explains, not by which is right - and that arrangement is the audit this folder has been missing.** The five levels are mathematical origin (softmax no-op; structural bias from causal masking and RoPE), training dynamics (active-dormant head reinforcement - the only account placed on that level), numerical mechanism (outlier circuits; outlier-driven rescaling; mix-compress-refine), geometric structure (geometric anchoring; anti-overmixing; spectral-energy association), and functional role (implicit attention bias). Placed against entries 704-712, this folder holds an independent reading of nearly every one of them, which converts the survey from a source of new mechanism into a **coverage and redundancy check**: the levels with only one account each in the whole literature are training dynamics and functional role, and those are exactly the levels where our own predictive ambitions sit. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever we add an account of a mechanism, we record which level it operates on and which other accounts occupy that level, and an account that is the sole occupant of its level is treated as untested rather than as established - because "no competitor" in this literature means "no one has built one", not "it survived".** Cross-reference run 704 (path-product bound: a mathematical-origin-level account), run 706 (link-by-link weight trace: numerical-mechanism level), run 710/711 (training-dynamics level, where every entry had to import its own dynamics experiment).
- **Pattern:** **Survey evidence is second-hand, and this document shows how second-hand it is at the level of the single number that the whole no-op account rests on.** The two classified evidence it marshals for the no-op family are observational (sink tokens have small value states, across BERT, ViT, LLaMA, OLMo and KV-quantization work) and causal (relax the sum-to-one constraint and the sink goes away - gating, clipped softmax, a `+1` denominator, an unnormalized rectified softmax, plain sigmoid), and every magnitude it quotes is inherited from the cited paper rather than re-measured; the survey's own study count is given as "over 210", "over 200" and "over 180" in three different sections. Note what this does to the *causal* half specifically: it is exactly the move runs 705-711 showed can be a relocation rather than a removal. Mechanical rule for us: **no survey-sourced number enters our own writing as a measurement - it enters as a pointer to the primary paper, and any account we adopt on survey authority alone is marked unverified until we have read the cited primary source; and where a survey's own internal counts disagree, that disagreement is recorded rather than averaged.** Cross-reference run 708 (a stability claim worth more as a curve than as a point), run 711 (mitigation success scored as a relocation), run 706 (the norm-matched control that made a correlation into a mechanism).
- **Pattern:** **Whether a sink "matters" is an architecture-level fact, not a sink-level one - and the survey's ablation table is the folder's widest cross-architecture sample of that.** ViTs degrade severely when their sink tokens are ablated; diffusion transformers lose almost nothing in text alignment but shift fine-grained perceptual structure by roughly six times a random-masking control; diffusion language models barely degrade at all because their sinks move during generation instead of sitting at a fixed position; MoE LLMs collapse entirely when three of several thousand experts are pruned; and in DiTs the sink carries semantic scene information rather than nullity, so the null-token reading of the word does not survive contact with that family. Read together with runs 704-712 this means the folder's most portable finding is a *conditional*: a sink is load-bearing to the degree the model's computation has been routed through the address. Mechanical rule for us: **every claim that a component is causally necessary is reported with the architecture family it was measured in and the ablation's damage on the sub-task the mechanism is supposed to serve, and a single-family ablation is never generalized into a statement about sinks as such - because the same ablation that zeroes a benchmark in one family leaves another family's quality unchanged, and the difference is the architecture's answer to "what else could carry this".** Cross-reference run 704 (deleting the first token zeroes RULER at 4096 context in Gemma 7B while costing nothing in a model trained without a fixed beginning token), run 711 (gating deletes NOP sinks and multiplies broadcast sinks fortyfold - the same family-internal version of the same lesson).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The first carried candidate from run 712 - `arxiv-2604.10098`, gate-tested FREE in runs 710, 711 and 712 and never taken - was **free** again, and the dedupe gate printed all five checks (`/tmp/lit/gate.sh`) before any fetch: `arxiv-2604.10098 aether:free core:free idx_a:0 idx_c:0`, `arxiv-2604.14722 aether:free core:free idx_a:0 idx_c:0`, plus a lowercased title-substring count (`attention sink in transformers`, and separately `survey on utilization`) of **0** on both INDEX files. The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:YES`, `core:free`, `idx_a:0`, `idx_c:0`) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the fifteenth consecutive run. **Discovery and tooling:** the `/abs/` page fetched first (46,231 raw / 5,299 clean chars: title, 27 authors, v1 2026-04-11 and v2 2026-06-05, full abstract, DOI), then the **v2 HTML fetched clean on the first attempt** - 306,268 bytes, **9,079 lines**, http 200. `fetch.py` took a **bare name** (`10098`). The **line-based slicer** `/tmp/lit/ln3.py <file> <a> <b>` was used for every slice. **Rendering quirks worth recording for the next survey:** the document is short-line, with **one sentence per line** in the body but **inline math doubled and typeset-plus-TeX on the same line** (`L L`, `τ \tau`), so numbers were read from the prose rather than from any formula; **displayed equations arrive as a TeX line, a bare `(N)` line and a typeset line**, so equation numbers 5, 6, 40-61 are locatable by grepping `^ (N) $`; **section headings appear as bare lines with a leading space** (` 4.1 Softmax Limitations and No-Op Theory`), which made the whole 100-section map a single grep; and **"Key Takeaways" blocks are interleaved as alternating bullet-marker and text lines**, so each section's three-part summary had to be read as a pair list rather than as prose. **Every number in the entry is quoted from the survey's prose** and attributed as the survey's report of a cited paper, not as a measurement; **no plotted value was ever taken from a figure image** (Figures 1-42 were read only from captions and the surrounding prose). **Table 1 (Appendix A) was deliberately not read row by row** - it is the per-paper index of surveyed work, and reading it would have been a substitute for reading the papers.

Developmental Trajectories of Capability and Attention-Sink Emergence Across Three 1B-Class Architectures (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§13 including related work and the full limitations section, all six findings with Tables 1-8, §8.1 null-calibration table, §11.1-§11.4 discussion including the exploratory activation-versus-weight spectral bridge and its Spearman values; Figures 1-3 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; reference list read only as far as resolving the works cited in §§1-2 and §11.3)
- **Pattern:** **A negative result about where a mechanism can live is cheaper and sharper than a positive result about what it is - the L0/L1 zero-BOS floor is now a falsification test for any account in this cluster.** Zero heads classify as first-token in layers 0 and 1 at every one of the 30 sampled revisions across two corpora and two architecture families, from random initialisation to trillions of tokens, so the phenomenon every entry from 704 onward described at a final checkpoint is bounded by an identity that holds throughout training. Note what this does to the folder: a mechanistic theory that predicts sinks from residual-stream statistics must also predict that the two shallowest layers are off-limits, and the author's own qualitative reading - that position 0's embedding is a fixed function of one token, so attending there discards content until depth has mixed positions - is exactly the kind of claim our white-box model should be forced to make explicitly. Mechanical rule for us: **for every mechanism we claim, we also state where in the stack it must NOT appear and verify that at every checkpoint we have, because a prediction that a component can be anywhere is unfalsifiable, and a floor that holds across 30 runs and two corpora is one of the few identity-like statements this literature has produced.** Cross-reference run 704 (removing the first token breaks the model in exactly the sub-task the mechanism serves), run 710 (the sink's position is chosen by the data pipeline, not the architecture - here the layer position is chosen by the architecture and cannot be moved by the pipeline).
- **Pattern:** **Circuit membership is not the circuit - the same capability is implemented by a different set of heads as training proceeds, and what happens to a decayed head decides whether it was retired or moved.** Formation-time and final induction circuits share only about a third of their heads (Jaccard 0.29-0.33, stable across screen thresholds), and the same group ablation gives opposite readings depending on fate: heads that decayed into sinks go inert (Pythia, +0.3 points, z = +1.0) while heads that slid into the duplicate-token role stay causal (OLMoE L6·H1, -3.1 points, z = -5.5), and a head that was never an induction head at all but only a duplicate detector is causally necessary for induction (L3·H15, -3.2 against -0.48 for matched-random controls). The cleanest part is the within-model comparison: OLMoE's own sink-fate decayer is inert and its own duplicate-fate decayers are causal, so the "dense turns over, MoE accretes" contrast is an aggregate artefact of which fate the heads happened to take. Mechanical rule for us: **any circuit or feature set we identify at one point in a process is reported with its overlap against the same identification at another point, plus a fate for every element that dropped out - because a screening criterion measures the pattern it screens for, and a unit that stops showing that pattern may have moved inside the circuit rather than left it; the second case is invisible to the screen and shows up only as an ablation that still bites.** Cross-reference run 704 (a no-op write is a learned else-branch, not an inactive unit), run 711 (a signature vanishing without showing where the computation went is a relocation, not a fix).
- **Pattern:** **Two quantities that look like one statistic are not interchangeable, and the developmental panel is what separates them - a percentile threshold is stable across training while a maximum swings by two orders of magnitude.** Null-selectivity p99 holds in a 1.45-2.08x band across 140K steps, so a threshold calibrated once at the final checkpoint applies throughout; null-selectivity max wanders from 7.3 to 767 over the same revisions because it is set by isolated pathological draws (a concentrated head whose preferred target happens to align with a random null position). The same lesson appears in the headline result from the other side: the induction transition and the sink transition look like one event at a single checkpoint and separate by 10-20x in tokens once both are tracked across revisions (11.5x on OLMo, 21x on OLMoE), and the same DCLM corpus produces a sharp jump under dense OLMo and a gradual ramp under MoE OLMoE. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever our read-out depends on a threshold or a summary of a distribution, we state which functional of that distribution it is and check its stability across the checkpoints or seeds we have, and we never compare a curve's shape between two models unless the sampling grid is identical - because a maximum is not a threshold, a between-checkpoint gap is not a transition, and a one-checkpoint comparison cannot tell one transition from two.** Cross-reference run 710 (a scalar regulariser swings a sink statistic threefold), run 705 (the same gate reads as flat or selective depending on which normalisation the statistic lives in).

- **Done:** arxiv-2606.08105 - A Unifying View of Attention Sinks: From Mechanisms to Architectural Interventions (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§6 including discussion, Appendix A.1-A.5 controlled-task setups and causal-validation tables, Appendices B.1-B.4 with all four lemma statements and proofs, C.1-C.3 recipes and probe protocols, D register results, E.1-E.3 relocation and penalty ablations; Tables 1, 2, 3, 8, 9, 14 as cells cross-checked against prose; Figures 1-18 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images)
- **Pattern:** **The same observable is compatible with two opposite computations, and the cheap discriminator is not the observable but the norm and the rank of what the attended position writes - this is the entry that finally gives the folder a scale on which its own sink statistics are ambiguous.** Every entry from 704 onward measured concentration, and concentration separates nothing: a NOP sink (near-zero value) and a broadcast sink (content-scale value, rank-one update) both produce a stripe, are both present in the same pretrained DINOv2-G, and both pass any sink-fraction count. The two modes are so distinguishable that a two-number projection of the same model splits them cleanly, but only once the value norm ratio and the update rank are added to the attention matrix. Mechanical rule for us: **no attention-concentration statistic is reported alone - it ships as a triple (fraction of attention mass on the destination, norm of what that destination writes relative to the head's ordinary values, rank of the resulting update), because a near-zero write and a rank-one shared write are identical in every pure-attention view we have been taking, and the write is what the residual stream actually receives.** Cross-reference run 705 (the same gate reading as flat or selective depending on which normalisation the statistic lives in), run 710 (the sink-fraction statistic swung threefold by weight decay alone - a statistic with no mechanism attached).
- **Pattern:** **Interventions must be split by what they leave intact, and this paper is the cleanest statement in the folder of the difference between touching the payload and touching the routing.** Value patching holds the learned attention matrix fixed, so it asks what information sits at the destination; removing the sink column and renormalising asks whether routing through the destination was necessary at all, and everything downstream is destroyed with it - the authors label the second a necessity test and refuse to read it as selective removal. The payoff is a case where the two disagree: in the controlled broadcast model the layer-one sink is causally necessary by every routing and zero-ablation measure, yet donor patching there transfers almost nothing (payload alignment 0.162 against 0.944 at layer two), so "this sink matters" and "this sink is the channel" come apart in the same model. Mechanical rule for us: **when we patch or ablate a component we state which of the three things the intervention preserves - the direction of the write, the routing to it, or neither - and we do not infer that a component carries a signal from the fact that deleting it hurts, because a necessary relay that carries nothing transferable fails exactly that way.** Cross-reference run 707 (a radial map designed so the forward and backward effects separate by construction), run 705 (a bound tested with the upstream gradients held fixed).
- **Pattern:** **An intervention that removes a mechanism does not remove the computation - the model routes it somewhere else, and the somewhere else is measurable.** Gating deletes detected NOP sinks outright (0.25 to 0.00 per image) and broadcast sinks multiply from 0.09 to 3.80, forty times over; registers absorb most sink mass and then divide into the same two phenotypes; the penalised global pathway was designed as a place for the shared computation to live and it takes the causal load off attention entirely in the controlled task (removing the attention update leaves task MSE at 0.3648, removing the global pathway raises it to 1.6539). And the variant that suppresses broadcast most effectively is the one that performs worst on dense probes, so the mitigation leaderboard and the representation-quality leaderboard are anti-correlated. Mechanical rule for us: **every architectural or training intervention we propose is scored on the full set of behaviours it could have displaced, plus downstream quality, and an experiment that shows a signature vanishing without showing where the computation went is reported as a relocation, not a fix - because an intervention that removes a sink can convert one null write into forty shared writes, and the score we designed the intervention to improve will not see that.** Cross-reference run 704 (removing the first token breaks the model in exactly the sub-task the mechanism serves), run 709 (a gate that survives its own routing being deleted is the gate that was the mechanism).

- **Done:** arxiv-2410.10781 - When Attention Sink Emerges in Language Models: An Empirical View (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§3 with Tables 1-3, §4-§7 with all takeaway summaries, §8 future work, Appendix C.1 Propositions 1-4 with proofs, Appendix B formulations and Appendix D/E located by heading; Tables 1-6 as cells cross-checked against prose; Figures 2-8 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; no limitations section exists in this paper, so the Limits section quotes its future-work paragraph instead)
- **Pattern:** **The sink is not a property of the architecture - the same recipe moves it by an order of magnitude on weight decay alone, and the data pipeline chooses its address.** Two runs with identical architecture differ from 15.20 to 41.08 on nothing but the weight-decay ratio, and the position of the sink follows the packing pipeline rather than the model: randomise the first token of each chunk and the fraction goes *up* to 27.03; randomise the first two and the sink moves to position two (14.08 there against 1.98 at position one); fix a designated token at position two or three and the sink sits there. Note what this does to the folder: every entry from 704 onward that reads a sink out of a released checkpoint is, on this evidence, reading a handful of undocumented pre-training decisions as much as a mechanism. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever we attribute a read-out to a head's learned behaviour, we state the pre-training or fine-tuning pipeline that produced the model in the same sentence, and when the pipeline is undocumented we say so and downgrade the claim to a description of that checkpoint - because trajectories that differ in the pipeline are not two samples of one phenomenon, and a statistic that a single scalar regulariser swings threefold cannot be a stable unit of analysis.** Cross-reference run 709 (a gate that survives its own routing being deleted is the gate), run 707 (a scale at initialisation, invisible in final accuracy - the same lesson about where a difference in outcome really came from).
- **Pattern:** **The paper manufactures the mechanism as an explicit parameter and then varies its magnitude continuously - the bias ladder is the cheapest causal instrument in this cluster and the value-bias sweep is its best result.** Key bias alone (value bias fixed at zero) relocates the sink off the first token while validation loss stays at 3.72, so the key-side score is doing the work and the value-side write is decoration. The sweep then shows the reverse direction: raise the length of the fixed value bias and attention migrates *back* from the bias to the first token (sink on the bias 73.34 -> 1.51 at twenty times the unit vector's length, with the first-token sink rising to 25.88), because a large value write is expensive to cancel inside the residual stream, so the optimiser re-points the keys of token one to absorb the same surplus. Mechanical rule for us: **when we claim a component supplies a function, we instantiate that function as an explicit parameter at the same position, hold the rest of the architecture fixed, and sweep the parameter through at least three magnitudes - because a knob that switches a behaviour on, off, and back off again at the far end is a claim about the fitted solution rather than about the component, and the far end is where the reason shows itself.** Cross-reference run 705 (a mechanism versus a scale knob separated by replacing selection with its average), run 708 (a derived parameter reported beside the swept optimum).

- **Done:** arxiv-2505.06708 - Gated Attention for Large Language Models: Non-linearity, Sparsity, and Attention-Sink-Free (read full text v1: abstract, §1-§6 including the limitations paragraph, §5 related work, Appendix A.1-A.5 in full; Tables 1-6 as cells; Figures 1-6 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; reference list not read beyond resolving works cited in §5)
- **Pattern:** **A component published inside a bundle may not be the part doing the work, and this paper's Appendix A.1 is the cleanest self-inflicted demonstration in the head.** The prior work this paper positions against was named for routing — a sigmoid that selects the top-k among pools of key/value/output experts — and when the authors collapse the selection to a single expert, deleting the routing mechanism entirely and leaving only the sigmoid on the value output, that single-expert configuration is the best row in the whole switch-head family on both perplexity (5.808) and benchmarks, while the largest routing configuration (8 experts, top-8) is worse and costs 38M parameters against 3M. Note the shape of the move: instead of arguing that routing matters less than reported, they remove it and re-measure the same family, which converts a bundled claim into a single-component claim at the cost of one training run per row. Mechanical rule for us: **when we attribute an effect to a selection, routing or competition mechanism, we rerun the comparison with the selection collapsed to a single option — same gate, same slots, no choice — and report that row beside the full mechanism, because a gate that survives its own routing being deleted is the gate that was the mechanism, and everything we would have said about "specialisation" belongs to the routing we just removed.** Cross-reference run 705 (a mechanism and a scale knob are indistinguishable unless the selective part is replaced by its own average - the same collapse of a selector, from the other side), run 704 (a unit whose write is near-zero is the load-bearing unit; here a module whose selection never selects is).
- **Pattern:** **Two named factors in one architectural addition can be separated by substituting, at the same position, a cheaper component that supplies only one of them - and that is what the RMSNorm row and the non-sparse-sigmoid row do here.** The gate was credited with two things: a non-linearity in a path that the algebra shows collapses into one low-rank map (value projection composed with output projection, further narrowed by grouped-query attention), and an input-dependent sparse score. A parameter-free RMSNorm at the identical position supplies only the non-linearity and still cuts average perplexity from 6.026 to 5.847, while a rescaled sigmoid confined to [0.5, 1.0] supplies only the non-linearity-and-gate-shape without the small scores and gives 5.900; but only the genuinely sparse, query-dependent gate drops the first-token attention share from 0.467 to 0.048, while the non-sparse gate leaves it at 0.451. So the perplexity gain and the sink suppression are carried by different halves, and neither half alone reproduces the other's effect. Mechanical rule for us: **when we credit a component with more than one job, we name each job and exhibit one substitute that supplies that job alone — in the head we already do this for "does this direction do anything" and "is this a scale artefact", but not yet for multi-purpose components — because a single ablation that removes the component removes all its jobs at once, and reports the sum as though it were the mechanism.** Cross-reference run 706 (variance versus magnitude separated by a norm-matched control), run 707 (magnitude and direction separated by three ratios before any conclusion), run 700 (a gain needs a null that spends the same budget - here the rival null is a parameter-free normaliser).

- **Done:** arxiv-2409.04431 - Theory, Analysis, and Best Practices for Sigmoid Self-Attention (read full text v1: abstract, §1-§7 including the conclusion, Appendix A limitations in full, Appendix E bias-term derivation including Proposition E.1 and its proof, Appendix G.1.2 bias ablation and G.2.1-G.2.4 vision ablations, plus the ASR findings list in §5.4; Appendices C, D and F (UAP proof, Jacobian proof, kernel details) and most of Appendix G located by heading and read only as far as their statements; every figure-borne magnitude taken from a caption or a prose sentence, never from a plotted value)
- **Pattern:** **A removed normaliser's functional role can be *restored analytically* by solving for one parameter, and this entry is the head's cleanest case of a hyperparameter that was derived rather than tuned.** Softmax supplied two things the elementwise sigmoid does not: per-element mass of about 1/n, and a specific gradient scale with respect to a logit. Requiring the sigmoid gates to sum to one yields a single solving bias bounded between −log(n−1)−M and −log(n−1)−m (Proposition E.1), and requiring the gate's derivative to match softmax's yields the same order −log n; the empirical sweep over constant biases then peaks near −10 and degrades at −15, which is the same order for the sequence lengths used. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever our white-box model replaces or drops a component of the network, we state the property that component supplied, solve for the parameter that restores it, and report the solved value beside the empirically best one - because a replacement whose only tuning is a sweep has not shown what the original component was for, and a derived value that lands near the sweep's optimum is evidence the explanation of the component is right.** Cross-reference run 707 (the intermediate quantity that transmits a training signal, named rather than correlated), run 704 (a bound that predicts the direction of an effect before it is measured).
- **Pattern:** **A stability claim is cheaper and more informative when it is a *curve over a sweep* rather than a point at one setting - and the LR-sensitivity score here is the reusable form of that.** The score is the expected excess loss over a learning-rate grid relative to the best learning rate on that grid, so a flat score means the configuration tolerates a wrong learning rate; the paper reads every ablation through it (LayerScale on/off, QK norm on/off) and treats "no spikes in gradient norm" with smooth loss as a success criterion in its own right. The same discipline appears in the bias ablation as a non-monotone curve with an interior optimum, which no single-setting comparison could have revealed. Mechanical rule for us: **any intervention or architectural variant we evaluate is reported as a curve over its own strength or over the learning rate, with the summary statistic stated (here an expected excess over the best point), and a single-setting success is not counted as a robustness result - because the interesting failure in this paper is at the *ends* of the range, and a sweep midpoint would have looked like a clean win.** Cross-reference run 703 (the plateau-then-cliff curve, where a correlation was being read off the flat part), run 700 (a gain needs a null that spends the same budget).
- **Pattern:** **The failure that cost prior work its result was a *scale at step zero*, invisible in final accuracy - and this paper's whole contribution is finding it by looking at an initialisation statistic.** Naive sigmoid attention was reported to work, did not reproduce, and the diagnosis was the initial attention output norm being large, fixed by moving initial logit mass into the sigmoid's near-zero region (ALiBi, or the −log n bias). Note what the fix has in common across the two routes: both reduce a norm at initialisation while leaving the trained function class alone, and the paper's own FLOP accounting shows the arithmetic difference between the two activations is negligible, so the entire gap was never about the activation's cost or capacity. Mechanical rule for us: **when a mechanism or a read-out "does not transfer" between two models, the first diagnostic is a scale statistic measured at initialisation or at the start of the fit, printed for both models - because a difference in initial geometry produces a difference in what the fit later finds, and an end-state comparison will attribute that difference to the mechanism under study.** Cross-reference run 707 (a normalisation that makes magnitude and direction inseparable, separated by ratios), run 706 (variance versus magnitude separated by a norm-matched control), run 701/702 (the offset ratio, the same statistic one level up: what the layer is fed decides what it learns).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection.** The first carried candidate from run 707 - `arxiv-2409.04431`, named there by id and title - was **free**, and the dedupe gate printed all three checks in one command before any fetch: `-f` reported free for both `aether/arxiv-2409.04431.md` and `core/` (no such file), `grep -c "^arxiv-2409.04431 |"` returned **0** on both INDEX files, and the lowercased title substring (`sigmoid self-attention`) returned **0** on both INDEX bodies. **Discovery and tooling note, new and worth keeping:** the v1 HTML fetched clean on the **first attempt** - 1,171,087 raw bytes, **209,425 chars** after tag strip, the largest document in the recent head (runs 704-707 were 59k-131k) because it carries four proof appendices and an extensive experiment appendix. The rendering quirk that matters: **`grep -n` line numbers and character offsets diverge sharply** because each displayed equation occupies a short line while the TeX restatement is a very long one, so slicing by character offset from a grep line number lands in the wrong place - the run's first two offset slices both returned the same inline footnote fragment ("...ble at https://github.com/apple/ml-sigmoid-attention") and looked like truncated sections. **Fix: switched to a line-based slicer (`/tmp/lit/ln.py <start> <end>`) that prints `lineNo|text`, so grep -n line numbers index directly into the document.** This will recur on every paper with heavy display maths and should be the default for such documents. Also note the affiliation block is split across three lines with a placeholder "Affiliation: Mystery" line preceding "Affiliation: Apple" - the real affiliation is the email domain, not the placeholder. **Every number in the entry is quoted from prose or a caption** (17.39% / 6.53% kernel speed-ups, ~8% / ~4% end-to-end, 1.23x step time, the 1/64 FLOP ratio, the 49.5/49.4 and 48.2/49.2 benchmark averages, the 2.2M-440M parameter ladder, the bias sweep values, the 1e-4 LayerScale init, the 70% / −15 over-regularisation observation); **no plotted value was taken from a figure image**.

## Run 707 (2026-11-24)

- Status 2026-11-24 (run 707): started clean - `git pull --rebase -q` left HEAD at `4c7033d3` ("literature (aether): entry 706 arxiv-2605.06611"), so run 706's entry commit and push both landed and nothing was inherited (`git status --short` empty before any write). Counts **measured** from the repo root with the anchored filter (`ls -1 docs/literature/aether/*.md | grep -v -E '/(INDEX|NOTES|SEED_QUEUE)\.md$' | wc -l`): entry files **706 -> 707**; `wc -l INDEX.md` **802 -> 803**, appended by `/tmp/lit/app17771.py`, which reads the line from `/tmp/lit/idx17771.txt` (whitespace-collapsed in Python) and asserts `line.startswith(key + ' | ')`, no embedded newline, `key not in s` before, `s.endswith('\n')`, `s2.startswith(s)`, `not s2.endswith('\n\n')`, `s2.count('\n')==n0+1`, then re-reads and checks `count(key)` - returned **1** (`ok lines 802 -> 803 keycount 1`). **707 mod 3 = 2, so THIS RUN IS SINGLE** (no batch commit, only the entry commit). **The NEXT run is THE BATCH at 708** (`708 mod 3 = 0`) which must carry both labels as two commits (`literature (aether): entry 708 <key>` then `literature (aether): 708 entries`), with `git log --oneline --all | grep -c "708 entries"` checked **before** writing the batch label.
- **Done:** arxiv-2603.17771 - Attention Sinks Induce Gradient Sinks (read full text v1: abstract, §1-§6 including the limitations paragraph, both theorem statements with their hypotheses, the V-scale Jacobian eigenvalues, all five figure captions plus the prose that reports what they show; figures not inspected as images; reference list read only as far as resolving the works cited in §§1-5)
- **Pattern:** **A backward quantity can be *read off* the forward pass when the two are tied by an exact identity, and this entry is the head's first case where the control parameter for a training signal is a number we already compute - the attention column.** The value-state gradient of a token is literally the attention column times the upstream output gradients, so the same column mass that makes a token a forward sink is the multiplier on its backward pressure; the expectation bound then makes that quantitative (squared gradient norm scales with the squared column mass plus a noise term). That is the structure our white-box model wants: not a correlation between a forward statistic and a training effect, but a named intermediate quantity that transmits between them and can be intervened on directly. Mechanical rule for us: **when we claim the optimiser reacted to some structure, we name the intermediate quantity that transmits the reaction, exhibit an identity or bound linking it to a forward-readable statistic, and intervene on the intermediate - because a claim that rests only on the two observables co-occurring cannot distinguish a cause from a shared consequence.** Cross-reference run 704 (the path-product bound, a forward-computable surrogate for a sensitivity claim - the same move one level up), run 706 (the same sink traced link by link down to named weight objects).
- **Pattern:** **A normalisation that attenuates in proportion to magnitude makes magnitude and direction structurally inseparable, so an ablation that changes one is always reportable as changing the other - and this entry's fix is to split the effect into three ratios before drawing any conclusion.** Bloat (gradient on the normed input over gradient on the branch residual) is large on the sink token; compress (branch gradient over normed-input gradient) tracks activation scale with slope close to minus one; change (block input over block output) stays near unity. Only the third is what the residual path sees, and it is the one that would have been reported as "the gradient is huge" if only an end-to-end norm had been measured. Mechanical rule for us: **any intervention or ablation we run across a normalisation boundary is reported as the triple (pre-normalisation magnitude, normaliser attenuation, net change at the residual), and a claim of selectivity is only made on the middle term - because in a pre-norm stack a big norm and a big directional effect are the same measurement unless the ratios separate them.** Cross-reference run 706 (variance is not magnitude, separated by a norm-matched control), run 705 (a mechanism and a scale knob are indistinguishable unless the selective part is replaced by its own average).
- **Pattern:** **A causal claim is earned by inventing a third object that changes one link of a chain and leaves the others alone - and here that object is a *radial* map chosen to be near-identity exactly where the forward pass matters.** V-scale multiplies each value by r/(r+C), so it contracts small-norm values (where the gradient intervention is strongest) and leaves large-norm values almost untouched (where the forward computation actually lives), which is why the model can keep its sink while losing the activation. Note what the intervention does not do: it removes only the dominant channel, so the mechanism claim is explicitly partial and the authors say so. Mechanical rule for us: **a mechanistic intervention we publish is designed so that its forward effect and its backward effect are separated by construction (a map that acts on norms, gates on a scalar, freezes a branch), and its partiality is stated in the same sentence as the claim - because an end-to-end perturbation that changes everything at once supports only a co-occurrence story.** Cross-reference run 705 (a conditional bound tested with fixed upstream gradients), run 700 (reconstruction and interpretability separated by freezing one branch).

- **Done:** arxiv-2605.06611 - The Structural Origin of Attention Sink: Variance Discrepancy, Super Neurons, and Dimension Disparity (read full text v1: abstract, §1-§6 including the limitations paragraph, Appendices A-D; Tables 1-4 as cells; figures 1-23 read from captions and the prose describing them, not inspected as images; the reference list skimmed only to resolve the works cited in §§1-6)
- **Pattern:** **A dominant representation direction can be traced down to named weight objects, and this entry is the folder's only full-path account of that - which is the missing half of run 705's spectral story.** The chain is measured link by link on one model: causal masking leaves position 0 unaveraged, so its dimension-wise variance stays high after layer-1 value aggregation; the output projection is biased to carry it (mean Kendall tau 0.32 between column weights and first-token input variance); a handful of huge-norm FFN gate/up columns are gate-open and massively active only for token 0; one of those rows in the down-projection is heavy-tailed and pushes the activation into a single coordinate; after the next layer's RMSNorm the outlier coordinate is 1.2568 against a mean absolute value of 0.0048, so the token has collapsed to a near-basis vector; and heads whose sink key aligns with the query matrix's leading singular direction then score positive on almost every position. Mechanical rule for us: **when we report that a representation direction is dominant, we name the smallest set of weight objects that produce it and give a measured statistic for each link, and we predict the intervention consequences of that chain rather than only correlating one end with the other - because run 705 could say the mode exists and how a gate contracts it, and only a link-by-link trace says which weights a prediction has to be conditioned on.** Cross-reference run 705 (the same sink as a low-rank spectral spike above a random-matrix edge), run 704 (the same sink as an over-mixing brake with a path-product bound).
- **Pattern:** **Variance is not magnitude, and this entry buys the distinction with a norm-matched control - the cleanest version in the head of a null that isolates the claimed quantity from the trivial one beside it.** Mean-centred rescaling of one token's aggregated output by a factor lambda turns that token into a dominant sink that grows with lambda; scaling the same vector by the same factor without centring does not produce a sink at all, even though it inflates the query-key dot product more directly. The same discipline shows up twice more: the variance measurement is run on random tokens so a fixed BOS cannot manufacture zero variance at position 0, and the early-layer outlier is separated from ordinary norm growth by a dominance ratio (max absolute coordinate over mean absolute coordinate) rather than by norm. Mechanical rule for us: **any claim that a statistic other than raw magnitude drives a behaviour comes with a control that holds magnitude fixed and varies only the claimed statistic - because in a residual stream where norms grow with depth, an inflated norm will usually reproduce the effect you thought you were attributing to structure.** Cross-reference run 705 (the random-matrix and random-subspace nulls), run 700 (a gain needs a null that spends the same budget), run 704 (the min-norm value write that is a function, not an inactive unit).

- **Done:** arxiv-2611.20403 - Attention Sinks as Spectral Spikes: A Mechanism Analysis of Gated Attention (read full text v1: abstract, §1-§7, Appendices D.1-D.8, E.1-E.3, F.1-F.4, G.1-G.3 and the limitations appendix H; Tables 1-14 as cells; figures 1-9 read from captions and the prose describing them, not inspected as images; Appendices A, B, C, I located but not read for algebra - only the statements of Theorem 3.1, Proposition 3.2, Theorem 4.1 and Corollary 4.2)
- **Pattern:** **A dominant direction in a representation is only a finding if it is scored against a null that has the same shape - and this entry is the folder's cleanest worked example because the null is a random-matrix noise edge, not a shuffled control.** Every spectral claim in the paper is reported as a distance above a Marchenko-Pastur bulk edge computed from the same sequence length and width, and every alignment claim is reported against a random k/T subspace baseline; the sink subspace clears that baseline by 20-36x, which is what makes "the leading mode is sink-aligned" more than an artefact of picking the leading eigenvector of a noisy matrix. The sink-subspace projection is the second half of the null: removing the claimed subspace drops the leading eigenvalue by 12-19 percent, so the claim is checked by subtraction as well as by correlation. Mechanical rule for us: **every low-rank, dominant-direction or subspace claim we publish carries (i) a random-matrix or random-subspace baseline computed from the same shapes and sample size, and (ii) the value of the statistic after projecting the claimed subspace out - because leading eigenvectors of noisy covariance matrices always look structured, and only the gap above the edge plus the projection test say otherwise.** Cross-reference run 696 (the shuffled null that beat every activation-only ranking), run 700 (a gain needs a null that spends the same budget).
- **Pattern:** **A mechanism and a scale knob are indistinguishable unless the selective part of the mechanism is replaced by its own average - and the pair of numbers here shows how easily the same gate reads as flat or as strongly selective depending on where the statistic lives.** The elementwise gate and its per-token feature average both shrink the output by roughly the same amount, so in raw activation energy retention the two are identical (0.047 vs 0.044 for strongly vs weakly sink-aligned directions). In the feature-standardised covariance the same comparison is 0.740 vs 0.945, and the gap collapses to 0.957 vs 0.990 once the gate is averaged - so the selectivity is real, but it was invisible in the unstandardised statistic. Mechanical rule for us: **any claim that a component suppresses or selects a particular structure is paired with an ablation in which the component is replaced by its own mean or scalar summary, and the effect is reported in the normalisation where it is claimed to live plus the raw scale - because a mechanism that only exists in one normalisation is a statement about the normalisation, and a uniform shrinkage can pass for selectivity in the wrong geometry.** Cross-reference run 699 (dictionary latents positioned by chat-format boilerplate - the same non-semantic variance), run 700 (the discarded component named rather than hidden).
- **Pattern:** **A local Jacobian bound is worth publishing only when it is probed with a counterfactual that holds the expensive half of the pass fixed - and this paper's fixed-upstream-gradient recomputation is the cheapest version of that recipe in the head.** The gradient claim is conditional on fixed attention weights and fixed incoming gradients, so instead of an end-to-end ablation they cache the upstream gradients from the real gated backward pass, recompute the sink-column contribution with the gate removed, and compare - yielding an attenuation ratio well below one that tracks a forward-computable proxy across layers. The same construction separates "the gate acts" from "the model trained differently": the trained gated model with the gate switched off still shows first-token gradient amplification above the baseline scale, so the active gate contributes attenuation that the learned weights did not compensate for. Mechanical rule for us: **a bound or surrogate that is stated conditionally is tested conditionally - hold the conditioning quantities fixed, toggle only the component under test, and report both the conditional ratio and the end-to-end trained comparison side by side - because an end-to-end difference confounds the mechanism with everything training did in response to it.** Cross-reference run 704 (the path-product bound used as a pre-ablation surrogate), run 700 (reconstruction versus interpretability separated by freezing one branch).

- Status 2026-11-24 (run 704): started clean - `git pull --rebase -q` left HEAD at `9b8b2e26` ("literature (aether): record 703 push status"), so run 703's entry commit and push both landed and nothing was inherited (`git status --short` empty before any write). Counts **measured** from the repo root with the anchored filter (`ls -1 docs/literature/aether/*.md | grep -v -E '/(INDEX|NOTES|SEED_QUEUE)\.md$' | wc -l`): entry files **703 -> 704**; `wc -l INDEX.md` **799 -> 800**, appended by `/tmp/lit/app02732.py`, which reads the line from `/tmp/lit/idx02732.txt` (whitespace-collapsed in Python) and asserts `line.startswith(key + ' | ')`, `key not in s` before, `s.endswith('\n')`, `s2.startswith(s)`, `not s2.endswith('\n\n')`, `s2.count('\n')==n0+1`, then re-reads and checks `count(key)` - returned **1** (`ok lines 799 -> 800 keycount 1`). **704 mod 3 = 2, so THIS RUN IS SINGLE**; the **NEXT run is THE BATCH at 705** (`705 mod 3 = 0`) which must carry **both** labels as two commits (`literature (aether): entry 705 <key>` then `literature (aether): 705 entries`), with `git log --oneline --all | grep -c "705 entries"` checked **before** writing the batch label.
- **Done:** arxiv-2504.02732 - Why do LLMs attend to the first token? (read full text v4: abstract, §1-§6, Appendices A-D including both proofs, Tables 1-3 as cells; figures 1-10 not inspected as images)
- **Pattern:** **The first entry in this folder to give a *path-product bound* that maps one attention weight monotonically onto one predicted sensitivity, which is the analytic surrogate our white-box model needs before it can call an ablation result predicted rather than post-hoc.** Extending prior over-squashing work to multiple heads, the norm of the Jacobian from an input token's representation to an output token's representation is bounded by a sum over attribution paths of a product of head-averaged attention coefficients; each factor is a number we can read off a forward pass, so lowering one weight path multiplicatively lowers the bound for every path through it. That turns "does this component matter" into a computable quantity with a stated direction, and the empirical shape matches it on all four axes the bound carries (depth, width via head count, context length, and packing). Mechanical rule for us: **every circuit or component we publish ships with a path-product bound computed from quantities available in one forward pass, and the intervention test is reported as agreement or disagreement with that bound's predicted direction - because a post-hoc ablation that happens to hurt is not a prediction, and the gap between the bound and the observed effect is itself the finding.** Cross-reference run 696 (a per-unit worth is a property of the ablated set, not the unit), run 701 (measure the input side before drawing a conclusion about the mechanism).
- **Pattern:** **A unit whose write is near-zero is not an inactive unit - here it is the most load-bearing unit in the block, and the paper shows it in a single worked head.** The apostrophe head in Gemma 7B layer 1 fires sharply when the previous token is an apostrophe and otherwise parks its attention on the first token, whose value vector has the smallest norm in the sequence; attending to a small value makes the attention output small, so the block contributes almost nothing and the residual pathway carries the information forward. That is a learned if-else whose else-branch is silence, and it is the same phenomenon that makes heads look "dead" on every activity ranking in this folder. Compare run 696, where a shuffled null beat every activation-only per-neuron ranking, and run 701/702, where dead-unit counts turned out to measure input geometry. Mechanical rule for us: **any per-unit activity or importance statistic we report is accompanied by the norm of what the unit WRITES (value norm, decoder-direction norm times activation) and by a test of whether its low score coincides with a deliberate no-op - because a unit that writes nothing on purpose and a unit that learned nothing are identical on every activity metric we currently print.** Cross-reference run 696 (the ranking beaten by a shuffled null), run 700 (the discarded component named rather than hidden).
- **Pattern:** **Removing a structural component does not degrade a model, it breaks it - and the failure is concentrated exactly where the theory says the component is for, which is both the strongest confirmation in the head and its sharpest warning about ablation claims.** Deleting the first token from Gemma 7B at inference takes ARC-e from 80.77 to 28.49 and RULER at 4096 context from 82.57 to **0.00**, while the same removal costs nothing when the model never learned to rely on that token in the first place (pre-training without a fixed beginning token leaves the sink on whatever token is first). The pre-training ablation is the control that makes this a mechanism claim: sink formation is inevitable, but *which* token carries it is a choice the training pipeline makes, and the two regimes fail in different ways. Mechanical rule for us: **when we ablate a structural component we (i) report the damage on the sub-task the mechanism is supposed to serve, not only the aggregate, and (ii) include a variant trained or configured without that component to show the damage is dependence rather than generic breakage - because an aggregate score hides the zero, and a catastrophic drop under ablation is evidence of reliance, not of necessity.** Cross-reference run 697 (decompose a drop to the sub-task that moved), run 700 (a gain needs a null that spends the same budget - here the null is a model that never formed the habit).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection.** The carried candidate from run 703 - Barbero et al. on first-token attention, named only by arXiv id - was **free**, and the dedupe gate printed all three checks in one command before any fetch: `file:` absent for both `aether/` and `core/`, `INDEX` line count 0 on both, and a lowercased title substring count of 0 on both INDEX bodies. Because the run 703 carry list named it by id only, `2504.02732` was **confirmed by the arXiv API** in the same sweep: `all:"attention sinks" AND all:"first token"` returned 15 records and the target came back as `2504.02732v4`, title exact ("Why do LLMs attend to the first token?"), first published 2025-04-03, department cs.CL. **Discovery:** the fetch was clean on the first attempt (`https://arxiv.org/html/2504.02732v4`, **69,392 chars clean**) and this document's rendering is the **cleanest of the last five runs** - no doubled equations, no interleaved TeX on the display formulas except the standard `expression + \displaystyle` pairing on a handful, and tables arriving as one cell per line with a run-together header, so Tables 1, 2 and 3 were read off each header's column ordering and cross-checked against prose restatements (the 80% figures, the 7.56/7.78 loss pair, and the zero on RULER all appear in prose as well as in cells). - **Done:** arxiv-2605.17659 - Bug or Feature 2: Weight Drift and Activation Sparsity (read full text v3: abstract, §1-§10 including related work, both theorems and the worked proof in §2, all of §4-§7 and the limitations in §8; Tables 1-3, 9, 10, 11 as cells; appendix B.3.2 read in full and B.4 read for its numbers; figures 1-7, 9, 10, 13, 15, 16 **not** inspected as images; appendices A.1-A.4, C, D located but not read in full)
- **Pattern:** **The sparsity and the sign of the weight distribution in a ReLU MLP block are set by a gradient bias that exists before any data-specific learning - so a "silent unit" may be reporting the optimiser, not a feature, and this is the mechanism behind the offset cluster of runs 701 and 702.** The proof is two steps: conditioning two random downstream weight rows on both surviving a ReLU gate forces them to share a positive component along the input direction, so their expected inner product is non-negative; then under MSE or softmax cross-entropy the expected gradient with respect to a positive pre-activation is non-negative, so gradient descent steps the weights negative, which pushes pre-activations negative, which the ReLU maps to zero - a closed loop. It appears on completely random Gaussian inputs, so the data cannot be the cause, and it is broken only by mean-centring normalisation (ResNet with batch norm is the sole configuration near 43% negative pre-activations against 60-80% everywhere else). Mechanical rule for us: **before attributing a low firing rate, a near-zero activation mean or a high activation sparsity to a semantic cause, report the mean of the downstream weight matrix and the fraction of negative pre-activations for that layer, and check whether the pattern survives subtracting a constant interlayer from frozen activations - because a statistic that moves when we subtract a constant is a statement about input geometry and optimisation, not about what the model learned.** Cross-reference run 701 and run 702 (the offset / mean-over-spread ratio, the same quantity one level upstream), run 699 (latents positioned by chat-format boilerplate rather than meaning): four consecutive entries, four levels, one lesson.
- **Pattern:** **A structural property can be shown harmless without being shown useful, and the way to do it is to force the property to a range of values with a mechanism that is not the model's own - this paper's Percentile Centering versus Top-K pair is the cheapest worked version in the head.** Percentile Centering is a layer-norm variant that subtracts a quantile of the pre-activation instead of the mean, so a chosen fraction falls below zero before the ReLU; Top-K is applied to GELU because ReLU's drift sets a floor that cannot be pushed lower. Sweeping either gives a plateau to roughly 70% sparsity then a cliff, and adding the mechanism label to the fitted curve moves R² only from 0.565 to 0.618, so the *level* is what matters and the *mechanism* barely does. Mechanical rule for us: **when we report that a structural property of a model (sparsity, rank, dictionary utilisation) correlates with or predicts capability, we also report the curve obtained by forcing that property across a range with at least one mechanism other than the model's own - because a correlation with an emergent property is not evidence the property does useful work, and a plateau-then-cliff shape means our correlation may be being read off the plateau where it carries almost no information.** Cross-reference run 695 (the construction baseline that made a score interpretable) and run 700 (a gain needs a null that spends the same budget).
- **Pattern:** **A post-hoc component swap at fixed weights separates "this component improved the read-out" from "this component disclosed something already present" - and here the swap is one epoch and it reverses the architecture recommendation.** ReLUfication trains with GELU and fine-tunes to ReLU for a single epoch: post-activation sparsity goes to 55-74% while the negative pre-activation fraction barely moves, so the conversion is not creating the near-zero region, it is truncating a region the drift had already built; accuracy drops by under two points and not at all on some models. The other half of the same lesson is the failure: ReLU-squared wins on the language model but collapses to chance on an unnormalised MLP and on ResNet with batch norm, so an activation's benefit is a property of the normalisation it was paired with, not of the activation. Mechanical rule for us: **wherever we can swap a component of a fitted read-out post hoc while holding everything upstream fixed, we do, and we report the pre-swap value beside the post-swap one - because a component that "improves" a read-out may only be disclosing structure that was already in the weights, and a component whose benefit disappears under a different normalisation was never the cause.** Cross-reference run 700 (reconstruction versus interpretability separated by freezing one branch) and run 696 (the construction baseline that validated a score).
