- **Done:** arxiv-2605.31518 - On the Relationship Between Activation Outliers and Feature Death in Sparse Autoencoders (read full text v1: abstract, sections 1-8, all six main figure captions, Tables 1 and S7 as cells, appendices A-E located and skimmed by heading and by the passages the main text cites; figures 1-6 and supplementary figures not inspected as images)
- **Pattern:** **A dead-or-live count is a property of the activation distribution's geometry, not of the dictionary - and this is the paper run 701's entry was building on, so the two should always be read as one mechanism with two settings.** The load-bearing quantity is the norm of the activation mean over the norm of the per-token spread; when it is large, each feature's pre-activation is dominated by a constant shift (the weight dotted with the mean) and the input-dependent signal barely moves it, so a feature whose shift is negative can never fire and one that wins the top-k competition never loses it. The closed forms need only the ratio and the sample count, and they hold across 454 model-layer combinations with rank correlations 0.82 and 0.89 and no fitting. Mechanical rule for us: **every dictionary, probe or read-out we fit on frozen activations carries this ratio for its input layer, measured after per-token normalisation, printed beside the dead or live fraction - because otherwise two dictionaries trained on two models can differ for a reason that has nothing to do with the models.** Cross-reference run 701 (the same ratio re-derived for a frozen random encoder and for reinforcement-learning agents), run 699 (dictionary latents positioned by chat-format boilerplate rather than meaning) and run 696 (a partial dictionary's coverage is a property of the pipeline).
- **Pattern:** **Two failure modes with one shared cause but different recovery timescales, and separating them is what turns a hyperparameter result into a mechanism.** Negative-shift death is permanent-ish because the only parameter that can lift it is the bias, which learns the mean slowly and slower the larger the mean (99 percent of it inside 200k steps at a ratio below 5, 50-70 percent after two million steps at 30 or more, directly proportional to the ratio); competition death recovers in about 200k steps because living features shrink and free slots. The existing auxiliary-loss mitigation is then re-explained: it helps by preventing *new* negative-shift deaths created during that shrinkage, not by reviving old ones, so its benefit shrinks as the ratio grows - which is exactly the observed pattern and is invisible if you only report the total dead count. Mechanical rule for us: **when we report a failure rate under an intervention, we break it into the sub-populations that share a cause but differ in recovery path, and we put each mitigation beside the pathway it actually acts on - because a flat total can hide active turnover in both directions, which is also the warning that a dead-feature count sampled at one step is not a stable statistic.** Cross-reference run 701 (the offset measured on the input side before any conclusion about the mechanism), run 697 (a statistic of an average is not the average of the statistic) and run 695 (the construction baseline that made a score interpretable).
- **Pattern:** **A preprocessing step the field already uses inconsistently is given a decision rule - measure one statistic, and if it is high the step is mandatory - and the paper then shows the cheap version of the step can itself be poisoned by the outliers it is meant to remove.** The rule is the ratio (centre when high) plus an effective-rank of the post-normalised covariance (whiten when low-rank, below about two percent of the hidden width). Centring by the arithmetic mean fails on four of nineteen models because a few extreme tokens pull the mean away from the typical token (angle between the two mean estimates 0.29-0.72 cosine), and the geometric median fixes it with at most 2.3 percent death; on synthetic data centring moves the mean maximum cosine between learned and true directions from 0.38 to 0.97, and on a protein model a centred 2048-feature dictionary recovers more annotated concepts (100 of 187) than an uncentred 8192-feature one (73). Mechanical rule for us: **any preprocessing or normalisation we apply before interpreting is published with the statistic that says whether it was necessary and a robust-location variant, and any comparison against a bigger unprocessed baseline is reported at equal or smaller budget - because "we cleaned the inputs" is otherwise indistinguishable from "we spent more capacity".** Cross-reference run 700 (the discarded residual component named rather than hidden) and run 698 (agreement between two read-outs of one forward pass cannot separate representation from input-side structure).

A second gate ran **after** the file was written and before the INDEX append, again returning 0 hits for the title. **Discovery:** the arXiv API search is the reliable route now that the SEED_QUEUE is exhausted at key level, and it is cheaper than a recent-listing scrape because it returns titles. Sections 1-8 are the whole main body and the reference list begins at the "References" heading, which was not read in full beyond locating the appendix headings. **Harness note:** the shell discipline rule from run 701 held - no chained one-liners, no inline `for`/`$(...)`; every multi-step operation went through a file (`/tmp/lit/sl.py` for slicing, `/tmp/lit/app31518.py` for the INDEX append). - **Done:** arxiv-2611.29752 - Why Units Die in the Layer After a Frozen Random Encoder, and What the Number of Live Units Measures (read full text v1: abstract, sections 1-9 including the discussion, all five tables as cells, all seven figure captions; figures 1-7 not inspected as images)
- **Pattern:** **A count of live, dead or dormant units is a function of the input distribution's geometry, not of the model or the task - and this paper is the head's cleanest worked demonstration because the author retracts his own metric with it.** The load-bearing quantity is an offset: the length of the mean input vector divided by the square root of total variance, large when the layer's inputs form a tight cluster far from the origin. A randomly placed ReLU boundary that misses such a cluster puts the unit on or off for *every* example, and a unit off for every example gets no gradient and stays off. Holding the game, the encoder and the training fixed and moving only the offset took Pong from 3-6 live units of 64 to 59-63, while a frozen encoder on unaltered screens - and the same author's earlier paper - read the ordering of these counts across games as an ordering of task complexity. Mechanical rule for us: **every unit-level statistic we publish (live units, dead features, firing rate, dictionary utilisation) is accompanied by the offset or an equivalent mean-magnitude-over-spread number for that layer's inputs, computed on the same data with the same rule - because otherwise a difference between two models may be nothing but the geometry of what was fed into the layer.** Cross-reference run 696 (a per-unit worth is a property of the ablated set, not the unit) and run 699 (dictionary latents positioned by chat-format boilerplate rather than meaning): the same lesson at three different levels, that the unit's reading is co-determined by its inputs.
- **Pattern:** **The cheapest pre-training diagnostic in the head is now a single normalised ratio over the inputs of any layer we are about to train, and it replaces a downstream explanation with an upstream measurement.** Because a random encoder multiplies patches by signs of either sign, it preserves the energy of the average input and of the deviations from it alike, so it passes the offset through nearly unchanged (33 conditions, rank correlation 0.988), and an analytic formula needing only a sample of inputs and the layer sizes predicts the median offset of the encoder's output to within 25 percent in all 44 conditions with a median error of 1.4 percent, without drawing a single weight. That makes the offset usable *before* building a front end - our case, where probes and SAEs are trained on fixed activations we cannot change. Mechanical rule for us: **before training any read-out layer on frozen features, run one pass that reports the mean-over-spread ratio of those features and, if it is large, first try subtracting the training-set mean; and when an ablation or probe surprisingly fails per-prompt, check this ratio before concluding the concept is absent - because units born off never see the gradient we assume they receive.** Cross-reference run 695 (the construction baseline that made a score interpretable) and run 700 (the discarded residual component named rather than hidden): both are the same habit of measuring the input side before drawing a conclusion about the mechanism.
- **Pattern:** **Two quantities that both explain the same structural outcome, with one much better evidenced than the other, and the weaker one is reported as weaker throughout - which is the honesty template this folder needs for its own multivariate story.** The offset is the strong factor: it moves with the count in Breakout, Space Invaders, image classifiers, a reservoir computer (rank correlation -0.908) and GPT-2 sparse autoencoders (dead fraction 0.991 at eight times the unaltered offset, most of those units alive at the start so they died *during* training). The step size - learning rate times fan-in, derivable from Adam's per-weight cap and the measured alignment of early weight updates with inputs - is the weak factor: an image-classifier exponent of 1.158 for a constant live-unit count is qualified down to 0.923 with the largest fan-in removed, and the sparse-autoencoder pre-registered test **failed at 0.670** before a design defect (unrotated full-size inputs left in GPT-2's own coordinates) was fixed and gave 0.971. The author also states that a second published mechanism predicts the same FC1-versus-FC2 ordering and that this measurement cannot separate them. Mechanical rule for us: **where two factors are offered for one outcome, each gets its own evidence statement, its own pre-registered test and its own failure record, and any alternative mechanism that predicts the same observation is named beside ours - because an ordering reproduced by two different causes is not evidence for either.** Cross-reference run 697 (the held-out adjudication set the statistical pass never saw) and run 693 (intervals frozen at the search root, the same uncertainty about which factor did the work).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. Everything load-bearing this run was therefore done from files (`idx_29752.txt`, `app29752.py`, this block). **Discovery:** the fetch was clean on the first attempt - `https://arxiv.org/html/2611.29752v1`, 129,200 bytes clean, **no doubling of displayed equations** (unlike run 696's document) and table cells one per line with the header row split, so Tables 1-5 were read off each header's column ordering and cross-checked against prose restatements. Sections 1-9 are the whole document; lines from 699 to the end are the reference list, which was not read in full. ## Run 700 (2026-11-24)

- Status 2026-11-24 (run 700): started clean - `git pull --rebase -q` left HEAD at `9b96e91a` ("literature (aether): 699 entries"), so run 699's two-commit batch landed and nothing was inherited (`git status --short` empty before any write). Priority block: still **none** (`grep -c '^- \[ \]'` = **0**). Counts **measured** from the repo root with the anchored filter (`ls -1 docs/literature/aether/*.md | grep -v -E '/(INDEX|NOTES|SEED_QUEUE)\.md$' | wc -l`): entry files **699 -> 700**; `wc -l INDEX.md` **795 -> 796**, appended by an inline Python append that asserts `line.startswith(key+' | ')`, `key not in s` before, `s.endswith('\n')`, `s2.startswith(s)`, `not s2.endswith('\n\n')`, `s2.count('\n')==n0+1`, then re-reads and checks `count(key)` - returned **1** (`ok lines 795 -> 796 keycount 1`). **700 mod 3 = 1, so THIS RUN IS SINGLE**; the **NEXT run is SINGLE at 701** (`701 mod 3 = 2`), next **BATCH at 702** (`702 mod 3 = 0`) which must carry **both** labels as two commits (`literature (aether): entry 702 <key>` then `literature (aether): 702 entries`), with `git log --oneline --all | grep -c "702 entries"` checked **before** writing the batch label.
- **Done:** arxiv-2611.25165 - Separating Interpretability and Reconstruction in Sparse Autoencoders (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§6, supplement metric definitions, Tables 1-2 and referenced supplementary tables as cells; figures 1-19 captions only, not inspected as images)
- **Pattern:** **A decomposition's leftover should be named, not hidden inside mixed units - and this paper is the cleanest worked example of that move because it makes the discarded component deliberately uninterpretable.** The sparse branch is allowed to be an incomplete reconstruction; the residual branch exists to absorb the rest so the sparse code is never pressured to encode non-semantic variance, and the L2 loss is applied to the sum of the two rather than to the sparse branch alone. That design makes the incompleteness of the explanation explicit and auditable, and it is the structural opposite of what the head's absorption and polysemanticity entries describe, where a single dictionary quietly spends latents on reconstruction-only directions. Mechanical rule for us: **every white-box decomposition we publish names the variance it has declared out of scope and reports the read-out for that declared-out component separately - because a component that is only implicitly discarded is indistinguishable from one that was never found.** Cross-reference run 699 (dictionary latents positioned by chat-format boilerplate rather than meaning - the same non-semantic variance, unaccounted for) and run 696 (the ranking that is worse than a shuffled null once a voxel of structure is preserved).
- **Pattern:** **The control that separates "this component improved" from "this component got more capacity" is to freeze it, let the second component absorb the remainder, and check whether the read-out moved - and this paper runs exactly that pair.** The sequential baseline (pretrained SAE frozen, residual branch trained alone) lifts reconstruction by 15.4-24.8% with interpretability *unchanged*, so the residual branch alone buys fidelity and nothing else; only joint training, with the decorrelation penalty that breaks the identity-map shortcut, buys 10.5-43.3% MonoScore on comparable R². The parameter-matched widened-SAE table rules out raw capacity as the explanation across 18 paired rows. This is the head's recurring "a gain needs a null that spends the same budget" lesson in its cheapest concrete form. Mechanical rule for us: **any claim that an added component improves a read-out is paired with (i) a frozen-component variant that spends the same extra parameters and (ii) a parameter-matched wider baseline, because both are one training run each and together they turn a plausible gain into an attributable one.** Cross-reference run 695 (the construction baseline that made a score interpretable), run 697 (the held-out adjudication set the statistical pass never saw).
- **Pattern:** **A coherence score computed in the same space the model was fit to compress is inflated by that model's own geometry, and this paper concedes the point and answers it with a label-only metric.** MonoScore is an activation-weighted mean pairwise cosine between strongly co-activating inputs, and the authors deliberately compute the similarity with the encoder being decomposed rather than an independent one - justified as removing a second model, but it removes the check that a latent's coherence is not an artefact of that embedding geometry; they therefore also report binary purity, which uses only ground-truth labels and no encoder, and note their absolute MonoScore values are not comparable to the original paper's. Compare run 698 (agreement between two read-outs of one forward pass cannot separate representation from input-side structure) and run 696 (activation-only attributions scoring below a shuffled null). Mechanical rule for us: **every coherence, purity or structure score we report is accompanied by a measure that never touches the representation the model was optimised to reproduce - labels, held-out behaviour, or an independent encoder - because a metric defined in the fitted space will report the fit, not the concept.**
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice. **Two candidates were retired on discovery and one file was nearly clobbered - the important part of this run.** (a) `arxiv-2511.16035` (Liars' Bench, Cadenza Labs) was selected as free by a **file-existence** test and was **overwritten** before the INDEX append caught it: `assert key not in s` on INDEX.md failed with "key already present", and `git ls-files` showed the entry had been committed **2026-11-13 in `e84cff0d` ("29 entries")** with an 8,079-byte body. It was restored immediately with `git checkout -- docs/literature/aether/arxiv-2511.16035.md` (verified: `git status --short` empty, 8,079 bytes). **Root cause: run 700 tested `[ -f docs/literature/{aether,core}/<key>.md ]` but read the result of a *different* loop, so the EXISTS line for the file never gated the fetch.** The pre-existing entry is at least as good as the draft (it carries the judge-prompt ablation numbers, HP-C AUROC 0.82 -> 0.87 under a knowledge filter, that the draft did not have), so nothing of value was lost - but the correct rule is now explicit: **before fetching, test BOTH `-f <key>.md` and `grep -c "^<key> |" INDEX.md`, and print the gate output in the same command that decides, never in a separate loop.** (b) The **SEED_QUEUE is now exhausted at the key level**: all **25** arXiv ids in the queue resolve to an existing file, and every one of the **44** non-arXiv rows has its title present in one of the two INDEX bodies at least once (tested by lowercased title substring, counts 1+). (c) **Discovery, and the fix for an exhausted queue: the arXiv API.** `curl -sL -A "Mozilla/5.0" "http://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=cat:cs.LG+AND+abs:%22sparse+autoencoder%22&start=0&max_results=40&sortBy=submittedDate&sortOrder=descending"` returns full `<entry>` records with title, id and date, which `web_extract` on `arxiv.org/list/cs.LG/recent` does **not** (that page came back as bare `[N] arXiv:2611.26607 [pdf, html, other]` lines with no titles). Of the 40 newest SAE papers returned, 8 of the 9 sampled were already in the folder (`2611.05655`, `2611.09196`, `2610.30474`, `2611.00606`, `2611.13548`, `2611.18089`, `2611.18274`) and exactly one was free - which is what this run took. **Next run should query a second axis (e.g. `abs:%22activation+steering%22` or `abs:%22circuit%22`) before assuming the field is covered.**
- **Next run.** (1) **Batch arithmetic**: files after this run are **700**; **700 mod 3 = 1**, so **this run was SINGLE**; the **NEXT run is SINGLE at 701** (`701 mod 3 = 2`), next **BATCH at 702** (`702 mod 3 = 0`) with both labels as two commits and the batch label pre-checked against `git log`. (2) **Dedupe gate, hardened**: one command that prints BOTH checks (`-f` and `grep -c "^<key> |" INDEX.md`) and refuses to fetch unless both are zero - the near-clobber above is the third dedupe incident in five runs (699: four carries already read; 698: one; 700: an overwrite). (3) **Queue**: exhausted at key level; use the arXiv API with a fresh `abs:` term and test each return against both folders, which is fast because the folder now holds 884 files. (4) **Retired, do not re-chase**: every Goodfire business/announcement post (Series A/B, SOC 2, Baseten, Mayo, Radical AI, fellowship, grants, "we can and must solve alignment", "AI safety still needs great engineers", intentional design, AP293, Silico homepage), `public_neuron_atlas` (JS app + 403 bucket), `natural-language-autoencoders` (= `core/tcpub-2026-nla`), `anthropic-com-research-{assistant-axis,emotion-concepts-function,introspection}` (all three exist as files), `arxiv-2511.16035` (**exists, restored this run**). **Carried candidates for run 701**: first **`arxiv-2611.29752`** ("Why Units Die in the Layer After a Frozen Random Encoder, and What the Number of Live Units Measures", 2026-11-22) - the unit-count-as-measurement question is exactly the reading-a-statistic-of-a-dictionary problem this folder keeps hitting; then `arxiv-2605.01148`-adjacent arithmetic work is exhausted, so fall back to an arXiv API query on `abs:%22attribution+graph%22` and take the newest free hit.

## Run 699 (2026-11-24)

- Status 2026-11-24 (run 699): started **dirty, and the dirt was mine** - `git pull --rebase -q` refused with "cannot pull with rebase: You have unstaged changes", because run 698 wrote its entry file, its INDEX line and its NOTES block and then **never committed any of them** (its own report said "single entry" but the commit did not happen; the run's NOTES text asserted `git status --short` empty *before* any write, which was true, and nothing checked it again *after*). Fixed first, before any new work: `git add docs/literature/aether && git commit -m "literature (aether): entry 698 goodfire-com-research-stories-in-space"`, then `git pull --rebase -q && git push -q` -> **PUSH_OK**, HEAD `b0c9333b`. So entry 698 landed as a single entry commit one run late, and this run's count arithmetic starts from **698 committed files**, not from run 698's claimed 698-on-disk. **Standing rule added: every run checks `git status --short` AFTER its writes and again after its commit, and does not report a count as landed until the commit hash exists.** Counts **measured** from the repo root with the anchored filter (`ls -1 docs/literature/aether/*.md | grep -v -E '/(INDEX|NOTES|SEED_QUEUE)\\.md$' | wc -l`): entry files **698 -> 699**; `wc -l INDEX.md` **794 -> 795**, appended by `/tmp/lit/app_map_llama.py`, which reads the line from `/tmp/lit/idx_map_llama.txt` (built with `write_file`, whitespace-collapsed in Python) and asserts `line.startswith(key + ' | ')`, no embedded newline, `key not in s` before, `s.endswith('\\n')`, `s2.startswith(s)`, `not s2.endswith('\\n\\n')`, `s2.count('\\n')==n0+1`, then re-reads and checks `count(key)` - returned **1** (`ok lines 794 -> 795 keycount 1`). **699 mod 3 = 0, so THIS RUN IS THE BATCH**: commit `literature (aether): entry 699 goodfire-com-research-mapping-latent-spaces-llama` then commit `literature (aether): 699 entries`; `git log --oneline --all | grep -c "699 entries"` was checked **before** writing the batch label and returned **0**. **The NEXT run is SINGLE at file count 700** (`700 mod 3 = 1`), next **BATCH at 702** (`702 mod 3 = 0`).
- **Done:** goodfire-com-research-mapping-latent-spaces-llama - Mapping the Latent Space of Llama 3.3 70B (read full blog post, every section: feature map, feature examples, steering, methods, moderation, limitations, references; the interactive UMAP explorer and the steering plots were not inspected beyond the axes and numbers stated in prose; no figures read directly)
- **Pattern:** **A model-judged behaviour score is not monotone with the capability the behaviour borrows from, and this post is the cheapest worked example in the head - because the authors report the failure against themselves.** Their steering sweep has the judge scoring coherence and pirate-speak on a 0-100 scale; the score rises sharply at mid-strength, but factual degradation begins at a *lower* strength than the behaviour change, and at the top of the range responses the authors describe as factually wrong are still scored well (magnitudes restated in nautical units). The post names it as a pitfall of LLM-judged steering evaluation rather than hiding it, which is why it is citable. Compare run 698 (behavioural-versus-internal agreement as the cheap sanity check) and run 697 (a composite benchmark hiding which component broke): all three are the same shape - one readout standing in for a multi-part effect. Mechanical rule for us: **every steering or intervention result is reported with at least one metric the judge was never asked about, the sweep is reported as a curve rather than a peak, and a high judge score in a region the authors or we have called broken is treated as evidence about the evaluation's narrowness - because here the best-scoring region is the most wrong one.** Cross-reference run 697 (GSM8K accuracy grading arithmetic), run 80 in this folder (the steering sweet spot and off-target effects, the same non-monotonicity measured behaviourally).
- **Pattern:** **Dictionary latents can be positioned by the *formatting* of the data rather than by meaning, and the diagnostic is geometric and free.** Features for special tokens and for strings present in every conversation (the knowledge-cutoff date is the example) appear as isolated points and small islands away from the central mass of the UMAP map; the authors give two candidate causes - very large magnitudes for special tokens, and memorisation pressure from frequent repetition - and cite the superposition-and-memorisation work for the second. Either way the cluster position is a property of the chat-formatted training corpus, not of the concept, and the post is honest that apparent behaviour-clusters were never verified to have distinct effects. Mechanical rule for us: **before interpreting any latent on chat-formatted data, check whether its top activating contexts are a formatting token or a boilerplate string, and report the map-position outlier status of every feature we build an argument on - because a feature that only fires on the system-prompt boilerplate can look like a clean concept cluster and be nothing but a data-format fingerprint.** Cross-reference run 698 (per-position readings whose movement may be input-side structure), run 696 (activation-only attributions scoring *below* a shuffled null - the same lesson that a plausible-looking feature can carry no mechanism).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned, but **the whole carry list was retired for the second run in a row, and this run produced a proper inventory instead of four more one-at-a-time misses.** All four of run 698's carries are already read, every one of them under an arXiv key rather than a slug: `a-geometric-calculator` = `aether/arxiv-2605.01148` (SEED_QUEUE row 147 is marked DONE), `forking-fast` = `aether/arxiv-2608.19611`, `why-larger-models-learn-more` = `core/arxiv-2605.29548`, `open-problems-in-mech-interp` = `core/arxiv-2501.16496`. **That is the sixth through ninth instances of the slug-dedupe hole run 145 named** - two full runs of carries, eight candidates, all already present. The queue test that finally worked, and that the next run should use as the default: parse SEED_QUEUE.md for single-line `- ` rows that are not `- [x]` (149 rows, 21 ticked, **128 unchecked**), normalise titles to lowercase-alphanumeric with punctuation stripped, and test each normalised title as a substring of the concatenated `INDEX.md` bodies of both folders. That returned **36 genuinely free rows**, of which the substantive ones are: `2512.01222` (already `aether/`, caught by key test), `2605.25459` (already), `2602.10067` (already), `2506.20790` (already), `2511.01836` **Priors in Time - already `aether/arxiv-2511.01836.md`**, the Anthropic summary posts `introspection`, `assistant-axis`, `emotion-concepts-function`, `natural-language-autoencoders`, `global-workspace` (the last retired in run 696 as JS-only), and the Goodfire **business/announcement** posts (Series A, Series B, SOC 2, Baseten partnership, Mayo Clinic, Radical AI, fellowship, research grants, "we can and must solve alignment", "AI safety still needs great engineers", "intentional design", AP293 guest lectures, Silico homepage) which carry no methodological content and should be **marked non-literature in SEED_QUEUE rather than chased**. **Discovery:** `web_search` was **not** called (run 662 recorded `daily_cap_reached`). - **Done:** goodfire-com-research-stories-in-space - Meandering on Manifolds: The Neural Geometry of Stories Over Time (read full blog post, all sections; figures and interactive demos not inspected; the linked arXiv paper was NOT read, and the entry says so)
- **Pattern:** **A per-position reading is a trajectory, not a point, and this is the first entry in the head whose unit is the *path* through activation space rather than a snapshot.** Its whole method is one forward pass over a story with something harvested at every sentence boundary - a behavioural six-emotion readout from prompting and a last-token activation - so the object of study is a sequence of states and the claim is about how the sequence moves, not about where a concept sits. That inverts the default in this folder, where a probe is fitted once, read at one position per input (usually the final token, sometimes an answer position) and averaged over a dataset; an average over positions destroys exactly the dynamic this post reports. Mechanical rule for us: **every probe we fit is also run densely over positions on long inputs and reported as a per-input path before it is averaged, and where we publish a single number it is labelled as a statistic over a path rather than as a state - because a mean over token positions is a statement about the corpus, not about the model's state at any moment.** Cross-reference run 697 (the same "a statistic of an average is not the average of the statistic" failure at the loss level) and run 696 (a per-unit worth is a property of the ablated set, the same level-of-aggregation error on units).
- **Pattern:** **The cheapest causal-free sanity check in this literature is to measure an observable and an internal quantity on the same forward passes and see whether they move together - and this post shows both the value and the trap of it.** They read the model's emotion verbally and its activation geometrically at the same sentence boundaries, so the two series are aligned by construction and the comparison needs no retraining; this is cheaper than an intervention and it catches gross misalignment (a manifold that does not move when the story turns). The trap is that both readings are downstream of the same input, so agreement is consistent with a shared surface feature as much as with a shared internal cause, and the post's own defence is only that separate internal probes are accurate. Mechanical rule for us: **a behavioural-vs-internal agreement plot is the first figure in any entry that claims a concept is represented, it is paired with at least one intervention that bends one series and is checked against the other, and agreement alone is never written up as evidence of a mechanism - because a correlation between two readouts of one model cannot separate representation from input-side structure.** Cross-reference run 697 (the held-out adjudication set the statistical pass never saw), run 695 (the construction baseline that made a score interpretable).

- **Pattern:** **This is the first entry in the head to give weight space a *basis whose axes have an operational meaning*, and the meaning is a prediction about intervention - which is the shape our white-box model needs.** K-FAC curvature eigenvectors over each weight matrix are re-expressed as widely-used versus narrowly-used components, the low-curvature end is zeroed, and the basis is computed once from unlabelled text with no trainable mask. Two features make it generalisable to us: the axis semantics are stated in advance of the test ("directions many examples depend on" should be the ones whose removal hurts broadly), and the adjudication is a held-out set the statistical pass never saw - memorised quotations versus fitted Dolma sequences - so a method that only hits its own fitting data is distinguishable from one that found a mechanism. Mechanical rule for us: **any weight-derived object we publish is a basis with a stated per-axis intervention prediction and a held-out probe set that the basis was not computed on; a decomposition graded only on the data it was fitted to does not earn a mechanism claim.**
- **Pattern:** **Sharpness flips sign between the per-example and the dataset level, and this paper is the head's cleanest statement that a statistic of an average is not the average of the statistic.** Prior work finds memorised examples sit in sharp regions; averaged over a corpus, the same quantity inverts, because a direction only one example uses barely moves the mean while attention and composition directions move it a lot. That is the same class of error as run 696's ablation result (a per-unit worth is not a property of the unit) and run 693's frozen intervals (a per-seed reading is not a per-hypothesis one), now at the level of the loss itself. Mechanical rule for us: **every statistic we report is labelled with the level it was computed at - per-input, per-batch, per-dataset - and where we compute both, we report both, because this literature repeatedly draws a qualitative conclusion from whichever level happened to be convenient.**
- **Pattern:** **A composite benchmark score can hide which component actually broke, and the paper's worked example is the diagnosis pattern worth copying.** GSM8K accuracy drops, and the post-level inspection shows the reasoning chain intact with the error confined to the final arithmetic step, so a benchmark named for reasoning was grading arithmetic. Combined with the spectrum result (pure memorisation 3-16% of original accuracy, reasoning robust or slightly better, arithmetic and factual recall in between), the lesson is that an aggregate is only as informative as the sub-capability it is most sensitive to. Mechanical rule for us: **when we report a drop under an intervention, we decompose it to the step or sub-task that moved and name the benchmark's blind spot - and when removal *improves* a metric (their ViT validation went 67% to 71.7% after deleting structure), we treat the improvement as a finding about the model, not as noise.**

- **Done:** arxiv-2611.23112 - Large-scale Neuron Community Discovery Reveals Neuron-Sparse Behaviors in Language Models (read full text v1, Sections 1-8 plus Appendices A-F; figures not inspected)
- **Pattern:** **Ten verification entries and five interpretability entries asked for the same thing - a per-unit reading that is printed rather than executed and discarded - and this is the first one that prints a *schedule of per-unit arrays at scale*, on the interpretability side, and it shows the array worth printing is the group membership, not the individual score.** One forward and one backward pass per prompt produce an attribution for every MLP neuron; the top five per layer become a sparse set; a popularity-corrected co-occurrence z-score becomes a graph; six Leiden resolutions become partitions; every surviving group is then certified per prompt by three KL divergences. Nothing in that chain needs a solve, and the published artefacts are the group list and a per-group behavioural label. The load-bearing measurement is the ablation, not the ranking: the activation-times-gradient score covers 9.94%, gradients alone 6.31%, and **every activation-only score (2.5-2.7%) is beaten by the shuffled-community null (2.91%)** - so a per-unit ranking is validated by the causal test it feeds, and a ranking that predicts nothing can be worse than a random one that preserves the group's shape. Mechanical rule for us: **we publish per-unit readings as group-level objects with a membership array, a per-prompt certificate vector and a shuffled-null coverage curve beside them, and we never report a per-unit score's usefulness from its correlation with anything other than the intervention it is supposed to predict - because here the honest ranking is little better than a shuffled one once a single voxel of structure (the group's layer set and size) is preserved.** Cross-reference run 693 (the per-unit case map computed and unprinted), run 690 (the reachable-set generator list unread), run 694 (the one-forward-pass screen as the first column), run 695 (the construction baseline that makes a score interpretable).
- **Pattern:** **A group's necessity does not distribute over its members, and this is the first entry in the head to measure the failure with a complete subset sweep - which invalidates the way every attribution paper in this folder reads a per-unit score.** All 64 subsets of the six-neuron `an`-vowel community are ablated across 100 prompts: the best single member alone nearly reproduces the behaviour (0.68 against 0.06 for the full set and 4.56 for none), yet removing any single member of the full set leaves KL at 0.06-0.07, and a pair that excludes the strongest member reaches about 0.2. So the strongest individual reading is redundant inside the group, and the same protocol on the in-context copying community finds a single member whose removal takes KL from 0.05 to 7.37 - i.e. the relationship between member and group ranges over both extremes and cannot be assumed from the individual score. Compare the head's verification entries, which report a per-neuron multiplier or split score as if it were a per-neuron worth (runs 678, 682, 688, 691): a worth is a property of the set you ablate, not of the unit. Mechanical rule for us: **any per-unit score we publish is accompanied by at least the single-unit and leave-one-out interventions, and where we can afford it the full power set on the small groups (2^6 here), because one unit's isolated effect and its marginal effect inside the group are different quantities that this literature routinely conflates.** Cross-reference run 694 (read-direction and write-direction specificity as an independent pair, the same "one number is not enough" finding on an SAE), run 688 (the split multiplier as an optimised per-neuron worth), run 149/150 in this folder (a descriptor graded by the rank it gives the true cause among matched alternatives).
- **Pattern:** **Coverage is a property of the discovery pipeline, not of the model, and this paper says so in the same sentence that reports the number - which makes it the cleanest model in the head for how to report a partial explanation's reach.** 10.3% of held-out prompts at one layer, 8.7% at four, 3.3% at twelve, 6.7% at one layer on a 16-neuron budget; the authors state explicitly that missed mechanisms are not absent mechanisms and that the figure is an evaluation-distribution property of *this* pipeline, then support the claim with a mechanism-level audit - attributions taken only at the final token, disjoint partitions forcing each neuron into one group, and a resolution sweep where six of fourteen thresholds retain 99.1% of the union yet the best single threshold reaches only 5.74%. The taxonomy is offered as illustration, not measurement, because an LLM wrote the labels. Compare run 695, which turned a confounded score into a reportable finding by adding a construction baseline, and run 690, where a granularity dial had to be reported at three settings. Mechanical rule for us: **every recall or coverage number we publish is prefixed with the pipeline that produced it, followed by the null and the granularity sweep, and suffixed with the negative statement it does not support - because a partial dictionary's reach invites exactly one overclaim, and the fix is not a stronger method but a longer sentence.** Cross-reference run 695 (the explanation-free rule beside every score), run 693 (intervals frozen at the search root), run 145 (the redirect-stub and slug-dedupe lesson that this run's retired candidates repeated).
